How to read this book

Book 2 explains what SpeakCraft's new system will look like and how it will be built, tested and released. A companion volume, Book 1, covers the product direction and requirements. Read Part I for the technical design, Part II for the user experience, and Parts III–IV for the plan and the delivery process.

Status tags

TagMeaning
DecidedA Product Owner decision has been recorded. Only one exists so far: D-08 "original wording, link out", decided on 2026-11-21.
ProposedA target design, ADR or value awaiting approval at a gate. Every architecture item in Part I is Proposed — awaiting approval (gate G3); every ADR is Proposed — requires approval.
PendingA decision that is open in the RD-99 §8 register (D-01…D-36).
Not runA planned test case. No test in this book has been executed.
RiskA known risk, a conflict between source documents, or an item that could change the design.
R0 R1 R2 R3The release in which an item is planned: R0 Foundation, R1 Core loop MVP, R2 Depth, R3 conditional.

Evidence labels and conventions

  • EXISTING describes the current app (the source documents give path:line evidence). PROPOSED describes the target design. INFERRED means reasoned from evidence or documented platform behaviour, not observed. RECOMMENDATION means a proposal that needs a decision.
  • Values marked † — thresholds, budgets, estimates and targets — are Proposed — requires approval. Many will be tuned after the device spike SPK-01 and the grammar corpus SPK-02.
  • Lines that start with Source: name the authoritative document and section for the material above them.
  • Personas (Aarav, Meera, Daniel, Sofia, Hoang) are hypotheses, not research findings. They are referred to by name or as "they".

What exists today vs what is planned

Exists today (EXISTING). A single-module Kotlin and Jetpack Compose app of 106 files (about 48,900 lines), built in three days of AI-agent commits. Its core promise does not work by default: no transcript is produced unless live transcription is found and enabled, and then only the first utterance is captured. Several scores measure something other than their label. Speaking-session saves are rejected by the Firestore rules, account deletion is unreachable, and CI can publish debug-signed builds without running a test.

Exists from Stage A. Documentation only: an audit of the current app, the product direction, requirements, UX design, architecture, security design, test strategy, delivery plan and this book.

Planned (PROPOSED). The rebuilt app described here. No part of it has been implemented, compiled or tested. Implementation starts only after gate G4 and an explicit "start Stage B" instruction from the Product Owner.

RD-99 §0, §1, §7; RD-03; GOV-02 DC-4; PLN-05 GR-01

The system on one page

SpeakCraft becomes a private, honest speaking coach: ten minutes of real speaking a day, feedback grounded in evidence the user can see, working offline and without an account.

76
software components (CMP-01…76)
18
persistent entities (ENT-01…18)
6
synced Firestore document types
16
ADRs — all Proposed (ADR-016 future AI)
42
screens (38 in R1, 4 in R2)
510
planned test cases — all Not run

What will be built

  • One Android app, no custom server. Kotlin, Jetpack Compose and Material 3 in a single :app module, organised by feature. Firebase Auth, Cloud Firestore and App Check are used only by people who choose to sign in. Background work runs on the device with WorkManager.
  • The phone is the system of record. Room databases hold all learning data; Firestore is an optional backup and sync target that receives metrics only — never audio, file paths or transcripts.
  • On-device, deterministic feedback. Platform speech recognition in four capability tiers feeds an evidence gate, a catalogue of labelled metrics (Measured or Estimated), qualified language rules, and a feedback composer that lists the mistakes found, with evidence, and coach tips chosen from the content pack. When evidence is insufficient the result is Inconclusive. No cloud AI is used.
  • Four tabs. Today · Practice · Words · Progress, with one full-screen session player shared by every speaking task.

How it will be delivered

  • R0 Foundation — safety fixes, CI gates, removals, data foundation and the speech spike: about 38–55 engineering days †.
  • R1 Core loop MVP: about 107–156 engineering days †, plus about 38 person-days † of content verification by the Product Owner in parallel.
  • R2 Depth (role-play, check-ins, notebook and quiz, inbox, content): about 31–46 days †. R3 is conditional (store launch, video self-review, recognition research).

What needs deciding

Gate G2 records D-18, D-33 and D-34 (palette and accessibility rules). Gate G3 approves or rejects each ADR and records D-10…D-16 and D-23…D-26. Gate G4 records D-27…D-31 and D-35 and ends with the instruction "start Stage B". The largest open risks are speech-recognition quality on real devices (SPK-01, RSK-01), single-developer capacity (RSK-06) and the content-verification effort (RSK-04/17).

RD-99 §0, §3, §5, §8, §9, §10; ARC-02 §2.3; ARC-03 §3.1, §8; DES-02 §4; TST-01 (via RD-99 §7.2)

Part I

System design and architecture

How the rebuilt SpeakCraft app is structured: its context and layers, the Android frontend, the managed cloud services it uses instead of a backend, the on-device speech and feedback pipeline, the data model, its integrations, its environments, and its security and privacy controls.

Every item in this part is Proposed — awaiting approval at gate G3. Nothing has been built.

Chapter 1Architecture overview

SpeakCraft will be rebuilt as a single-module, offline-first Android application on its existing technology stack. The phone is the system of record. Firebase is an optional backup and sync target for people who choose to sign in. All speech processing happens on the device.

Status of this chapter The target architecture is Proposed and awaits approval at gate G3. Each ADR is approved or rejected individually. The current code is described as EXISTING only to explain why a change is proposed.

1.1 System context

Figure 1.1 shows everything SpeakCraft talks to. The app runs entirely on the learner's phone. It uses Android platform services for speech, audio, text-to-speech, background work, notifications, sign-in and file export. It reaches the internet only in three situations, each one chosen by the user: signing in and syncing (Firebase), online transcription on phones without an on-device recogniser (tier T3a, after explicit consent), and opening a source-video link in the YouTube app or a browser.

ARC-01 §1, §12; ARC-04 §1 (P-1…P-6), §3.9; SEC-01 §4.1

There is no custom backend SpeakCraft has no server of its own and none is proposed: no REST or GraphQL APIs, no Cloud Functions and no admin endpoints. The device talks only to managed services through their SDKs — Firebase Authentication and Cloud Firestore, protected by Firebase Auth identity and security rules — and only when the user is signed in. Any server-side component would need its own ADR, a privacy review and Product Owner approval (ARC-04 P-1, P-5).
External partyRole in the target designWhen it is contacted
LearnerOne active profile at a time; an account is optional (D-05 Pending)—
Android platform servicesSpeech recognition in tiers, text-to-speech, audio capture, background jobs, reminders, Google sign-in, file exportOn the device; no network except as below
Firebase AuthGoogle sign-in through Credential Manager; process-wide auth stateExplicit sign-in, silent restore of a linked account, re-authentication before deletion
Cloud FirestoreOptional replica of metrics, plans, word progress and events under users/{uid}Signed in and online; only through the sync worker, sign-in, deletion and legacy-import flows
App CheckAttestation of app requests; Play Integrity once distributed through PlayWith Firestore calls; enforcement deferred (D-26)
Device speech serviceOnline transcription for phones with no on-device recogniserTier T3a only, after explicit consent that can be withdrawn
YouTube app or browserShows the specific source video of a technique (D-08 Decided)Only when the user taps "Watch the original lesson"

ARC-04 §1–§2; SEC-01 §4, §7; D-05, D-08, D-26 (RD-99 §8)

1.2 Architecture principles

These principles turn the product principles (RD-15) into technical rules. When two design options conflict, the one that better honours the earlier principle wins.

#PrincipleWhat it means in the designSource
1The device is the system of recordRoom learning.db is authoritative. Screens read Room Flows. Firestore is a replica for signed-in users and is never read on a UI path.ADR-002; ARC-03 DP-01
2No custom backendManaged services through SDKs only. No endpoint is invented; the cloud trusts no client-computed aggregate.ARC-04 P-1…P-3
3Offline first, account optionalEvery core flow works in airplane mode and without sign-in. Sync is deferred through an outbox and never blocks the UI.ARC-01 §8; PP-6
4On-device, deterministic intelligenceNo cloud AI and no language model. Rules and metric definitions are versioned; the same inputs always give the same feedback.ADR-003; ARC-05; NFR-QUAL-006
5Honest by constructionA metric without evidence is null with a reason. No default, baseline or placeholder values. Every metric carries a Measured or Estimated label; weak evidence gives Inconclusive.ARC-03 DP-10; ARC-02 §11.5; PP-1
6Private by defaultAudio and transcripts never leave the device through SpeakCraft. The cloud receives only an exact allow-list of fields.SEC-01 §3.3; ARC-03 §8
7Immutable facts, derived aggregatesAttempts and events are append-only with client-generated UUIDs. Streaks, totals and trends are computed from facts, never stored as counters.ADR-014; ARC-03 DP-02/03
8Existing stack by defaultAny new dependency, removal or replacement needs an approved ADR.ADR-001; EP-1
9Package by feature, interfaces at boundariesFeatures never import each other. Repositories are interfaces with fakes for tests. Composables never touch repositories.ADR-012; ARC-01 §3
10One of eachOne session player, one evaluation pipeline, one capability service, one recogniser factory, one class that calls Firestore.ARC-02 §2.3; PP-5
11Small, reversible changesThe restructure happens inside the existing project, epic by epic, behind compile-time feature flags, with tests written alongside the code.ARC-01 §13; EP-6

ARC-01 §1–§13; ARC-03 §2; ARC-04 §1; RD-15 §2–§4

1.3 Layers and dependency rules

The code is organised in six top-level packages inside the single :app module, under the new namespace app.speakcraft (the applicationId is decision D-15). Figure 1.2 shows which layer may depend on which. A simple CI import check will enforce the rules (WBS T-02.2.3).

FromMay importMust not import
feature/*domain, speech service interfaces, data/repository interfaces, coreOther features; data/local, data/remote; Firebase or Room types; android.speech.*
speech/*domain, data/repository interfaces, core, Android platformfeature/*, Firebase
domain/*Pure parts of core/common, the Kotlin standard libraryandroid.*, androidx.*, Firebase, Context
data/*domain, core, Room, DataStore, Firebase, WorkManagerfeature/*, speech/* implementations
core/*Kotlin standard library; AndroidX (design system and UI only)Any other app package
app/*Everything — wiring only, no business logic—

Narrower import allow-lists make the boundaries checkable: only the Firestore data source (CMP-52) imports Firestore; only the Firebase provider and account repository (CMP-51/53) import Firebase Auth; only the recogniser factory and session (CMP-25/28) import SpeechRecognizer; only the capture controller (CMP-26) imports AudioRecord.

ARC-01 §3; ARC-02 §2.1–§2.2, §11.4; ADR-012; NFR-MAINT-001

1.7 Key quality attributes

The non-functional requirements below drive most architectural choices. Every target is † Proposed and none has been measured; the devices RD-LOW, RD-MID and RD-HIGH are the reference devices still to be chosen (RD-99 §7.3).

AttributeTarget †RequirementArchitectural response
Start-upCold start to an interactive Today ≤ 2.0 s on RD-MID, ≤ 3.5 s on RD-LOW (P90); brand moment ≤ 1 sNFR-PERF-001/002First frame held only for one DataStore and one Room read
Recording start≤ 300 ms (T1/T3b); ≤ 800 ms (T2/T3a)NFR-PERF-004Dedicated audio thread; single recording lease
Feedback latencyStop → result ≤ 2.5 s on RD-MID, ≤ 5 s on RD-LOW for a 60 s attemptNFR-PERF-005Streaming features; metrics + rules + feedback ≤ 200 ms budget
Memory and size≤ 120 MB heap increase for a 120 s attempt; install ≤ 25 MB; ≤ 60 MB app data after 30 daysNFR-PERF-009/010, NFR-COMP-004Constant-memory audio; ML libraries removed; AAC transcode
ReliabilityCrash-free users ≥ 99 % (beta), ≥ 99.5 % (production); 0 lost valid attempts; 100 % of core flows offlineNFR-REL-001/004/006One transaction per attempt; outbox; offline-first
PrivacyNo audio or transcript leaves the device in T1/T2/T3b; sync carries only the ARC-03 §8 fieldsNFR-PRIV-002/003Allow-listed mappers; consent-gated recogniser factory
Evaluation qualityNo fabricated outputs; grammar rules ≥ 0.90 precision; Inconclusive ≤ 15 % on T1/T2; deterministicNFR-QUAL-001/003/005/006Evidence gate; qualified rules; versioned fixtures
Compatibility2 GB RAM devices; compact, medium and expanded widths; 16 KB page sizeNFR-COMP-003/005/008No native ML libraries; adaptive navigation
MaintainabilityNo feature-to-feature imports; files ≤ 600 lines without justificationNFR-MAINT-001/002Package-by-feature; CI checks
AccessibilityWCAG 2.2 AA-equivalent on Android (Chapter 12)NFR-A11Y-001…006Semantics built into components

NFR §2–§8; ARC-05 §11; ARC-02 §7.3

1.8 Technical risks the architecture must absorb

  • RSK-01 Recognition quality varies across devices. On-device recognition may be unavailable or poor on many target phones. Spike SPK-01 decides tier T1/T2 behaviour before the R1 build; the tier design, consented online mode and honest audio-only mode limit the damage.
  • CL-72 java.time on Android 7.x. The current code uses java.time with minSdk 24 and no desugaring, which is likely to crash on API 24–25. D-14 recommends raising minSdk to 26; the domain layer uses epoch milliseconds and date keys so it is safe either way.
  • Storage Recordings need AAC transcoding. Thirty days of daily 16 kHz WAV recordings would take about 290 MB, so the transcode is now mandatory (ADR-015 revision); encoder reliability on API 24–28 is checked in SPK-01.
  • Lifecycle Rotation must not stop a recording. Capture stops when the app goes to the background, but not on configuration changes (Chapter 2).
  • Platform TTS on API 30+ probably needs a <queries> entry for the TTS service, in addition to the recognition service; SPK-01 confirms it.
  • RSK-18 / RSK-03 Wrong or over-reported mistakes. Issues are raised only beyond noise bands; grammar rules ship only with ≥ 0.90 precision †; golden tests and the "Was this accurate?" rating monitor quality.

RD-99 §5, §6; ARC-02 §12.1 (TQ-12, TQ-14, TQ-17); ADR-015

Chapter 2Frontend: the Android app

The app is one Activity hosting a Compose navigation graph. Screens render a single immutable state object and send intents to a ViewModel; ViewModels call domain engines, speech services and repository interfaces; a process-wide container wires everything together. This chapter describes that structure, the session player at its centre, and the rules for threading, lifecycle, errors and feature flags.

2.1 Package-by-feature structure

ADR-012 replaces today's flat, layer-based layout (106 files under the placeholder namespace com.example, with files of up to 1,950 lines) with packages by feature inside the same module. A split into Gradle modules is deferred until the code exceeds about 60,000 lines or has more than two contributors.

app.speakcraft
├── app/        SpeakCraftApplication · MainActivity · di/ (AppContainer, ViewModelFactories) · navigation/ · startup/
├── core/
│   ├── common/      time (Clock, DateKeyProvider) · dispatch · logging (AppLogger, DiagnosticLog)
│   │                config (FeatureFlags) · result (Outcome, AppError) · id · lifecycle (AppVisibility) · privacy
│   ├── designsystem/  SpeakCraftTheme, tokens, components (Chapter 11)
│   ├── ui/          state templates, UserMessage, permission education, ExternalLinkLauncher
│   └── testing/     fakes and fixtures (test source sets only)
├── domain/     model · plan · progress · review · evaluation (pipeline contract + EvaluationConfig)
├── speech/     capability · capture · recognition · analysis · rules · feedback · tts
├── data/       local (learning.db, sensitive.db) · prefs · content · remote · sync · account
│               repository (implementations) · background (workers) · export
└── feature/    onboarding · today · session · practice · words · progress · profile
                settings (incl. account) · reminders · inbox (R2)

The reuse plan classifies every one of the 106 existing main Kotlin files: Keep 1 · Refactor 24 · Re-verify & migrate 4 · Delete 77. Reuse is high for algorithms and content (the evidence gate, text alignment, DSP building blocks, Credential Manager flows, the Robolectric test harness, the 150 hand-written activities and the curriculum catalogue) and low for UI and wiring. The four "re-verify and migrate" files hold content that moves into the bundled content pack only after editorial review against specific source videos (D-08).

ADR-012; ARC-02 §2.1, §10 (reuse plan, totals); RD-03 §13

2.2 Dependency injection: the AppContainer

The app keeps manual dependency injection (ADR-001) — no Hilt, Dagger or Koin. The fix is where the container lives: today it is created inside a composable; in the target design SpeakCraftApplication.onCreate builds one process-scoped AppContainer (CMP-02) with lazily created singletons. ViewModels are constructed only in ViewModelFactories (CMP-05), which pass interfaces through constructors. No composable looks anything up in the container.

ScopeObjectsLifetime
ProcessAppContainer and its appScope; clock, date-key provider, dispatchers, ID generator, logger, feature flags, privacy facts; both Room databases and both DataStores; the content repository (index cached after first load); every repository; sync engine; account repository and its auth state; Firebase provider (lazy); speech capability service; consent store; recording arbiter and store; reminder scheduler; stateless enginesUntil process death. Nothing user-specific is cached outside Room or DataStore, so sign-out and deletion need only store wipes and a Firebase reset
ViewModelOne SpeechAttemptRunner per session ViewModel; a TTS player; a recording playerReleased in onCleared()
AttemptCapture handle, recognition session, pipe writer, temporary WAV file, recording leaseCreated at Record; released in finally on Stop, interruption, cancel or onCleared
Worker runSync session token (uid + profile + epoch), batchesOne doWork(); the token is re-checked in every transaction so a sign-out mid-run cannot write into a wiped partition

ARC-01 §7; ARC-02 §2.3.1, §4.2, §4.4; ADR-001

2.3 Navigation

Navigation Compose stays, with type-safe @Serializable route objects instead of 26 string routes in a 1,548-line shell. The graph (CMP-04) has three parts:

  • OnboardingGraph — the eight onboarding screens, shown on first run.
  • MainGraph — four tab graphs (Today, Practice, Words, Progress), each with its own saved back stack (saveState/restoreState). A bottom navigation bar on compact widths becomes a navigation rail on medium and expanded widths; the four destinations are the same at every width.
  • Full-screen routes — Session(source), SignIn(origin), DeleteAccount, DeletionResume, Permission(kind) and the R2 Inbox. They hide the tab bar and always offer a clear exit.

Start destination. StartupCoordinator (CMP-06) resolves it before the first frame, from at most one DataStore read and one indexed Room read: an unfinished account deletion goes first (SCR-SYS-01), then an unfinished onboarding step, otherwise Today. Everything else that runs at process start — orphan sweeps, the retention sweep, the speech-capability probe, the reminder re-check, a sync request and any legacy-import retry — runs after the first frame on appScope.

Deep links. A reminder notification opens Today with an explicit intent and no extras. No external deep links exist in R1, and MainActivity reads no intent extras at all (SEC-REQ-29). Coach tips and activity pages link to Technique detail (SCR-PRAC-04) from outside the Practice tab; that screen opens above the current one and Back returns there, so a session in progress is never lost.

ARC-01 §5; ARC-02 §2.3.1 (CMP-03, CMP-04, CMP-06); DES-02 §2; DES-03 I-24

2.4 State management

ConcernApproach
Screen stateOne StateFlow<XUiState> per ViewModel, built with stateIn(viewModelScope, WhileSubscribed(5_000), Loading). UiState is a sealed type with Loading, Content, Empty, Error and Offline variants where they apply — every screen defines all its states
Input that must survive rotation or process deathSavedStateHandle in the ViewModel and rememberSaveable in composables
One-off eventsNavigation, snackbars, permission requests and share sheets go through a buffered Channel exposed as a Flow and consumed once
Cross-screen dataRepository Flows only — no shared mutable singletons. A saved attempt updates Today, Words and Progress without a manual refresh
User-visible textViewModels emit UserMessage(@StringRes id, args) codes; text is produced in the UI from string resources
ScreensEach screen is an XRoute that collects state with collectAsStateWithLifecycle() and a stateless, previewable XScreen(state, onIntent)

The session player's state type shows the pattern. Its Result variant fixes the order of the result sections required by the Product Owner on 2026-11-21:

sealed interface SessionUiState {                      // ARC-02 §3.3, illustrative and PROPOSED
    data object Loading : SessionUiState
    data class Prompt(val header: SessionHeaderUi, val prompt: PromptUi, val canSkipPrep: Boolean) : SessionUiState
    data class Preparing(val header: SessionHeaderUi, val prompt: PromptUi, val remainingSec: Int) : SessionUiState
    data class Ready(val header: SessionHeaderUi, val prompt: PromptUi, val mic: MicState) : SessionUiState
    data class Recording(val elapsedMs: Long, val remainingMs: Long, val level: Float,
                         val nearLimit: Boolean, val partialTranscript: String?) : SessionUiState
    data class Processing(val header: SessionHeaderUi) : SessionUiState
    data class Result(                                 // sections render in this order
        val transcript: TranscriptUi?, val tierLabel: TierLabel,      // 1 what we heard
        val headline: List<MetricUi>, val passFail: PassFailUi?,     // 2 up to three labelled metrics
        val strength: FeedbackItemUi,                                 // 3 what went well
        val mistakes: MistakesUi,                                     // 4 Found(firstThree, more) or NoneFound
        val tips: List<CoachTipUi>,                                  // 5 focus tip first, up to 2 more
        val selfCheck: SelfCheckUi?, val rating: RatingUi, val next: NextActionUi,
    ) : SessionUiState
    data class Inconclusive(val reason: InconclusiveReason, val advice: UserMessage, val canSkip: Boolean) : SessionUiState
    data class Interrupted(val cause: InterruptionCause) : SessionUiState
    data class Error(val message: UserMessage, val action: ErrorAction) : SessionUiState
}

ARC-01 §6; ARC-02 §3.3, §7.7, §11.7

2.5 The session player state machine

Every spoken task — a Today step, a library activity, a word stage, a review item, a "Try again" from history — runs in one player (SCR-SESS-01, owned by SessionViewModel, CMP-65). It replaces about ten separate recorders with their own labels and scorers.

FromToTrigger and rule
LoadingPrompt · ErrorActivity or word task loaded · content not found
PromptPreparing · ReadyStart when the template has preparation time · Start without preparation, or Skip
ReadyRecordingRecord, and only if the app-wide recording lease is acquired. Microphone permission is requested in place
RecordingProcessing · Interrupted · ExitedStop or time limit reached · audio-focus loss, app backgrounded, permission revoked, microphone busy, storage full · leave confirmed (the attempt is discarded)
ProcessingResult · Inconclusive · ErrorValid evidence, saved · evidence gate failed, saved as Inconclusive · evaluation timeout or save failure (audio kept)
ResultReady · ExitedTry again (compared with this attempt) · Continue or Done
InconclusiveReady · ExitedRetry · Skip
Interrupted · ErrorReady · Processing · ExitedRetry recording · Retry evaluation or save · Exit (audio deleted)

The prepared prompt, step index and phase before Recording are kept in SavedStateHandle. A recording survives rotation. After process death the player restores to Ready; an in-flight recording cannot survive process death and is discarded.

ARC-02 §6.1, §2.3.6 (CMP-65); SRS-FR-SESS-001, -011, -013; DES-02 §4 (SCR-SESS-01)

2.6 Threading and lifecycle rules

DispatcherBackingUsed forRule
mainDispatchers.Main.immediateCompose, state updates, all SpeechRecognizer calls and callbacks, TTS, Credential Manager UI, audio-focus listenerNever disk or network I/O; never runBlocking
defaultDispatchers.DefaultEvaluation pipeline, plan generation, progress calculations, transcript assemblyCPU only; pipeline runs under withTimeout
ioDispatchers.IODataStore, files (recordings, export, diagnostics), Firestore awaits inside workersRoom suspend DAOs use Room's own executors
audioOne dedicated thread at urgent-audio priorityAudioRecord.read loop, PCM fan-out, WAV writer, streaming feature extractorConstant memory; no allocation per frame; no boxed collections
pipeOne dedicated threadWrites PCM to the T1 recogniser pipe from a bounded queue (50 frames = 1 s †)Never blocks the audio thread: overflow closes the pipe and capture continues
WorkManagerCoroutineWorkerSync, reminder and cleanup workers (Chapter 3)Cooperative cancellation

All dispatchers come from DispatcherProvider (CMP-08) so tests can substitute a test dispatcher.

Single active recording

An app-scoped RecordingArbiter (CMP-27) grants one recording lease at a time with a non-blocking compare-and-set. It covers both app-owned capture (T1, T3b) and recogniser-owned capture (T2, T3a). The Record button is disabled while a lease is held, a second tap is ignored, and reference audio and playback are stopped before a lease is taken. A suspending lock is deliberately not used: it could queue a second recording behind the first.

Main-thread and lifecycle rules

  • No disk, database or network work on the main thread. Debug builds run StrictMode with the death penalty for disk and network access, and a CI test asserts zero violations on core flows.
  • SpeechRecognizer is created, started, stopped, cancelled and destroyed on the main thread only, and destroyed in finally.
  • Rotation does not stop a recording. AppVisibility (CMP-13) reports "app in foreground" from MainActivity's onStart/onStop, excluding configuration changes (isChangingConfigurations). Backgrounding the app interrupts capture; rotating the screen does not. This corrects ARC-01 v0.1, where stopping on every lifecycle ON_STOP would have interrupted recordings on rotation.
  • ViewModels never hold an Activity. The Credential Manager host is passed per call and not stored.
  • The content pack is loaded on io and indexed on default once; screens show Loading until it is ready.
Cancellation situationBehaviour
User leaves the player while recordingConfirmation; on confirm the recogniser is cancelled and destroyed, AudioRecord stopped and released, focus abandoned (all in a non-cancellable finally), the temporary WAV deleted and the lease released
User leaves during ProcessingEvaluation continues on appScope until the save completes, so a valid attempt is never lost; the result appears in History
Evaluation timeoutThe pipeline is cancelled; audio is kept until Retry or Exit
Sign-out or deletion during syncUnique work cancelled; the session-token check aborts the next transaction
Worker stopped by the systemOutbox rows in flight return to pending on the next run (stale rows reset after 15 min †)

ARC-01 §6; ARC-02 §7.1–§7.6, §12.2 (DI-20); SRS-FR-SESS-010, -013, -014; NFR-PERF-012

2.7 Mapping errors to the UI

Repositories and services return typed results (Outcome<T, E : AppError>, Chapter 6) instead of throwing. Each error maps to a screen state with a message that says how to fix the problem. Messages come from string resources, are announced to TalkBack, and never contain exception text, codes or class names.

ErrorUI stateMessage intent and action
CaptureError.PermissionDeniedReady with microphone denied; Today shows speaking unavailableSpeakCraft needs the microphone to hear you; other features still work · Try again, or Open settings when permanently denied
CaptureError.MicUnavailable / AudioFocusLost / BackgroundedInterrupted (cause)Another app is using the microphone · another sound started · you left the app — Retry
CaptureError.StorageFullInterrupted (storage full)Not enough space to record · Open storage settings, Retry
RecognitionError.NoMatch / SpeechTimeoutNot an error; may lead to InconclusiveWe didn't catch enough words · Retry
RecognitionError.Busy / Network (T3a) / LanguageUnavailableAttempt continues audio-only; result labelled "No transcript — audio-only"A transcript wasn't available this time (Settings link if persistent)
Inconclusive (a result, not an error)Inconclusive (reason) + one tipToo short, too quiet, no speech, transcript unavailable, too few words · Retry, Skip
EvaluationError.Timeout / InternalError (retry evaluation)Feedback is taking longer than usual · Retry (audio kept), Exit — never a fabricated result
DataError.WriteFailed / StorageFullOne automatic retry, then Error (retry save) or a snackbarWe couldn't save this; nothing was lost yet · Retry — success is never shown for a failed write
ContentError.MissingItemHistory row with stored titleThis activity is no longer in the library
SyncError.Offline / Transient / SchemaRejectedAccount status: Waiting for connection · Syncing · Error — RetryYour practice is saved on this phone and will back up when you're online
SyncError.NewerSchema / DeletionPending / RestorePausedSync pausedUpdate SpeakCraft · finish deleting the account · sign in to resume backup
AuthError.*Per the SEC-01 §4.6 tableFor example: no Google account available — keep practising without signing in
TtsError.UnavailableReference-audio controls hidden + noteReference audio isn't available on this device
Unexpected exceptionGeneric Error variant; crash recorder writes a redacted entry if uncaughtSomething went wrong. Your practice on this phone is safe · Retry

ARC-02 §8; ARC-04 §6; SEC-01 §4.6; SRS §15

2.8 Compile-time feature flags

The redevelopment happens inside the existing project, so unfinished and retired features are controlled by compile-time BuildConfig booleans read through one FeatureFlags interface (CMP-10). There is no Remote Config: it would be a new dependency, and turning off a shipped feature therefore needs a new release.

  • Flags gate navigation entries, routes, DI wiring and UI — never data shape. Room schema, Firestore documents and the content-pack schema are identical with a flag on or off.
  • Retired-feature flags (LEGACY_*) are false in every distributed build from the first R0 build, and the code behind them is then deleted. A flag never substitutes for removing a dependency: firebase-ai must be absent from the classpath (SEC-REQ-28).
  • Unfinished features are true in debug and false in release until their acceptance criteria and approvals are met. Every flag has an owner and a removal criterion.
  • Privacy text is derived from the flags that change data flows, so in-app statements always match behaviour (SEC-REQ-36).
FlagPurposeRelease setting
LEGACY_* (8 flags)Hide the YouTube tool, cloud-AI UI, self-updater, admin console, AI Mentor, video analysis, tutorials and fabricated dashboard widgets while their code still existsfalse in every build
NEW_SHELLSwitches from the legacy shell to the new four-tab navigationfalse → true at first R1 beta
FEATURE_ACCOUNT_SYNCSign-in entry points and sync UItrue in R1 once the new rules are deployed
FEATURE_LEGACY_MIGRATIOND-19 import of profile and vocabulary for signed-in legacy userstrue in R1; removed after the migration window
FEATURE_WORDS_TAB · FEATURE_TECHNIQUESWords destination (D-20) · Techniques collection and technique detailtrue in R1 if approved
FEATURE_SELF_CHECKSelf-reported checklist on the resultfalse until items pass content QA
FEATURE_T1_AUDIO_SOURCE_PIPET1 audio piping; when false, T1-capable phones run as T2 (no replay)per SPK-01 go/no-go
FEATURE_ONLINE_TRANSCRIPTIONT3a consent flow; when false, T3 phones are audio-onlyper D-14 / D-25
FEATURE_AAC_TRANSCODE · FEATURE_EXPORT_AUDIO_ZIPWAV → AAC after evaluation · optional recordings ZIP in exporttrue · per D-22
FEATURE_ACOUSTIC_CANDIDATESPitch range and volume variation (T1 only)false until validated
R2 flagsNotebook, quiz, role-play, inbox, check-ins, transcript sync (D-16)false
DIAG_VERBOSE_LOGS · STRICT_MODEVerbose logging · StrictMode death penaltyalways false (debug only)

ARC-02 §9.1–§9.2; ENG-01 §2.4

ARC-02 §2.3.1–§2.3.6

2.10 Coding rules that protect honesty

  1. A metric without inputs is null with a reason. Default, baseline, clamp-to-plausible or placeholder values (patterns such as ?: 0 or ?: 62) block a code review.
  2. Recogniser confidence is used only when the recogniser supplies it.
  3. Every metric shown carries its label. "Recognised as intended" is never called pronunciation.
  4. Feedback, issues and tips are deterministic functions of stored inputs. The only "randomness" is a specified stable hash that varies tip wording reproducibly. No generative or cloud model is used.
  5. Counters are derived from facts; nothing increments on navigation or taps.
  6. Time comes only from the injected Clock and DateKeyProvider; the local date key is captured at event time and never recomputed.
  7. Files stay under 600 lines unless justified; all strings and plurals live in resources.

ARC-02 §11.1–§11.8; NFR-QUAL-001; PP-1

Chapter 3Backend and cloud services

SpeakCraft has no backend of its own. The "server side" is a small set of Google-managed services — Firebase Authentication, one Cloud Firestore database and App Check — configured with security rules, plus jobs that run on the phone under WorkManager. This chapter says plainly what exists in the cloud, what runs on the device instead, and what was removed.

3.1 What "no custom backend" means

There are no REST or GraphQL endpoints, no Cloud Functions, no admin console and no server code anywhere in the target design. Today's repository already has no server code apart from one Node.js script that CI uses to publish release records into Firestore with an Admin SDK key; that script is retired with the self-updater (ADR-004) and its service-account key revoked.

Cloud elementWhat it isHow it is managed
Firebase project (prod)The existing project, retained so signed-in legacy users can be migrated (D-19)Firebase console; a second dev project is proposed for debug builds (D-27, Chapter 7)
Firebase AuthenticationGoogle provider only; email, phone and anonymous providers disabled (SEC-REQ-03)Console configuration, recorded as release evidence
Cloud FirestoreThe existing named database; only users/{uid}/… is reachableSecurity rules and their emulator tests in the repository; deployed manually with recorded evidence in R0–R1 (D-29)
App CheckRequest attestation; Play Integrity once the app is linked in Play ConsoleNot enforced while side-loaded builds exist (D-26)
Budget and usage alertsGoogle Cloud budget alerts and Firestore usage alerts with named recipients (SEC-REQ-38)Console configuration
Web deletion pageA public page where users can request account deletion without the app (a Google Play requirement)Hosted outside the app (GitHub Pages proposed, D-30); handled through an operator runbook
Operator runbook instead of an admin API A web deletion request is handled by a person, not a server: verify that the requester controls the Google account, look up the uid in the Auth console, recursively delete users/<uid> in the named database, delete the Auth user, and record the request id, date and uid in a deletion register. The proposed service level is ≤ 30 days †.

ARC-04 §1 (P-1…P-6), §2 rows 13 and 18; SEC-01 §4.2, §9.4; ENG-01 §2.1, §6; RD-02 §2.3

3.2 Firebase Auth through Credential Manager

Sign-in is optional (D-05). A first-time user gets a local profile with a random UUID and the app makes no network request at all — neither SpeakCraft nor the Firebase SDKs (SEC-REQ-02). Firebase components are initialised lazily, only when someone signs in or is already signed in. Whether the SDK's auto-initialisation creates traffic in no-account mode must still be verified by traffic capture (TC-SEC-002).

ModeIdentityNetworkWhere the data lives
No account (default on first run)Local profile id (UUID v4)None, except the OS speech service after T3a consentRoom and DataStore on the phone
Signed in (optional)Firebase uid, Google providerFirestore sync through the sync workerPhone remains the source of truth; a cloud copy of metrics, plans, words and events
StepBehaviour (PROPOSED)
Explicit sign-inGetSignInWithGoogleOption from the sign-in screen, Settings → Account or the Progress prompt. No sign-in prompt on cold start for people who never signed in. A per-request nonce where supported end to end (to verify)
Silent restoreGetGoogleIdOption with authorised accounts and auto-select, only when this install has a linked uid whose Firebase session is missing (the existing pattern is reused)
Token exchangeGoogle ID token → GoogleAuthProvider.getCredential → signInWithCredential. The web client id is resolved at runtime; if it is missing, sign-in is hidden ("Sign-in isn't available in this version")
First sign-inIf the phone has data and the account is empty, local rows are re-keyed to the uid in one transaction and uploaded. If both hold data, or the phone was linked to a different account, the app asks: Merge or Keep separate on this device, with the email masked. This stops another person's history on a shared phone from being merged silently
SessionThe SDK keeps the session and refreshes ID tokens. One process-scoped auth state is exposed as a StateFlow. If the account was deleted elsewhere, sync stops and the user sees "You've been signed out"; local data stays as a local profile
Sign-out (D-21 Pending)Two explicit choices: Remove from this device (pre-selected when everything is backed up) or Keep on this device (pre-selected, with a warning, when items are not yet uploaded). Credential state is cleared, Auth signs out, Firestore is terminated and its persistence cleared
Re-authenticationRequired before any data is deleted; the re-authenticated uid must match, otherwise "That's a different account" and nothing changes
ErrorsMapped messages only, for example "No Google account is available on this device. You can keep practising without signing in." Raw exception text is never shown (SEC-REQ-06)

SEC-01 §4.1–§4.6; ARC-04 §3.7; ARC-02 CMP-51, CMP-53; FS-01 (via SEC-01)

3.3 Cloud Firestore: the named database as a sync target

  • One database, one subtree. The existing named database is retained. Only the signed-in owner's subtree users/{uid} exists in the target schema, holding six document types (Chapter 5). Shared, admin and release collections are frozen in R0 and removed later.
  • One caller. Only FirestoreRemoteDataSource (CMP-52) issues Firestore calls, and only from the sync worker, the sign-in flow, the deletion executor and the legacy migrator. Repositories write Room rows and outbox entries; ViewModels never touch Firestore.
  • No listeners. The 16 unbounded snapshot listeners started today are replaced by bounded, paginated pulls keyed by a server-timestamp watermark.
  • No trusted aggregates. Streaks, totals and scores are never stored in the cloud; they are derived on the phone from immutable facts. A user who forges facts affects only their own data.
  • Memory-only SDK cache (RECOMMENDATION). Room is the source of truth, so the SDK's on-disk cache would be a second, unencrypted copy that can diverge and could grow to about 100 MB (INFERRED). Retries belong to the outbox, so a write counts as done only when the server confirms it.

Indicative cost. ARC-03 estimates about 15 writes and 40 reads per active user per day, roughly $0.0015 per user per month at list prices, plus 4–6 MB of storage per user after two years † (INFERRED; prices vary by location, and the free daily quota may not apply to a named database).

ARC-03 §8.1, §8.7, §8.12; ARC-04 §4.1–§4.2, §4.6; ADR-014; SEC-01 §5.1

3.4 App Check

ItemToday (EXISTING)Target (PROPOSED, ADR-008)
Debug providerInstalled in debug builds, with a debug secret read from a launch-intent extra; the debug library ships in releaseIntent injection removed. Debug library becomes debugImplementation; debug tokens registered only in the dev project
Release providerreCAPTCHA (Android artifact availability unverified)Play Integrity once the app is linked in Play Console; reCAPTCHA and its site key removed
EnforcementNot effectiveNot enforced while side-loaded GitHub builds exist. Enforce for Firestore only when distribution is Play-only and ≥ 98 % of requests verify over 14 consecutive days † (D-26)
Real control meanwhile—Firebase Auth plus security rules protect per-user data; App Check reduces automated abuse

ADR-008; SEC-01 §4.7; ARC-04 §2 row 3

3.5 Background work on the device

Work that a backend might otherwise do runs on the phone under WorkManager (ADR-005, decision D-12 Pending). WorkManager survives reboot and process death, respects Doze and supports network constraints. The app initialises it on demand with its own worker factory, so workers receive dependencies from the container.

WorkType and unique nameConstraints and back-offTrigger
SyncWorker (CMP-57)One-time, sync-<profileId>, policy KEEPNetwork connected; exponential back-off from 30 s † (WorkManager caps at 5 h)After each syncable write, app start, sign-in, user Retry
Sync safety netPeriodic 24 h †, sync-periodic-<profileId>Network connectedEnqueued at sign-in; catches writes made at the end of a run
ReminderWorker (CMP-59)One-time with initial delay, reminder-<profileId>, policy REPLACENonePreference change, each run (schedules the next), app start, time-zone change
CleanupWorker (CMP-60)Periodic 24 h, cleanup-retentionNone — retention must run within 24 h of expiryEnsured at app start; an app-start sweep also runs
  • Reminders are posted "around" the chosen local time (the UI says so honestly), skipped if a valid attempt already exists today, kept out of quiet hours 22:00–07:00 †, and use one channel, "Practice reminders", with generic text only. On Android 13+ the notification permission is requested only when the user turns reminders on.
  • Cleanup deletes expired recordings, sweeps orphan files and transcripts, retries failed AAC transcodes and prunes plans older than 90 days †.
  • Not workers: account deletion runs on the application scope and resumes from its persisted state at the next start; export runs in the ViewModel with progress because the user is waiting.
  • Every request is tagged with the profile id so sign-out and deletion can cancel all of a profile's work.

ADR-005; ADR-014; ARC-02 §7.4, CMP-57…62; ARC-04 §3.5–§3.6; SRS-BR-019 (via ARC-02)

ARC-04 §2 (integration inventory and disposition); ADR-003, ADR-004, ADR-006, ADR-010, ADR-011

Chapter 4Local intelligence (speech and feedback)

The heart of SpeakCraft is a single on-device pipeline that turns a spoken attempt into a transcript, three labelled metrics, one strength, the mistakes our checks found — each with evidence — and coach tips for the next try. It uses Android's own speech recogniser and deterministic, versioned rules. It uses no cloud AI and no language model, and when the evidence is too weak it says Inconclusive instead of guessing.

Validation still to come The tier design must be validated on real devices by spike SPK-01 before the R1 build, and grammar rules ship only after the SPK-02 corpus shows ≥ 0.90 precision †. Every threshold, band and noise band in this chapter is † Proposed and will be tuned on those corpora. Nothing here has been run.

4.1 Goals, constraints and four separate concepts

GoalConstraint
Real transcripts for every attempt where the device allowsNo cloud AI and no LLM (NG-01); platform recognisers only (ADR-009)
Honest, evidence-based metricsDeterministic rules, versioned, tested with fixtures (NFR-QUAL)
One pipeline for every template and word stageReplaces 2 scoring engines, 4 recogniser wrappers and 5 availability sources
Work offline and on 2 GB devicesStreaming audio processing; no large models (NFR-PERF-009)

The current app mixes up four things that the new design keeps apart. The UI shows a speech status only when all four are consistent and verified:

ConceptMeaningSource of truth
User preferenceWhat the user chose: recognition language, online consent, retentionDataStore speech preferences
Model or pack availabilityWhether the on-device language pack for the locale is installedcheckRecognitionSupport (API 33+); otherwise unknown
Device capabilityWhat the OS and hardware can do: API level, on-device recogniser, microphoneSpeechCapabilityService probes at start-up
Runtime availabilityWhether it works right now: recogniser busy, permission revoked, no network for T3aThe per-attempt recognition session

ARC-05 §1

4.2 Capability tiers

Android's speech recognition differs widely by API level and manufacturer. Rather than promise one experience everywhere, the app detects one of four tiers and labels it honestly.

TierDetectionCaptureTranscriptReplayUser-facing label
T1API ≥ 33, on-device recogniser available, locale pack installed, audio piping verified at first useApp's AudioRecord → WAV file and a pipe to the recogniserContinuous, on-device (segmented session)Yes"On-device transcription"
T2API ≥ 31 with an on-device recogniser, or T1 piping failedRecogniser owns the microphoneOn-device; segments restarted and stitchedNo"On-device transcription (no replay)"
T3aNo on-device recogniser, a recogniser exists, and the user consented to online transcriptionRecogniser owns the microphoneDevice's online speech service (consented)No"Online transcription (you agreed)"
T3bOtherwiseApp's AudioRecordNone — delivery metrics onlyYes"Audio-only feedback"
  • The device tier is re-evaluated at start-up and after a consent change, a locale change or a pack installation. A T1 piping failure at runtime demotes the device to T2 and is remembered.
  • A per-attempt fallback (busy recogniser, lost network in T3a) changes only that attempt's tier and result label, never the device tier.
  • On API 33+ with a downloadable but uninstalled pack, Settings and onboarding offer "Install offline speech".
  • D-14 Pending recommends minSdk 26; API 24–25 devices would only ever reach the T3 tiers. D-32 Pending decides how word practice behaves on T3b phones, where stages that need a transcript cannot be checked ("Needs a transcript").

ADR-009; ARC-05 §3; ARC-02 §6.4; D-14, D-32 (RD-99 §8)

4.3 The pipeline

New component (CMP)Replaces (EXISTING)
CaptureController + PcmTee, WavWriter (CMP-26)AudioRecorderManager (MediaRecorder AAC) and the boxed-array decode path
RecognitionSession, RecognitionSessionFactory (CMP-28, -25)Four recogniser wrappers, including a silent fallback to the network recogniser
SpeechCapabilityService (CMP-23)Five availability sources that reported unverified states
StreamingFeatureExtractor, EvidenceGate (CMP-30, -31)The feature extractor (bugs fixed, streaming) and the existing fail-closed evidence validator (kept and extended)
MetricEngine, ReferenceMatcher, RuleEngine (CMP-32…34)Parts of three scoring engines; the alignment code (relabelled "recognised as intended"); re-qualified language rules
FeedbackComposer, TipSelector, WordStageEvaluator (CMP-35, -75, -36)Five overlapping feedback generators and the vocabulary evaluator's stages 2–4
— (removed)Firebase AI Logic, Gemini coach, hybrid coordinator cloud paths, MediaPipe engines, video frame processing, YouTube extraction

ARC-05 §2; ARC-02 §2.3.4

4.4 Capture and recognition

  • Capture. AudioRecord with the voice-recognition source (falling back to the microphone source), 16 kHz, mono, 16-bit PCM in 20 ms frames. Frames fan out to the WAV writer, the feature extractor and, in T1, a pipe to the recogniser. Fixed ring buffers keep memory constant whatever the attempt length. Audio focus is requested as exclusive; its loss interrupts the attempt, as does the app going to the background.
  • One recognition session per attempt, created on the main thread and released in finally. T1 uses a segmented session over the audio pipe. T2 and T3a restart listening within 150 ms † when a segment ends and stitch segments with de-duplication of overlapping words; results arriving after Stop are accepted only for the active segment, which fixes today's duplicated text.
  • Error classification. "No match" and speech time-outs mean no text for that segment (not a capability failure); network errors in T3a let the attempt continue without a transcript; a busy recogniser gets one retry, then audio-only for that attempt; a revoked permission interrupts.
  • Confidence comes from the recogniser only when it supplies it; otherwise it is null, never a constant.
  • Punctuation independence. Formatting is requested where supported, but no rule or metric depends on punctuation; any sentence-level heuristic uses pause timing.
  • Language packs (API 33+). Installed, pending and supported languages are read from the platform; "Install offline speech" calls the platform download. The app manages no model files of its own.

ARC-05 §4; ARC-04 §3.2–§3.3

4.5 The evidence gate

The current app's fail-closed evidence validator is one of the strengths to keep. It is extended so that no score is produced without enough evidence.

CheckThreshold †Applies
Duration≥ 1.5 sAll attempts
Voiced speech≥ 0.5 s and ≥ 25 % of the durationWhere audio or level callbacks are available
LevelMean active-frame RMS above an adaptive silence floor measured in the first 300 msWhere audio is available
Transcript presence≥ 1 word (read aloud, repeat, word stages); ≥ 8 words (answer, structured answer, story)Templates that need a transcript (not T3b)
Session scopeTranscript segments carry the attempt IDT1–T3a
Clipping> 1 % of samples at full scale → quality warning, not invalidWhere audio is available

The outcome is Valid or Inconclusive(reason), with the reasons TOO_SHORT, TOO_QUIET, NO_SPEECH, NO_TRANSCRIPT and TOO_FEW_WORDS. Inconclusive attempts are saved, but they never change a word's stage, never count toward the streak or practice time, and never show partial metrics as if complete.

ARC-05 §5; SRS-BR-001, -008 (via ARC-05 and ARC-03 §8.3.2)

ARC-05 §6, §7, §10

4.7 Rules and the feedback composer

Rule qualification (SPK-02). Each grammar rule needs a corpus of at least 30 positive and 30 negative examples written as unpunctuated, lower-case, recogniser-style text, including the false positives found in the audit. A rule ships only with precision ≥ 0.90 †; recall is reported but not gated. Expected survivors include unambiguous third-person agreement ("he don't"), "more better", "didn't went", common article errors with explicit vowel-sound lists, double negatives and a wrong past participle after "have". Run-on, sentence-length and "a lot of → many" rules are dropped. At most two grammar observations are shown per attempt.

Feedback composer. A deterministic, priority-ordered table yields exactly one focus:

PriorityFocus conditionFocus text pattern (illustrative)
1InconclusiveReason-specific retry advice
2Read or repeat: words not recognised as intended"Try these words again: '{w1}', '{w2}' — listen to the slow reference first."
3Structure element missing"Add the {Result}: what changed because of what you did?"
4Fillers above band"You used '{top filler}' {n} times. Pause silently instead."
5Pace fast"Slow down slightly — aim for {target} words per minute."
6Many long pauses"Plan your next sentence before you start speaking it."
7Below the target length"Say a little more: add one example."
8Possible grammar issue"Possible issue: '{span}' → '{suggestion}'."

The strength is the best metric relative to its band, or an improvement on the previous attempt beyond the noise band ("Fewer fillers than last time: 6 → 2"). It never praises a metric that is null or an Estimated metric with weak evidence. When nothing qualifies, an honest fallback acknowledges the completed attempt and makes no skill claim. Strength codes are stored and synced as the first feedback code.

ARC-05 §8.1–§8.3; NFR-QUAL-003

4.8 Mistakes found and coach tips

After every submitted attempt — speaking activity, technique exercise, word stage or review item — the result lists the mistakes our checks found, with evidence, and coach tips for the next try (Product Owner direction, 2026-11-21; PRD-FR-047). All of it is deterministic logic over the on-device outputs above. An optional on-device generative rewording of tips would need its own ADR and is out of R1.

Issue catalogue

An issue is raised only when its source metric is non-null and lies outside the target band by more than the metric's noise band, so borderline values are never reported as mistakes.

Issue codeRaised whenEvidence shownLabel
ISSUE_WORD_NOT_RECOGNISEDAlignment marks reference words as not recognised as intendedThe words, in contextMeasured
ISSUE_WRONG_FORMA near-match to the target word (edit similarity ≥ 0.6 †) that is not the target or an accepted inflection"We heard '{heard}'"Measured
ISSUE_TARGET_WORD_MISSINGTarget word absent and no near-match (exclusive with the previous code)Target wordMeasured
ISSUE_TOO_SHORTBelow the target range; for stage 4, fewer than 5 words or 3 content wordsWords or seconds vs targetMeasured
ISSUE_STRUCT_ELEMENT_MISSINGA framework element (STAR, PREP) not foundMissing element; cue phrases foundEstimated
ISSUE_FILLERS_HIGHFiller rate above bandTop fillers with countsEstimated
ISSUE_PAUSES_LONGLong pauses above bandCount; longest pause and where it wasMeasured
ISSUE_PACE_FAST / _SLOWPace outside bandValue vs target rangeMeasured
ISSUE_HEDGING_HIGHHedging above bandHedge phrases quotedEstimated
ISSUE_LOW_VARIETYVocabulary variety below band (≥ 25 words only)Most repeated content wordsEstimated
ISSUE_GRAMMAR_POSSIBLEA qualified rule firesQuoted span → suggestion; rule IDEstimated
ISSUE_TOO_QUIETLoudness quality note"Recording was quiet or uneven"Measured (quality)

Display order. 1 too quiet (it may explain other issues) · 2 word not recognised, wrong form, target missing · 3 structure element missing · 4 fillers · 5 pace · 6 long pauses · 7 too short · 8 possible grammar · 9 hedging · 10 low variety. Ties go to the larger distance beyond the threshold, then alphabetically. Three issues are shown first and the rest sit behind "Show all". Inconclusive attempts show the evidence-gate reason instead.

MetricTarget band †Issue threshold †Noise band †Notes
Pace100–160 wpm< 100 or > 160±10 wpmRead aloud uses the reference text's natural rate ±20 %
Long pauses≤ 2 per minute of speaking> 2 per minute±1 pauseLeading and trailing silence excluded
Filler words≤ 3 per minute> 3 per minute±1/minRecognisers often drop "um/uh" — stated as a caveat
Vocabulary variety≥ 0.55< 0.55±0.05Only with ≥ 25 words
Hedging≤ 3 per 100 words> 3 per 100 words±1Estimated
LengthActivity's target rangeBelow the lower bound±5 words or ±3 sAbove the range is not a mistake in R1
Recognised as intended≥ 75 % (pass)Any reference word not recognised— (word level)The percentage carries ±5 pts
Structure · grammarAll elements · 0 hitsAny element missing · ≥ 1 hit— (discrete)Grammar: qualified rules only, at most 2 shown

Techniques and activities in the content pack may carry issue overrides so that feedback never contradicts the technique being practised — for example a deliberate-pause technique suppresses "long pauses" or re-bands pace. Overrides change only the "mistake" interpretation, never metric values or labels (RECOMMENDATION, CL-60).

Coach-tip selection

  1. Candidates are content-pack tips whose triggers match the attempt: an issue code, a metric and band, a template, or a technique ID.
  2. They are ranked by trigger specificity (issue code > metric band > technique > template), then by the tip's priority, then by a stable hash of tipId + attemptId. The hash varies wording across attempts while staying reproducible.
  3. The focus tip ("Try this next") is the top tip for the highest-priority issue and doubles as the focus. Up to two more tips cover the next issues or, failing that, the activity's technique.
  4. If no issue is found, the result says "Our checks didn't find issues in this attempt", names what the checks cover, shows the strength and gives one general tip for the technique.
  5. Each tip links to its technique (SCR-PRAC-04) and, where provenance exists, to the specific source video.

Self-check. Some technique aspects cannot be measured from audio and transcript — eye contact, gestures, facial expression. For those, the result offers a short checklist the user ticks. Answers are stored as self-reported and are never shown as Measured or Estimated or used in trends.

Candidate acoustic metrics (not in the R1 catalogue): pitch range in semitones and volume variation in decibels, T1 only, approved only if they pass SPK-01 on the reference devices — and never named "melody", "expressiveness" or "pronunciation".

ARC-05 §8.4, §6 (band table); ARC-03 §6.8–§6.9; ARC-02 CMP-35, CMP-75

4.9 Word-stage evaluation

StageCheckScorePass †
2 Say itTranscript tokens vs target: exact, listed inflection, or near-match (edit similarity ≥ 0.8 on single-token output)100 exact · 90 inflection · 75–89 near-match · < 75 other≥ 75
3 Use itRecognised as intended on each given sentenceM-MATCHBoth ≥ 75
4 Make your ownTarget present and ≥ 5 words (both required); grammar hits ≤ 1 (required); composite = 50 (target + length) + 25 × (1 − grammar hits) + 25 × (≥ 3 content words)0–100≥ 70 and all required

Inflections are accepted only from explicit lists in the content pack — not from stem-prefix guesses that accepted "convenient" for "convey". Word-task attempts use the evidence gate and count toward the streak. Users are told: "Speech recognition may recognise a word even when it isn't pronounced clearly, so 'Say it' checks that you were understood, not your accent."

ARC-05 §9

4.10 Versioning, testing and performance

  • evaluationVersion is the semantic version of the rule set, metric definitions and issue catalogue. It is stored with every attempt and changes are listed in a changelog; history is not re-evaluated. Tips are versioned with the content pack's contentVersion.
  • A fixture suite of WAV recordings (silence, noise, short, normal, fast, many pauses) and transcripts has golden outputs per version and runs on the JVM in CI.
  • "How we measured this" text is generated from the metric catalogue entries, a single source.
Step (60 s attempt on RD-MID)Budget †
Streaming featuresReal time during capture (< 10 % CPU)
Recogniser finalisationPlatform-dependent; target ≤ 1.5 s, to be measured
Metrics + rules + feedback≤ 200 ms
Database write≤ 50 ms

ARC-05 §4.4, §8.1, §12; ADR-009; ARC-04 §3.12

Chapter 5Database and data model

Learning data lives in two Room databases on the phone, preferences in two DataStores, recordings in a folder that is never backed up, and content in a read-only pack inside the app. For signed-in users, six kinds of Firestore document mirror the learning facts — metrics only, never audio, file paths or transcripts. This chapter describes those stores, the 18 entities, the cloud schema, the sync and conflict rules, migrations, the export file and the content pack.

5.1 Design principles

IDPrincipleConsequence
DP-01Local source of truthlearning.db is authoritative; Firestore is a replica, never read on a UI path
DP-02/03Immutable facts, derived aggregatesAttempts, metrics, word reviews and events are append-only; update triggers block changes. No streak, total, count or score column exists anywhere
DP-04No audio, paths or transcripts in the cloud by defaultAudio stays on the phone; transcripts live in sensitive.db, excluded from backup and never synced in R1
DP-05Versioning everywhereRoom versions with exported schemas; schemaVersion on every cloud document; pack and schema versions on the content pack; evaluationVersion and contentVersion on attempts
DP-06Idempotency keysEvery fact has a client-generated UUID that is the same locally and in the cloud; natural duplicates are blocked by unique keys
DP-07UTC instants plus a local date keyTimes are epoch milliseconds; each fact also stores the local YYYY-MM-DD date captured at event time, never recomputed
DP-08Profile partitioningEvery learning row carries profile_id; every query is scoped to it; child rows are tied to their parent's profile by composite keys
DP-09One transaction per user actionAn attempt save writes attempt, metrics, issues, step status, events, word state and outbox rows together
DP-10Null means unknownA metric without evidence is NULL with a reason; no column has a default score, confidence or baseline
DP-11/12Explicit retention; content separate from user dataEach entity has a retention rule and a named job; user data references content by id plus version, so retired content stays readable in history

ARC-03 §2

5.2 Local stores

StoreLocationEngineHoldsBackup and device transfer (D-23)
learning.dbdatabases/learning.dbRoom, LearningDatabaseENT-01, -02, -03 (attempt part), -04…-13, -15…-17Included (include-only rule)
sensitive.dbdatabases/sensitive.dbRoom, SensitiveDatabaseTranscripts (ENT-03 part), sync watermarks (ENT-14), account-deletion state (ENT-18)Excluded
settingsfiles/datastore/settings.preferences_pbPreferences DataStoreDevice and UI preferences: theme, reduce motion, recognition locale, reference speed, reminder on/off, seen tipsIncluded
device_statefiles/datastore/device_state.preferences_pbPreferences DataStoreInstall identity (deviceId), online-transcription consent record, tier cache, crash-recovery markersExcluded
RecordingsnoBackupFilesDir/recordings/<profileId>/Files (ADR-015)Audio — WAV during the attempt, AAC/M4A after evaluation; metadata in ENT-11Excluded by location and by rule
Content packAPK assets/content/Read-only JSON loaded into memoryTracks, templates, activities, techniques, tips, wordsn/a

Two Room databases are used because the transcripts (sensitive voice data, class C1) and device-scoped technical state must never leave the phone through Android backup. A restored sync watermark or deletion state would be wrong on a new device. The price is that the two databases cannot share a transaction, so the design uses a crash-safe write order with compensation and a start-up orphan sweep.

ARC-03 §3.0; SEC-01 §3.2, §6.3; ADR-015; ARC-02 §7.5, TQ-15
