Room cannot express CHECK constraints, so integrity is enforced in three layers: Kotlin types and converters, repository validation with unit tests, and a small set of SQLite triggers for invariants that must hold even against a faulty DAO — mainly the immutability of facts. Indexes are designed from a query catalogue so that the Today and Progress queries stay within 50 ms † on RD-LOW with two years of daily data (about 3,000 attempts).

ARC-03 §3.0–§3.19, §4, §5; NFR-PERF-008

5.4 Firestore target schema

ARC-03 §8 is the authoritative list of what leaves the device: nothing is synced unless it appears there. Only the signed-in owner's subtree exists:

users/{uid}                          ENT-01 + ENT-02   mutable, last-writer-wins per document
users/{uid}/attempts/{attemptId}     ENT-03 + ENT-04   immutable fact (create-only)
users/{uid}/plans/{dateKey}          ENT-05 + ENT-06   mutable, last-writer-wins + step preservation
users/{uid}/words/{wordId}           ENT-07            mutable, merge by most advanced stage
users/{uid}/events/{eventId}         ENT-09 (+ ENT-08) immutable fact (create-only)
users/{uid}/meta/sync                markers           mutable, field-wise
(R2 only, if D-16 is approved)  users/{uid}/transcripts/{attemptId}
DocumentMain fieldsSize †
users/{uid}schemaVersion, level (L1–L5), goal (one of five tracks), dailyMinutes (5/10/15); optional displayName, reminderTime, timeZone; prefs (daily word goal 0–5, review reminders, recording retention); createdAt, updatedAt, editedAt, deviceId. No email≈ 0.35 KB
attempts/{attemptId}Template, content id and version, evaluationVersion, result (EVALUATED · PASS · FAIL · INCONCLUSIVE), start time, dateKey, duration, metrics map (measured), estimates map, feedbackCodes (strength code plus the top three issue codes — no counts or evidence), optional plan date, step index and target word≈ 0.75 KB
plans/{dateKey}Generator version, minutes, 1–4 steps (kind, content id, status PENDING · DONE · SKIPPED, optional attempt id), completion time≈ 0.5 KB
words/{wordId}Stage 0–4, status (NEW · LEARNING · LEARNED · REVIEWING · MASTERED), next review date, interval, ease, reviews, lapses≈ 0.3 KB
events/{eventId}Type (SESSION_COMPLETED · WORD_STAGE_PASSED · WORD_REVIEWED), occurrence time, dateKey, small typed payload≈ 0.35 KB
meta/syncHighest data schema written, legacy-migration state, clear-history and reset-vocabulary markers, account-deletion marker≈ 0.25 KB

Conventions. Every document carries schemaVersion = 1; a client that reads a higher version never overwrites it and pauses uploads. Server times (createdAt, updatedAt, syncedAt) are server timestamps; device times are bounded by the rules to the window [now − 400 days, now + 5 minutes] †. Optional fields are omitted rather than written as null. Explicit mappers (no bean mapping) have unit tests asserting that every outgoing key is on the allow-list.

Never synced (normative) Audio in any form; recording rows, file names and paths; transcripts, segments and every quoted span; issue evidence (values, counts, token indices) and any issue beyond the top three codes; feedback parameters; recogniser confidence; tier and recognition locale; Inconclusive reasons; metric bands and targets; accuracy ratings and self-check answers; saved activities; notebook, inbox and quiz data; outbox, sync and deletion state; onboarding state; every DataStore key; the email address; consent records.

Every document is far below Firestore's 1 MiB limit, and two years of one user's data comes to about 4 MB in total †. No composite indexes are needed: every query has one range filter and an orderBy on the same field within one subcollection (to be confirmed on the emulator).

ARC-03 §8.1–§8.6, §8.9; SEC-01 §3.3, §5.1

5.5 Sync and conflict rules

  • Local first. Every write goes to Room in one transaction and returns immediately; each syncable change adds or coalesces one outbox row (ADR-014).
  • Upload. The sync worker drains the outbox in order — meta and purges, profile, attempts, events, words, plans, deletes — when signed in and online. Facts are created in batches; a retried create that the rules deny is checked with a server read and acknowledged if the identical document already exists, so retries never duplicate. Mutable documents are written in a transaction that reads the server copy, applies the merge rule and writes the result. Outbox rows move through PENDING → IN_FLIGHT → acknowledged, with FAILED and back-off, and DEAD (with a user-visible Retry) after repeated failures.
  • Pull. At sign-in, at app start when online, after an upload run and on manual Retry, the worker reads meta/sync and the profile, then pages through attempts, words, plans and events changed since a per-collection watermark (minus a 60 s † overlap), 200 † documents per page, merging each page into Room in one transaction. Facts are pulled by syncedAt, mutable documents by updatedAt.
  • Markers. Deletions are invisible to watermark pulls, so "Clear history", "Reset vocabulary" and account deletion write a marker in meta/sync before deleting cloud documents; other devices apply the marker on their next pull and do not re-upload purged items.
DocumentConflict rule
Attempts, eventsUnion. Same id = same fact (insert-or-ignore). Never updated
Profile and preferencesLast writer wins per document, by client editedAt, then deviceId
PlansLast writer wins, then step preservation: per index a DONE step beats a non-DONE step, so completed practice is never lost; statuses are re-derived from local valid attempts
WordsStage = maximum; schedule fields from the side with the later next-review date; reviews and lapses = maximum; status recomputed
meta/syncField-wise: markers take the later timestamp; a completed legacy migration dominates; the deletion marker is sticky
Worked exampleOutcome
Phone A and tablet B are both offline on 2026-11-21; A records attempt a1, B records b1Both upload. Each device ends with both attempts. The streak counts the day once; practice time adds both durations
A uploads a1, then the connection drops before the acknowledgementThe retried create is denied; a server read finds a1; the outbox row is acknowledged. No duplicate
A (offline) changes level L3 → L4 at 10:00; B (online) changes daily minutes to 15 at 11:00; A reconnects at 12:00The server copy is newer, so A ends with level L3 and 15 minutes. A's level change is lost — the accepted cost of per-document last-writer-wins (open question OQ-02)
Both devices generated today's plan offline; A completes step 0, B later completes step 1B's plan wins, but step preservation keeps A's completed step 0: both steps end DONE

ADR-014; ARC-03 §8.8–§8.11; ARC-04 §4.3–§4.5; ARC-02 §6.3

5.6 Migrations and legacy import

Schema versioning policy

RuleDetail (PROPOSED)
Two version lineslearning.db and sensitive.db each start at version 1, counted from the first distributed build that contains Room
Exported schemasOne schema JSON per version committed under app/schemas/; CI fails if the generated schema differs or changed without a version bump
Forward-only, never destructiveEach step n → n+1 is an auto-migration or hand-written SQL. Destructive fallback is never used in a distributed build. Downgrade is unsupported; if detected, the app asks the user to update and leaves the files untouched
Heavy fixesLogic that needs the content pack or evaluation code runs as a one-off WorkManager job after the database opens, not inside a migration
Cloud documentsschemaVersion stays 1 while changes are additive. Rules are deployed before the app version that writes a new field. A breaking change bumps the version and older clients pause uploads
Test harnessInstrumented migration tests for every step and the full chain, with seeded fixtures, integrity and foreign-key checks, trigger presence and preserved outbox rows; emulator-based tests for the legacy import (test cases TC-MIG, all Not run)
StoreVersionReleaseContents
learning.db1R1ENT-01…13 and ENT-15 tables (including attempt_issue), indexes, triggers
learning.db2R2Inbox (ENT-16) and quiz (ENT-17) tables by auto-migration
sensitive.db1R1Transcripts, sync state (ENT-14), deletion state (ENT-18)
sensitive.db2R2 if D-16Transcript cloud-sync columns
Cloud documents · content packschema 1R1–R2Additive changes only; techniques, tips with triggers and provenance included from the start

Legacy import (D-19: profile and vocabulary only)

D-19 Pending recommends importing only the legacy profile (display name, level, goal, daily minutes) and vocabulary progress. Speaking sessions, plans, routines, programmes, activity progress, notifications, streaks, counters and scores are not imported — they are either client-written aggregates or unvalidated scores. The import runs in the background on the first launch of an R1 build with a Firebase session, never blocks use, retries on later launches and is idempotent.

Legacy fieldRule
Display nameTrimmed; blank or the legacy default "Speaker" becomes empty; at most 40 characters
Communication levelBeginner → L1, Elementary → L2, Intermediate → L3, Upper intermediate → L4, Advanced → L5; anything else → L3
Free-text goalKeyword match to one of the five tracks (interview, present/story, work/meeting, pronunciation/accent); otherwise the default track — a heuristic the user can correct
Daily minutes≤ 7 → 5; 8–12 → 10; ≥ 13 → 15
VocabularyWords matching the content pack become word progress at stage 1 "Learn" at most — spoken stages are never credited because legacy reviews were self-rated flashcards. Unmatched and custom words go to the notebook table
Streaks, totals, scores, self-ratingsNot imported
PhaseWhenWhat happens to legacy cloud data
L0R0Client writes to shared and admin collections frozen
L1R1New rules; legacy user subcollections become read-and-delete only. The import reads but does not modify them; account deletion also removes them
L2End of the migration window †An operator script deletes shared and admin collections and remaining legacy subcollections; legacy rules removed (default deny). Users who never upgraded lose legacy data, disclosed in advance
L3After L2Stale blueprint entries and the old rules tests replaced
Migration window The legacy-data migration window is R1 + 90 days †, the same in ARC-03 §9.6, ARC-02, SEC-01 and RD-99 D-19 (aligned on 2026-11-21). The Product Owner confirms or changes it when recording D-19.

Legacy local stores (JSON files, old DataStores, the API-key Keystore entry, old recording, backup, update and model folders, and the old Firestore disk cache) are deleted once, after the import has read what it needs. Release notes state that speaking history and recordings from the previous version are not carried over.

ARC-03 §9.1–§9.7; SEC-01 §5.6; D-19 (RD-99 §8)

5.7 Export file

Users can export all their personal data offline and without an account (PRD-FR-093). The export is one streamed UTF-8 JSON file, speakcraft-export-YYYYMMDD-HHmm.json, with format = "speakcraft-export" and formatVersion = 1. Signed-in users get a pull first when online, and the file states the time it is synced up to. It is written through the Storage Access Framework, so the user chooses where it goes and no temporary copy is left behind (Chapter 6).

Top-level keyContent
app, syncApp version, content-pack version, evaluationVersion; signed-in flag and synced-up-to time
profile, preferences, deviceSettingsENT-01, ENT-02 and the device settings (theme, reduce motion, recognition locale, reference speed, reminder on/off)
attemptsEach attempt with metrics (label, value or null with a reason, band, target), mistakes (issue code, label, rendered text, evidence), feedback (strength, focus, tips — re-derived), transcript while kept, accuracy rating, self-check answers marked SELF_REPORTED, and recording metadata
plans, words, wordReviews, events, savedActivities, notebookENT-05…09, ENT-12, ENT-15; R2 adds inbox and quizzes
notIncludedA plain-language list: audio recordings (unless the ZIP option is chosen), device identifiers, sign-in tokens, the online-transcription consent record, sync technical state, the diagnostic log

Optional recordings ZIP (D-22 Pending): an off-by-default option "Include my recordings (ZIP)" with a warning that recordings contain the user's voice. The ZIP holds the same JSON, the retained recordings named by attempt id only, and a plain README; its size is shown first (about 30 MB at the default 30-day retention †). The security alternative is JSON only in R1.

ARC-03 §10; SEC-01 §9.1; D-22 (RD-99 §8)

5.8 The content pack

All learning content is bundled, versioned JSON in the APK, parsed once per process with kotlinx-serialization into in-memory indexes (about 0.9 MB for R1 †, INFERRED). There is no remote content in R1–R2. The pack implements the decided content rule D-08 Decided: the techniques, frameworks, exercises and tips taught on the source YouTube channel are re-expressed in SpeakCraft's own words and examples, each linked to its specific source video, with no creator name, likeness or branding.

assets/content/
  manifest.json            packId, packVersion (monotonic), schemaVersion, counts, file checksums
  tracks.json              exactly 5: Everyday, Work, Interview, Present, Clear speech
  templates.json           display text only; behaviour lives in code (Chapter 4)
  techniques.json          new content type
  tips.json                coach tips with structured triggers
  activities/              trk-everyday · trk-work · trk-interview · trk-present · trk-clear · shared-warmups · onboarding
  words/                   words-l1.json … words-l5.json
build-support (not shipped): content-ids.lock · branding-blocklist.txt

Provenance and licence status

"provenance": {
  "sourceVideoId": "AbCdEfGhIjK",
  "sourceUrl": "https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AbCdEfGhIjK&t=75s",
  "timestampStart": 75, "timestampEnd": 142,
  "techniqueNameAsTaught": "(internal reference only — never displayed)",
  "verifiedBy": "content-reviewer-02", "verifiedOn": "2026-12-03",
  "notes": "Re-expressed in own words; examples rewritten for workplace track"
},
"licenceStatus": "ORIGINAL_REEXPRESSION",
"attributionText": null
licenceStatusMeaningIn a release build?
ORIGINALWritten from scratch by SpeakCraft (all R1 words)Yes
ORIGINAL_REEXPRESSIONDerived from a specific channel video and re-expressed in SpeakCraft's own wording and examples (D-08); full provenance requiredYes
LICENSEDReserved for future licensed contentNone in R1
PENDING_REVIEWNot yet matched to a specific video and verifiedNever — debug builds only, with a visible badge

Provenance is internal metadata and is never displayed, except through the outbound "Watch the original lesson" link, which opens sourceUrl in the YouTube app or a browser. The URL must be a specific video — never a channel root, handle or playlist.

Techniques and tips

  • Technique: a plain original name (generic names such as STAR and PREP are allowed; distinctive names coined by the creator stay only in provenance), a one-line summary, "why it works", 2–7 steps, its tips (at least one general tip), related activities, target metrics drawn only from the ARC-05 catalogue (empty when no honest metric exists), up to 6 self-check items, optional issue overrides, tracks and levels.
  • Tip: its technique (or none, for global general tips), text of up to 240 characters in our own words with optional placeholders, a priority, and triggers. A trigger combines any of issue code, metric key plus band, template id and technique id; a tip with no triggers is the general tip for its technique. Selection follows Chapter 4.8, and because every input is stored, the tips shown for an attempt are reproducible.
  • Activities list at least one technique or are marked general; fields such as reference text, inflections and expected framework elements are immutable per id, because stored evidence refers to them.

Build-time validation

A JVM Gradle task validateContentPack runs before every build and in CI and fails the build on any violation. Selected rules:

RuleCheck
V-01 · V-02 · V-03Schema conformance with unknown keys rejected; ids unique and never deleted; referential integrity in both directions
V-07Immutable fields unchanged for existing ids (hash in content-ids.lock)
V-10No PENDING_REVIEW item in a release build
V-11 · V-12Re-expressed items carry full provenance; sourceUrl is a specific video URL whose id matches; attribution text is null for every R1 item
V-13 · V-14No verbatim-transcript fields exist in the schema; no creator name, handle, branding or creator-coined framework name in any displayed field (blocklist)
V-15 · V-16Every issue code has at least one matching tip; every technique a general tip; every template a global general tip; triggers reference valid codes, metrics, bands, templates and techniques
V-17 · V-22Technique target metrics are catalogue keys only; self-check items never reference metrics; issue overrides are valid
Minimum R1 content †Count
TracksExactly 5
Activities≥ 15 per track (≥ 75 total), with warm-ups and main tasks in each level band, plus a shared warm-up pool of ≥ 30
Words≥ 300; ≥ 40 per level; ≥ 50 tagged per track
TechniquesEvery technique that passes re-verification; D-36 Pending recommends a curated subset of about 15 for R1
Tips≥ 1 per issue code; ≥ 1 general per technique; ≥ 1 global general per template

Moving existing content. Content migration is editorial work in epic EP-09 (about 38 person-days †, mostly the Product Owner's): inventory the existing items; for creator-derived items, find the specific official video and timestamps by hand (no scraping or caption download); rewrite in original wording and examples; fill provenance; have a second reviewer check wording and branding; only then mark the item active. Items that cannot be matched stay PENDING_REVIEW and are excluded from release builds.

ARC-03 §6.1–§6.14; D-08, D-36 (RD-99 §8); ADR-011 (revised); CNT-01 (via ARC-03)

Chapter 6APIs and integrations

Because SpeakCraft has no server, "API" here means three things: the internal Kotlin interfaces between packages, the Android platform APIs the app relies on, and the Firebase SDK operations it performs. This chapter specifies each, the integrations that are retired, the typed error model and how versions stay compatible. It invents no backend endpoint.

6.1 Principle: no backend APIs

#Principle
P-1SpeakCraft has no server of its own and none is proposed: no REST or GraphQL APIs, no Cloud Functions, no admin endpoints. Any server-side component needs an ADR
P-2The device talks only to managed services through their SDKs, and only when the user is signed in: Firebase Authentication (Google via Credential Manager) and Cloud Firestore (existing named database). In no-account mode the app makes no network request
P-3"API" therefore means Android platform APIs, Firebase SDK contracts and Firestore document operations, and internal module interfaces
P-4Every interface in this chapter is PROPOSED; every integration described as present today is EXISTING with source evidence in ARC-04
P-5A new external integration — SDK, endpoint, embedded web content or permission — requires an ADR, the SEC-01 privacy review and Product Owner approval
P-6Speech audio never leaves the device through SpeakCraft. The only exception is tier T3a, where the device's own speech service — not SpeakCraft — receives audio after explicit consent

ARC-04 §1

6.2 Internal module interfaces

ARC-04 §5 defines 25 interfaces: 16 required ones and 9 supporting ones. They live in data/repository (data) or the owning speech.* package; engines live in domain.*. No Firebase or Room type appears in any signature. Every suspend function is main-safe, every Flow is cold and distinct, reads are scoped to the active profile internally, writes keyed by client UUIDs are idempotent, and each interface has a hand-written fake for tests.

// core/common/result — CMP-11 (illustrative, PROPOSED)
sealed interface Outcome<out T, out E : AppError> {
    data class Ok<out T>(val value: T) : Outcome<T, Nothing>
    data class Err<out E : AppError>(val error: E) : Outcome<Nothing, E>
}

interface ProfileRepository {                                   // CMP-45; ENT-01
    val activeProfile: Flow<Profile?>
    val onboardingState: Flow<OnboardingState?>                 // drives the start destination
    suspend fun ensureLocalProfile(): Profile                     // first launch: local UUID, no network
    suspend fun updateProfile(edit: ProfileEdit): DataResult<Unit> // writes an outbox row; never resets history
    suspend fun adoptForAccount(uid: String, mode: AdoptionMode): DataResult<AdoptionReport>
    suspend fun markSignedOut(keepLocal: Boolean): DataResult<Unit>
}

ARC-04 §5.0–§5.23; ARC-02 §3.4

6.3 Platform API contracts

Platform facts below are INFERRED from the Android API reference (not re-checked online in Stage A). Items marked SPK-01 must be verified on the reference devices before the R1 build.

API levelCapability usedImpact
24–25AudioRecord, legacy audio focus, createSpeechRecognizer, TTS, MediaCodec, WorkManager, Credential Manager via Play servicesT3a/T3b only; java.time unavailable without desugaring
26+AudioFocusRequest, notification channels—
29+Recording callbacks and "client silenced" detectionDetect a microphone silenced by the OS (SPK-01)
30+Package visibility: <queries> for the recognition service (and probably the TTS service); privacy-sensitive captureRecognition may fail without <queries>
31+isOnDeviceRecognitionAvailable, createOnDeviceSpeechRecognizerT2
33+EXTRA_AUDIO_SOURCE, EXTRA_SEGMENTED_SESSION, segment callbacks, checkRecognitionSupport, triggerModelDownload, formatting; POST_NOTIFICATIONST1, language packs, notification permission
34+Model-download listener; word-level timing and confidencePack progress; optional word timing (SPK-01)

SpeechRecognizer

  • createOnDeviceSpeechRecognizer is the only constructor for T1/T2 and never uses the network by design. createSpeechRecognizer is used for T3a only, and only when the consent store holds a valid consent for the current disclosure version and recogniser package; otherwise the factory refuses and the attempt runs audio-only (TC-SEC-015). The old silent fallback to the network recogniser is removed.
  • EXTRA_PREFER_OFFLINE is advisory and is never treated as a privacy control.
  • All calls and callbacks run on the main thread; one instance per attempt; destroy() in finally. The manifest declares <queries> for android.speech.RecognitionService.
Platform error (value)Mapped toBehaviour
No match (7), speech timeout (6)NoMatch, SpeechTimeoutNo text for the segment; restart while recording; never a capability failure
Network timeout (1), network (2), server (4), server disconnected (11)NetworkT3a: attempt continues without a transcript
Recogniser busy (8), too many requests (10)BusyOne retry, then audio-only for the attempt
Audio (3), client (5)Audio, ClientAudio-only for the attempt; client errors ignored during our own stop
Insufficient permissions (9)PermissionInterrupt the attempt
Language not supported / unavailable (12, 13)LanguageUnavailableRe-probe capability; offer the pack install
Cannot check support (14, 15)CannotCheckSupportPack state "unknown" — never shown as ready

Codes and values are INFERRED and must be confirmed against the compile SDK in Stage B.

Other platform contracts

APIContract in the target design
AudioRecord and audio focusVoice-recognition source (fallback: microphone), 16 kHz mono 16-bit; buffer at least twice the minimum or 100 ms; blocking reads of 20 ms frames on the audio thread. Exclusive transient focus; any loss interrupts. Free storage is checked before recording (maximum seconds × 32 kB + 5 MB †). WAV written to a .part file and renamed at the end. In T2/T3a the app does not open AudioRecord; level comes from the recogniser's callbacks
T1 recogniser pipeA ParcelFileDescriptor pipe: the read side goes to the recogniser as its audio source; a dedicated thread writes little-endian PCM from a bounded queue; closing it ends input. A stall marks the attempt audio-only and demotes the device to T2 (SPK-01)
TextToSpeechInitialised with the application context in the recognition locale; rates 1.0 and 0.75; stopped before any recording lease; shut down in onCleared. A <queries> entry for the TTS service on API 30+ is added if SPK-01 confirms it is needed
WorkManagerOn-demand initialisation through the Application's configuration and worker factory; the default initializer removed in the manifest; unique work names and policies as in Chapter 3; every request tagged with the profile id; work-testing for tests
NotificationsOne channel "Practice reminders". On API 33+ the permission is requested only when the user turns reminders on; if denied, Settings links to the system notification settings with no repeated prompts. Generic text only; immutable pending intent to MainActivity with no extras
Credential Manager and Firebase AuthActivity context passed per call, never stored. Explicit sign-in with the Google sign-in option and a nonce where supported; silent restore with authorised accounts; token exchange with signInWithCredential; re-authenticate and assert the same uid before deletion; sign-out clears credential state, signs out, terminates Firestore and clears its persistence
Export — Storage Access FrameworkCreateDocument("application/json") (ZIP: application/zip) → the user picks a location → streamed write; no FileProvider and no temporary copy. A FileProvider limited to a diagnostics/ cache path remains only for "Share diagnostics". ARC-04 keeps a share-sheet alternative specified, but SAF is the security-authoritative recommendation
Outbound video link (ACTION_VIEW)"Watch the original lesson" starts ACTION_VIEW with the browsable category inside a try/catch ("No app can open this link"). The URL comes only from the bundled, build-validated pack and must match a host allow-list (www.youtube.com, youtube.com, m.youtube.com with /watch?v= and an 11-character id, or youtu.be/<id>). No Data API, no API key, no scraping, no WebView or embedded player, no thumbnails. SpeakCraft itself makes no network request; the privacy text says YouTube's own policy applies
Playback and transcodingMediaPlayer on the app-private file for replay; MediaCodec AAC encoder (about 24–32 kbps †, 16 kHz mono) and MediaMuxer MPEG-4 after evaluation; the WAV is deleted only after a verified M4A (encoder availability on API 24–28 checked in SPK-01)
Manifest elementTarget
PermissionsINTERNET, RECORD_AUDIO, POST_NOTIFICATIONS (33+), plus reviewed library-merged permissions. Removed: CAMERA. Never REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES
<queries>Recognition service; TTS service after SPK-01 confirms the need
ApplicationNamed Application class; network security configuration with cleartext disabled; backup attributes per D-23
ComponentsOnly MainActivity exported; FileProvider for diagnostics only; WorkManager initializer removed

AspectRule (PROPOSED)
Query shapesAttempts and events: syncedAt ≥ watermark − overlap, ordered by syncedAt then document id, 200 † per page, continued with startAfter. Plans and words: the same on updatedAt. Deletion pages of 400; emptiness verified with a server read of one document
Write semanticsFacts in batches with server syncedAt; a denied create triggers a per-document existence check (acknowledge if present, otherwise dead-letter). Mutable documents in a transaction that reads the server copy, merges and writes. Every await is wrapped in a time-out (30 s †) inside the worker — never on a UI path
Retry and back-offTransient errors (unavailable, deadline exceeded, resource exhausted, network, our time-out) → FAILED with exponential back-off and jitter †; unauthenticated → stop sync, keep local data; a newer schema → pause uploads and ask the user to update; rules rejections → dead-letter with a PII-free diagnostic
Client settingsMemory-only cache (recommended); lazy initialisation after sign-in; App Check provider installed before the first call; SDK logging disabled in release

ARC-04 §4.1–§4.6; ARC-03 §8.8–§8.9

ARC-04 §2

6.6 Typed error model

Errors are values. Each carries a stable log code and never a user-facing message; Chapter 2.7 maps each to UI behaviour. Exceptions are caught at the boundary; only CancellationException crosses an interface.

FamilyMembersTypical sources
DataErrorWriteFailed, StorageFull, NotFound, ConstraintViolation, ProfileChangedSQLite exceptions (an ignored duplicate fact insert is not an error)
ContentErrorMissingItemRetired content referenced by history
CaptureErrorPermissionDenied, MicUnavailable, AudioFocusLost, Backgrounded, Silenced, StorageFull, DeviceAudioRecord states, permission results, ENOSPC
RecognitionErrorNoMatch, SpeechTimeout, Network, Busy, Audio, Client, Permission, LanguageUnavailable, CannotCheckSupport, Unavailable, ConsentRequired, PipeDegradedRecogniser error codes 1–15; the consent guard
EvaluationErrorTimeout, InternalPipeline time-out
AuthErrorCancelled, NoCredential, Interrupted, Config, Offline, InvalidCredential, UserInvalid, ReauthRequired, RateLimited, DifferentAccount, UnknownCredential Manager and Firebase Auth exceptions
SyncErrorNotSignedIn, Offline, Transient, AppCheckFailed, SchemaRejected, NewerSchema, DeletionPending, RestorePaused, UnauthenticatedFirestore exception codes
DeletionError, TtsError, PackInstallError, ExportErrorOffline, re-auth, different account, cloud, local · unavailable, language missing · not supported, download failed · cancelled, storage full, write failedDeletion executor; TTS initialisation; pack download; SAF result

ARC-04 §6

6.7 Versioning and compatibility

IdentifierApplies toRule
schemaVersionEvery Firestore document (1 in R1)Additive changes within a version; a new required field or meaning bumps it; rules accept old and new and are deployed before the app
maxDataSchemaVersionmeta/syncA client that sees a higher value than it supports stops uploading and asks the user to update, so last-writer-wins cannot overwrite newer data
Room versionslearning.db, sensitive.dbNever destructive; downgrade unsupported; migration tests in CI
evaluationVersionEvery attemptSemantic version of rules, metric definitions and issue catalogue; history is not re-evaluated; trends compare attempts within one major version (RECOMMENDATION)
contentVersion / pack schemaVersionAttempts; the bundled packAttempts keep content id + version; retired content stays readable; build-time validation
generatorVersionPlansA new generator never rewrites completed steps
Export formatVersionExport file (1)Bumped only on a breaking change; no importer in R1
Consent disclosureVersionT3a consent recordA material disclosure change or a new default recogniser package requires new consent
Open issue — tips on old attempts Only the current content pack ships with the app, so tips re-derived for an old attempt after a pack update could come from newer content, or be missing if a technique was retired. ARC-04 II-04 asks for a decision before R1 content updates: store the shown tip ids, or label re-derived tips on old attempts as current advice.

ARC-04 §7, §9.2 (II-04)

6.8 What SPK-01 must verify

#Item
V-1 · V-2Audio-source piping accepted and continuous with acceptable finalisation latency; segmented-session behaviour when the pipe closes
V-3T2 restart gap ≤ 150 ms † and word loss at segment boundaries
V-4 · V-5Language-pack support check and download per manufacturer; word timing and confidence availability (API 34)
V-6 · V-7 · V-8AudioRecord levels against the evidence-gate silence floor; focus loss on incoming calls; silenced-microphone detection; concurrency when the recogniser owns the microphone

ARC-04 §3.12; ARC-05 §4.4

Chapter 7Infrastructure and environments

SpeakCraft's infrastructure is small: developer machines, GitHub Actions, two Firebase projects and the distribution channels. This chapter describes the environments, build variants, signing and distribution that the architecture depends on. Pipelines, release procedures and operations are covered in Part IV.

Status Everything here is a RECOMMENDATION in ENG-01 and awaits approval at gate G4 (decisions D-27…D-31). No GitHub, Play Console or Firebase setting was inspected online; their current state is unknown unless visible in the repository.

7.1 Toolchain

ItemStandard (PROPOSED)
GradleCommitted wrapper for Gradle 9.3.1 with a distribution checksum (today only the wrapper properties are committed); CI uses ./gradlew only
JDK21 to run Gradle (matching CI); bytecode target stays Java 11 unless ADR-006 changes it
Android SDKPlatform 36, build-tools 36.0.0
IDECurrent stable Android Studio that supports AGP 9.1.1 (confirmed in Stage B)
Node.js and Firebase CLINode 20 LTS only for the Firestore rules tests; the CLI installed from a committed lockfile, never globally

A contributor without Firebase access can still build and run the full no-account core loop: ./gradlew assembleDebug testDebugUnitTest lintDebug works with no google-services.json, because the web client id is resolved at runtime and a missing value simply hides sign-in (ED-03 option C). Release builds fail if their configuration is missing, so production is never built without it.

ENG-01 §3.1–§3.3

One more project to configure

Automated CI tests never use a real project: they run against the Firestore emulator with a demo- project id.

Feature flags (Chapter 2.8) are declared per build type. D-15 decides the identity: changing the applicationId would make a new app (existing installs could not update, local legacy data would become unreachable, and a new Firebase registration and OAuth client would be needed). The recommendation is to change only the code namespace to app.speakcraft and to decide before the first Play upload, so the identity changes at most once.

ENG-01 §10; RD-99 §7.3

7.5 Signing (D-28)

PhaseKey that signs distributed buildsNotes
Interim (GitHub Releases, before Play)Project-owned app signing keyCI signs only inside the protected release environment
Play (recommended)Google signs with the same project-owned key under Play App Signing; CI signs bundles with a separate upload keyGitHub-installed builds can then update to Play builds without reinstalling (INFERRED; to verify at onboarding)
AlternativePlay generates the app signing keyGitHub testers reinstall once at the Play transition
  • Existing debug-signed installs must reinstall in every option, because the certificate changes. The shared CI debug keystore is treated as compromised and retired, and its fingerprints are removed from the production Firebase app (SEC-REQ-39).
  • Key custody: the Release Manager (primary) and the Product Owner (secondary) hold the app signing key in a password-manager vault with two encrypted offline copies; restore tested yearly †; removed from CI after Play onboarding.
  • Keys are RSA ≥ 2048 bits (4096 recommended) with validity of at least 30 years. The certificate SHA-256 is published in the README and every release note so side-loading testers can verify it.

Versions follow SemVer with an optional -beta.N; the version code is derived deterministically (MAJOR × 1,000,000 + MINOR × 10,000 + PATCH × 100 + beta number, or 99 for a final release), so a final release always sorts above its betas. Release mechanics are in Part IV.

Chapter 8Security and privacy

The product promise is "private by design", and the current app breaks it in several places. This chapter summarises how the target design classifies data, which threats it addresses, the key security requirements, authentication and rules, backup, deletion, logging and the user's data rights. SEC-01 is the authoritative home of the 40 security requirements (SEC-REQ-01…40), 22 threats (THR-01…22) and 50 security tests; none of those tests has been run.

8.1 Where security stands today

Finding (EXISTING)SeveritySummary
FND-SEC-02CriticalEvery push to the main branches builds a beta; without the release keystore secret it produces a debuggable, debug-signed APK that installs the App Check debug provider, and the launcher accepts a debug secret from an intent extra
FND-SEC-03HighAccount deletion exists in code but has no UI caller and no re-authentication; partial deletion is possible; there is no export
FND-SEC-05HighTranscripts are written to Firestore while Settings says they stay on the device
FND-SEC-06HighBackup is allowed with template rules, so recordings and learning files can go to cloud backup and device transfer
FND-SEC-07HighWithout an on-device recogniser the app silently falls back to the network recogniser
FND-OPS-01 / -04HighCI runs no tests or rules checks; a self-updater with a user-editable source repository
FND-SEC-08 · -09 · -10 · -11Mediumuid and email in Logcat; sign-out clears nothing and recordings are unpartitioned; rule validation gaps and client-writable scores; R8 disabled

Priority containment (RECOMMENDATION — the first Stage B changes, each needing approval): stop push-triggered distribution; freeze admin-writable shared collections in the deployed rules; remove the App Check debug-token intent injection from any distributed build; remove PII from Firestore error logging.

8.3 Threat model summary

SEC-01 applies STRIDE to nine assets (recordings, transcripts, learning data, account identity, signing keys and release channel, the Firebase project, CI secrets, user trust in privacy claims, and no-account profile data). The highest current exposures and their planned residual risk:

ThreatScenarioMain mitigationsResidual
THR-04Malicious APK through the update channel (user-editable repo, debug-key holder)Updater removed; release-signed only; published certificate fingerprints; debug certificate retired; Play App SigningLow (Medium–Low during the GitHub beta)
THR-09Cloud backup or device transfer leaks recordings, transcripts or tokensRecordings in no-backup storage; include-only rules for backup and transfer; sensitive.db excluded; backup testsLow
THR-12Privacy misrepresentation: UI, policy or data-safety claims contradict behaviourCloud-AI SDK removed with a classpath check; no transcripts or paths in cloud documents; consent gating; one privacy-facts source with a testLow
THR-13Audio streamed to a network recogniser without the user knowingOn-device API for T1/T2; consent-gated recogniser factoryLow
THR-02Shared or second-hand phone: the next user sees or merges someone's historySign-out "Remove" default when synced; profile partitioning; merge confirmationLow
THR-06Quota or cost abuse with a valid accountSize and id limits; no listing of users; bounded queries; Google-only provider; API-key restrictions; budget alerts; App Check after PlayMedium until App Check is enforced
THR-10Supply-chain compromise (action tags, dependencies, wrapper, npm)SHA-pinned actions, wrapper validation, locked dependencies, minimal permissionsMedium–Low
THR-01 · THR-15Device theft or forensic extraction · incomplete account deletionApp-private storage, device encryption, 30-day retention · resumable verified cascade, marker, web deletion routeLow–Medium (accepted)

SEC-01 §12

8.5 Authorization and Firestore rules

The single principal is request.auth.uid. The app builds paths only from the signed-in user's uid, never from input or intent data. Rules deny by default, allow only the owner to reach users/{uid}/…, validate every write with required and allowed key lists, types, enums, ranges and sizes, and treat attempts and events as create-only. There are no trusted aggregates, no shared collections and no admin role, so a user who forges facts affects only their own data and there are no leaderboards to corrupt.

Pathget / listcreateupdatedelete
users/{uid}owner / deniedowner, schema v1owner, mutable keys onlyowner
…/attempts/{attemptId}ownerowner; id = attemptIddeniedowner
…/plans/{dateKey}ownerowner; ≤ 4 steps validatedownerowner
…/words/{wordId}ownerowner; review-schedule rangesownerowner
…/events/{eventId}ownerowner; typed payloaddeniedowner
…/meta/syncownerownerownerowner
Legacy user subcollectionsowner (L1 window)denieddeniedowner (L1 window)
Everything elsedenied — shared, admin and release collections are removed at L1/L2
Rules must be regenerated The illustrative rules in SEC-01 §5.3 drift from the authoritative schema in ARC-03 §8 (field names, word status values, stage and goal ranges, word-task templates, feedback codes). Both ARC-02 and ARC-04 require the rules and their emulator test suite to be regenerated from ARC-03 §8 before release phase L1.

SEC-01 §5.1–§5.6; ARC-04 §9.2 (II-02); ARC-02 §12.2 (DI-06…DI-10)

8.6 Backup policy (D-23)

The recommendation is include-only backup: Android cloud backup and device-to-device transfer carry only learning.db (learning progress and synced preferences) and the settings DataStore. People without an account therefore keep their progress when they change phones, while sensitive voice data never leaves the device. Disabling backup altogether was rejected because no-account users would lose everything on a phone change — and device transfer still happens for apps targeting API 31+ unless excluded, so extraction rules are needed either way.

<!-- res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml (Android 12+) — PROPOSED; backup_rules.xml mirrors it for Android 11 and lower -->
<data-extraction-rules>
  <cloud-backup>
    <include domain="database" path="learning.db"/>
    <include domain="file" path="datastore/settings.preferences_pb"/>
  </cloud-backup>
  <device-transfer>
    <include domain="database" path="learning.db"/>
    <include domain="file" path="datastore/settings.preferences_pb"/>
  </device-transfer>
</data-extraction-rules>

Explicitly not included (verified absent by TC-SEC-012/013, Not run): sensitive.db, the no-backup folder (recordings, diagnostics), device_state, Firebase SDK stores, the Firestore cache, the WorkManager database and legacy paths. A restore is detected when learning.db exists but device_state has no device id: the app generates a new id, resets the watermark and asks the user to sign in to resume backup. No app-level database encryption is proposed for R1 (SQLCipher would be a new dependency); the residual risk is rated Low–Medium and accepted, with stated triggers to revisit.

SEC-01 §6.2–§6.6; D-23

8.7 Deletion

Account deletion is a client-side cascade with re-authentication, verification and resumable state — there is no server-side function to do it. Each phase is persisted in ENT-18 before its side effects count as done, and every step is idempotent, so a resume repeats at most the current phase.

PhaseAction
CONFIRMED → REAUTHENTICATEDExplicit confirmation online; Google account chooser; the uid must match, otherwise nothing is deleted
MARKER_WRITTEN → SYNC_STOPPEDA server-timestamped deletion marker stops other devices from uploading and offers them "Finish deleting account"; the sync worker is cancelled and the outbox cleared
CLOUD_SUBCOLLECTIONS → VERIFIEDAttempts, events, plans, words and legacy subcollections deleted in pages of 400 until empty; then each collection is confirmed empty with a server read
CLOUD_PROFILE → AUTH_DELETEDMeta and profile documents deleted; then the Auth user (re-authenticating again if the platform demands a recent login)
LOCAL_WIPED → DONEFull local wipe including Firestore persistence and credential state; the app returns to first run

If the process dies, the next launch shows a blocking "Finishing account deletion" screen and resumes; offline, it waits for a connection. Other data-rights actions follow a wipe matrix: Clear history removes attempts, metrics, plans, session events, transcripts and recordings locally and in the cloud (marker first); Reset vocabulary removes word progress, reviews and word events; Delete local data wipes everything on a phone without an account.

SEC-01 §6.4, §9.2–§9.4; ARC-02 §6.5

8.8 Logging and diagnostics

  • All logging goes through AppLogger; a CI check fails on any direct android.util.Log or println. Release builds drop verbose, debug and info levels and redact warnings and errors.
  • Never logged: transcripts and partials, audio, email, display name, uid, any token, Firestore paths containing a uid, file paths containing a profile id, consent details. A redaction filter replaces emails, uid-like tokens, JWTs, users/… paths and UUIDs, and strips URL query strings.
  • Events are enumerated codes with non-personal parameters, for example SYNC_UPLOAD_FAILED{collection=attempts, code=PERMISSION_DENIED, attempt=3}.
  • A rolling, PII-free diagnostic log (2 × 256 KB, 7 days †) stays in no-backup storage and is shared only from Settings → About → "Share diagnostics" after a preview. Crash reporting relies on this log and Play vitals; Crashlytics would need separate, opt-in approval (D-13).

SEC-01 §10; ADR-007

8.9 Privacy controls and data rights

Right or controlHow the design provides it
Use without an accountLocal profile; no network in no-account mode
Informed consent for online transcriptionT3a consent screen states what is sent, to whom and under whose policy; revocable with immediate effect; stored locally with a disclosure version
Recording retentionDefault 30 days (D-10 †); user options; daily enforcement and an app-start sweep
Access and portabilityJSON export of all personal data categories, with an optional recordings ZIP (D-22)
ErasureClear history, reset vocabulary, delete local data, delete account (in-app) and a web deletion request
Truthful disclosuresIn-app privacy text, the privacy policy and the Play data-safety inputs derive from one reviewed privacy-facts source; a test and a release sign-off check consistency

The privacy policy must cover, among other things: that the account is optional; the processors (Google Firebase, Google Play, and the device speech service under its own policy for T3a); what is not collected (no audio uploads, no transcripts in the cloud in R1, no analytics, no ads, no location, no contacts); on-device retention and backup behaviour; and that opening a source-video link leaves SpeakCraft. For the Play data-safety form, D-25 Pending recommends declaring T3a audio conservatively as "shared, optional", subject to policy review. Any new data flow, SDK, permission or default change goes through the SEC-01 §9.7 privacy review before approval.

SEC-01 §7.3, §9.1–§9.7; ARC-04 §3.9; D-10, D-22, D-25

Part II

UI/UX design

Who the app is for and how they move through it: the personas and journeys, the information architecture and its 42 screens, the "Calm Indigo" design system, and the usability and accessibility rules every screen must meet.

Part II is a RECOMMENDATION awaiting Product Owner approval at gate G2 (decisions D-18, D-33, D-34). No screen has been built and no usability or accessibility test has been run.

Chapter 9User journeys

The redesign starts from the people SpeakCraft serves and the moments that matter to them: the first three minutes, the daily ten-minute session, and seeing honest progress over weeks. This chapter summarises the personas, what their journey looks like today, the fourteen target flows, and the targets for first use and daily practice.

Personas are hypotheses No user research, interviews, surveys, analytics or support data exist for SpeakCraft. The personas are evidence-informed hypotheses built from Product Owner requests, the existing content and code, and competitor research. All attributes are assumptions to validate in the R1 beta (ASM-01…06), and time estimates in this chapter are expert estimates, not measurements.

9.1 Personas

PersonaSegment and levelGoalsPain points and barriersSuccess looks like
Aarav — software engineer, 28 · primaryWorking professional · B2Concise stand-up updates; explain technical issues clearly; stop rambling; sound confident with clientsFreezes on unexpected questions; fillers ("basically", "actually"); no honest feedback; little time; embarrassed to practise aloud; distrusts apps that "grade accent"Fewer fillers per minute; answers within the time target; a clear point → reason → example structure
Meera — returning to work, 31 · primaryJob seeker · B1+Answer "Tell me about yourself" and behavioural questions with confidence; stronger vocabularyAnxiety; answers too short; struggles to recall words; does not want recordings uploaded; irregular scheduleComplete STAR answers; length within target; learned words used in answers
Daniel — final-year student, 21 · secondaryStudent / graduate · B1Introduce himself well; present a project in two minutes; steady paceSpeaks too fast when nervous; limited vocabulary; budget-sensitive; patchy connectivity; older mid-range phonePace within the comfortable band; fewer long pauses
Sofia — senior analyst, 38 · servedAdvanced professional · C1Polish delivery: fewer hedges, deliberate pauses, crisp structureGeneric apps are too basic; wants precise, honest metricsMeasurable reduction in hedges and fillers over four weeks
Hoang — shop supervisor, 34 · served with guided templatesElementary everyday speaker · A2+Say common phrases clearly; learn practical wordsFree speaking is hard; recognition may fail on accented speech"Recognised as intended" rising on everyday phrases

Not designed for: absolute beginners who need a full curriculum, exam candidates wanting a band prediction, people looking for an AI chat companion, and content administrators (non-goals NG-01, -02, -04, -05, -10). The primary segment is decision D-01 Pending.

RD-08 §1–§6; D-01 (RD-99 §8)

9.2 The journey today and the fixes that matter most

RD-09 reconstructs Meera's current journey from the code. It falls at the first hurdles — a mandatory Google sign-in before any value, a three-step wizard with seven self-rating sliders — and then frustrates at the dashboard (about 21 widgets, a fake score of 62, "1 Day Streak" on day 0), in feedback (no transcript by default, "pronunciation" computed from loudness) and in the absence of reminders.

Journey stageCurrent implementation (EXISTING)Opportunity (target)
Open2.9 s non-skippable overlay; colour jump from splash to appBrand moment ≤ 1 s †
RegistrationGoogle sign-in mandatory; raw error text on failureAccount optional (D-05)
OnboardingLong wizard; self-ratings later shown as "objective" skills; no speakingShort choices with defaults, then a first attempt inside onboarding
Dashboard and daily practice~21 widgets; three competing daily plans; ticking a box counts as doneToday with one session card and one Start; completion only from valid attempts
Speaking and evaluationOne of ten recorders with twelve labels; no transcript by default; activity score = a length formulaOne session player; tiered transcription; labelled metric catalogue

Vocabulary20-word bank; "words learned" means words seenFive-stage lifecycle with spaced review, ≥ 300 words †
Re-engagementNo operating-system notificationsOne daily reminder at the chosen time, skipped if already practised

The target emotional curve rises at onboarding — the first attempt already returns a real transcript and one useful tip — and stays positive through short, finishable sessions and visible evidence of progress. The six priority fixes map to flows: real transcript and honest feedback (FLOW-05); first attempt inside onboarding with an optional account (FLOW-01); one Today session (FLOW-02/03); evidence-only progress (FLOW-08); reminders (FLOW-11); and the vocabulary lifecycle (FLOW-06/07).

DES-04 §2

9.4 First use: time to the first valid attempt (KPI-02)

The target is a median of ≤ 3 minutes † from first opening the app to the first valid attempt (KPI-02). In FLOW-01 the user taps Get started, chooses goal, level and daily time (each pre-selected, so one tap each), decides about a reminder, reads a plain privacy statement and allows the microphone, sees a speech check, and records "Introduce yourself" inside onboarding. Feedback appears automatically after Stop.

MomentTaps (cumulative)Time (cumulative, INFERRED)
App open → Welcome screen interactive00:02
Get started; goal, level, daily time; reminder "Not now"50:45
Privacy read, Allow microphone, system Allow71:04
Speech check, Continue; first attempt intro, Start91:17
Read prompt, Record101:22–1:37
Speak 40–60 s, Stop; finalisation and evaluation → first valid attempt shown112:06–2:41
Read feedback (including mistakes found and coach tips), Go to Today122:36–3:11
Path variantTime to first valid attemptAgainst KPI-02 (≤ 3:00 †)
Baseline (T1/T2, reminder declined)2:06–2:41Meets on paper, margin 19–54 s
With recommendation R-1 (skip the speech-check stop when no choice is needed)about 7 s faster, 9 taps to recordingMeets on paper
T3a consent read+20–30 sNarrow margin
Hoang (A2+, slower reading)2:36–3:41At risk
First attempt Inconclusive, one retry+60–80 sFails — KPI-02 counts the first valid attempt

Mitigations: keep onboarding copy at CEFR B1 and short; show a one-line recording tip ("Speak in a quiet place, about 20 cm from the phone"); make the Inconclusive reason specific so the retry succeeds first time. The test cases that will measure this (TC-ONB-018) are Not run.

DES-04 §1.3, FLOW-01 step table and variants; BRD KPI-02 (via DES-04)

9.5 Daily practice: two taps to recording (UX-2)

For a returning user the target is ≤ 2 taps from app open to recording (UX-2). The app opens on Today with one session card: tap 1 is Start session (or Continue session or Practise more), tap 2 is Record. The first step of a fresh day is a warm-up with no preparation time. Estimated time from opening to capture running is 8–11 s on RD-MID and ≤ 13 s on RD-LOW (INFERRED).

AspectCurrent (EXISTING, counted from code)Proposed
Open2.1 s overlay + profile load; possible blocking update dialog≤ 1 s brand moment; Today interactive ≤ 2 s †
Which planThree competing plansOne session card
Taps to recording2 from the dashboard to a free recorder — not into the daily plan2 into today's session
Taps per speaking step≈ 6 (open, Record, Stop, Analyze, Back, tick)3 (Record, Stop, Continue) — 2 if the time limit stops it
Whole routine≈ 40 taps for 8 self-ticked steps≈ 11–14 taps for a 10-minute session
Completion and streakSelf-reported ticks; streak seeded at 1; two different streak numbersOnly valid attempts or finished review sets; one streak

Reading the comparison honestly. In first use the tap count barely changes (9 today → 10 to first recording, or 9 with R-1). The gains are elsewhere: no account wall or network dependency, no tour or form-filling, a permission explanation with a recovery path, automatic feedback, and real content in that feedback.

Open flow issues

  • F-3 UX-2 needs 3 taps when the next incomplete step is a word "Learn" stage; FS-03 should open the next speaking step first, or UX-2 should exclude non-speaking steps.
  • F-5 / D-32 On T3b phones word stages 2–4 cannot be checked; D-32 recommends "Needs a transcript" with ways to enable recognition.
  • F-6 In T3a the recogniser owns the microphone, so a mid-attempt network loss cannot switch to audio-only; DES-04 checks connectivity before recording.
  • F-7 Whether a session with skipped steps counts as complete (and for KPI-04) is undecided; linked to D-34.
  • F-19 Every result can now carry up to 3 metrics, 3 mistakes and 3 tips, which adds reading time the 10-minute session does not yet budget.

DES-04 FLOW-02, §4.1–§4.2, §5; RD-15 UX-2

Chapter 10Information architecture and screens

Twenty-six routes, five tabs (one named after a third-party creator) and a 22-tile home directory become four purpose-led destinations with a maximum depth of three. This chapter shows the navigation model, lists all 42 screens, and maps every retired route to its new home.

10.1 Navigation model

SlotDestinationThe question it answersRoot screen
1Today"What should I practise now?"SCR-TODAY-01
2Practice"I want to rehearse a specific situation."SCR-PRAC-01
3Words"Which words am I learning and what's due?"SCR-WORD-01
4Progress"Am I improving?"SCR-PROG-01
  • A bottom navigation bar on compact widths (< 600 dp); a navigation rail on medium (600–839 dp) and expanded (≥ 840 dp) widths, with list-detail layouts in Practice, Words and Progress. The same four destinations appear at every width.
  • Profile and Settings sit behind an avatar in the top bar of every primary destination, not in a tab. The session player, onboarding, sign-in and permission education are full-screen tasks that hide the bar and always offer a clear exit.
  • Principles: maximum depth 3 from a primary destination (for example Practice → Track → Activity → Session player); predictable Back (one level; from a tab root to Today; from Today out of the app); no dead ends — every empty, error or offline state offers an action; one name per concept.

DES-02 §5–§6; DES-03 §7

Chapter 11Design system

"Calm Indigo" replaces today's saturated violet theme, pastel tiles and gradients with one accent colour, neutral surfaces, real font weights and short, purposeful motion. It is built on Material 3 in Jetpack Compose with no new libraries — fonts and icons are asset changes only. Its components make honesty structural: a number cannot be shown without an evidence label.

Status DES-05 v0.2 is a RECOMMENDATION. It becomes authoritative for visual tokens and components once the Product Owner approves it; the palette itself is decision D-18 Pending (gate G2).

11.1 Principles

IDPrincipleWhat it means for tokens and components
DS-1Calm by defaultOne accent colour, neutral surfaces, generous spacing, no gradients or pastel decoration; short, purposeful motion
DS-2The task is the heroOne primary (filled) action per task screen; decoration never competes with the prompt, the Record button or the feedback
DS-3Honest by constructionComponents cannot display a number without an evidence label; empty and Inconclusive states are first-class components
DS-4Readable for B1–B2 speakersPlain words, comfortable sizes, sentence case, no monospace or code-like text
DS-5Accessible without exceptionsContrast, 200 % text, 48 dp targets, colour-independent states and reduced motion built into components
DS-6One component per patternOne Record button, one feedback card, one metric tile, one empty-state layout; variants come from parameters
DS-7Material 3 firstUse M3 components and roles as designed; customise through tokens, not forks
DS-8Works anywhereEvery component has offline, loading and error variants; nothing depends on network imagery

Tokens come in three tiers: reference tokens (raw values such as Indigo40 = #4054A6), system tokens (roles such as colorScheme.primary, typography.titleMedium, Spacing.lg, Motion.standard) and component usage. UI code may use system tokens only; reference tokens are internal to the design-system package, and a grep-based CI guardrail flags hard-coded colours.

DES-05 §1–§2, §13.4

11.2 Colour

Option (D-18)DescriptionAssessment
A — Calm Indigo (recommended)Desaturated indigo-blue accent on cool, lightly tinted neutral greys; muted plum tertiary used sparingly; standard semantic huesContinuity with the current indigo/violet identity, far calmer; distinct from success, warning, streak and error hues; every pair passes WCAG AA by computation
B — Deep TealTeal accentAdjacent to success green; would need re-tuning; not computed
C — Keep "Violet Studio" and fix contrastMinimum changeContradicts "calm and premium"; its 13 failing light-theme pairs must be fixed anyway
  • Contrast (computed, not yet measured on a device). Ratios were computed by script from the hex values: every text pair is at least 5.34:1 (the dark snackbar action is the lowest) and most main text pairs exceed 7:1; every boundary pair is at least 4.07:1. The current palette, by comparison, has 13 light-theme failures, including an outline at 1.24:1.
  • One accent. primary marks the single primary action, selection and focus — never decoration or large fills. Large areas use surface and container roles only; no gradients, pastel tiles or image scrims.
  • Semantic colours mean one thing. Success means completed, error means error or destructive, recording means the microphone is capturing, streak means streak. Metric judgements ("within target", "faster than target") use neutral text, never green or red, so speakers are not graded by colour.
  • Colour is never the only carrier of meaning; every coloured state also has text or an icon. Dark theme uses #121318, not pure black.
  • No dynamic colour (Material You). Measured, Estimated, Inconclusive, success, warning, streak and recording are product signals that must look the same on every device; a wallpaper-derived primary could land on green or orange; contrast can only be assured for a fixed palette; and dynamic colour needs API 31+.

DES-05 §3.1–§3.6; RDD-20, RDD-21

11.3 Typography

Today the app ships three single-weight fonts (Plus Jakarta Sans, DM Sans, JetBrains Mono) and lets the platform synthesise bold weights; one style uses a monospace font in 103 places. The recommendation is Inter (SIL Open Font License) for every role, in three real static weights — 400, 500 and 600 — subset to Latin and Latin Extended, with no weight requested that is not bundled and no monospace anywhere in UI labels. Static files are used because variable-font axes need API 26+; if D-14 raises minSdk, one variable file could replace them.

RoleSize / line height (sp)WeightTypical use
displaySmall36 / 44500Key metric value on metric detail and session complete (tabular figures)
headlineLarge30 / 38600Onboarding question titles
headlineMedium26 / 34600Result headline "Here's how it went"; session complete title
titleLarge20 / 28600Top app bar and bottom sheet titles
titleMedium16 / 24600Card titles; "What went well", "Mistakes found", "Try this next"
bodyLarge16 / 24400Default reading text; transcripts; feedback sentences
bodyMedium14 / 20400Supporting text; evidence lines
labelLarge / labelMedium14 / 20 · 12 / 16500Buttons · chips, badges, navigation labels
promptText (extended)20 / 30400Read-aloud passages, questions, sentences to repeat

Scaling rules: all text in sp; layouts work at a 200 % system font scale with the largest display size; no fixed-height containers hold text; essential text (prompts, feedback, errors, button labels, metric values) is never truncated; side-by-side buttons stack when they do not fit; navigation labels are single words of at most 8 characters. Sentence case everywhere, start-aligned, reading width ≤ 640 dp, emphasis by weight rather than colour, tabular figures for metrics and timers.

DES-05 §4.1–§4.5

Depth comes from container colour roles, not shadows: surface for screens, a low container for cards and sheets, a mid container for navigation and sticky action areas, a high container for dialogs. There are no elevated cards, no custom shadows and no floating action button in R1. Icons follow the Material Symbols outlined style (filled when a tab is selected), drawn from the icon sets already in the build; icon-only buttons are named by their action, not their icon.

DES-05 §5–§7

11.5 Motion

  • Material 3 duration tokens (50–500 ms) and easing curves; in-app motion is at most 400 ms, except the launch brand moment (≤ 1,000 ms, using the platform splash) and a milestone celebration (≤ 800 ms, once).
  • No looping animation except the live recording level meter (driven by real input, at most 30 frames per second) and indeterminate progress. No parallax, bounce or overshoot; nothing flashes more than three times a second; motion never blocks input and is never used to fake progress. Skeleton placeholders are static — no shimmer.
  • Reduced motion follows an in-app preference (Follow system · On · Off). "Follow system" observes the system "Remove animations" setting reactively. With reduced motion on, screen and tab transitions, session state changes and expand/collapse become instant, the level meter becomes a single bar updated at most four times a second, and celebrations become a static icon and text.

DES-05 §8; A11Y-21, A11Y-22

11.6 Key components

Buttons and the Record control

Exactly one filled button marks the primary action on a task screen ("Start session", "Try again", "Continue"); tonal, outlined and text buttons carry lesser actions. Buttons are at least 48 dp tall (56 dp for a bottom call to action), grow with text, and show a spinner in place of the label while loading so they cannot be pressed twice. Labels are verb-first, sentence case, ideally three words or fewer, never "OK" as a primary action and never "AI".

Record stateVisualLabel · statusAccessibility
Ready80 dp circle in primary, microphone icon"Record" · "Tap to start when you're ready""Start recording"
RecordingRecording colour, stop icon, solid dot beside the timer "0:23 / 1:30""Stop" · "Recording""Stop recording"; state "Recording, 23 seconds" on focus, not announced continuously
Near limitAs Recording"10 seconds left"Announced once
ProcessingIndeterminate progress"Checking your recording…"Polite live region
Microphone unavailable · InterruptedDisabled button with microphone-off icon · interrupted state"The microphone is being used by another app" · "Recording stopped because of a call"Announced

Single tap to start, single tap to stop — never press-and-hold — with taps debounced for 500 ms and keyboard Enter or Space toggling when focused. The level meter shows real input only; if input stays near zero for 3 s the status reads "We can't hear anything. Check your microphone."

Metric tile and evidence badge

A metric tile shows a label ("Pace"), a value and unit ("128 words/min"), a required evidence badge, an optional target in neutral words ("Target 120–160 · Within target"), an optional delta against an attempt at the same prompt or template ("12 fewer than last time"), and an info button that opens "How we measured this". At most three tiles appear on a result; the rest sit under "All measurements". Fallback values, values without a badge and composite "overall" scores for free speaking are prohibited.

BadgeVisualText (mandatory)
MeasuredFilled indigo container, ruler icon"Measured"
EstimatedTransparent with a 1 dp outline, no icon"Estimated"
InconclusiveNeutral grey container, help icon"Inconclusive"

The result: order and components

One feedback structure serves every template. Its order is authoritative and identical in FS-05, DES-05 §11.15 and DES-06 §3.3 (revised 2026-11-21 for the mistakes-and-tips direction):

OrderSectionContent
1Header"Here's how it went" plus the tier line ("On-device transcription", "Audio-only feedback"…). For an evidence failure the Inconclusive card replaces sections 3–6
2What we heardTranscript, first three lines then "Show full transcript"; play control for the local recording; plain statement if recognition was unavailable
3MetricsUp to three metric tiles for the template. Read aloud and repeat show "Recognised as intended: 82 % (pass mark 75 %)"; a result below the mark reads "Not yet. Try again." in neutral styling — never a red "Fail"
4What went wellOne sentence (the strength)
5Mistakes foundMistakeList
6Coach tipsCoachTipCard stack: the focus tip first, titled "Try this next", then up to two more (one tip for word results)
(6a)Self-checkSelfCheckList, only when the technique has aspects audio cannot show
7ActionsOne filled button: "Continue" inside a Today session, "Try again" in Practice and Words
8Was this accurate?"Yes" / "No" with optional reasons, including "A mistake was wrong"

MistakeList

Title "Mistakes found" with the count, as a heading. Each row: a neutral info icon (never error red), a plain statement, an evidence line (quoted span, count or value with unit) and its evidence badge. Measured items are stated plainly ("You used 'um' 7 times"); Estimated items start "Possible …". Never "Wrong", "Error" or "Fail". Three rows first, then "Show all (n)". Empty state: "Our checks didn't find issues in this attempt." with a note on what the checks cover — no "perfect".

CoachTipCard

Low container, lightbulb icon, title "Try this next" for the focus tip or the technique's plain name for the others. One or two sentences in our own words. Links: "See the technique" (SCR-PRAC-04) and, only when provenance exists, "Watch the original lesson", which opens the specific video outside the app ("Needs an internet connection" when offline). Never labelled "AI"; no sparkle icons; selection is deterministic.

SelfCheckList

Title "Check yourself" with the line "We can't see this from audio — your answers are just for you." One to four checkbox rows (whole row ≥ 48 dp), for example "I looked at the camera or my notes for most of the answer". Stored and shown as self-reported; never given a Measured or Estimated badge; never used in trends.

Inconclusive card. Neutral container, help icon, title "We couldn't measure this attempt", a specific reason mapped from the evidence gate ("It was too quiet to hear clearly", "No words were recognised", "Recording stopped because of a call"), the reassurance "This attempt doesn't count against you", a filled "Try again" and "Tips for a clear recording". It never shows partial metrics as if complete, and uses no red and no "fail".

Other components. A streak week strip and month grid (practised days filled with a check icon; missed days plain — no red, no cross; a new user sees "Start your streak today", never a seeded 1). Charts drawn with Compose Canvas (no library) plot only stored values, leave gaps for missing days, label target bands, draw Estimated series dashed and labelled, and offer a "View as list" alternative. State patterns for empty, loading (static skeleton after 300 ms), error (keeps user input, no exception text), offline (a non-blocking notice only where a feature needs the network), microphone permission and "capability unavailable".

DES-05 §11.1–§11.22; DES-06 §3.3.5; RDD-06, RDD-07

11.7 Terminology, voice and banned terms

Voice traitMeansAvoid
CalmEven, unhurried statementsExclamation marks (at most one, milestones only), hype, urgency

RespectfulTreat users as capable adults; never blame"Oops", "You failed", "Wrong"

Today's session · Step · AttemptThe daily plan of 3–4 steps · one item in it · one recording and its result ("Session" is reserved for the daily plan)
Track · Activity · TechniqueA themed collection · one practice item based on a template · a named way of speaking better, with steps, tips and exercises — plain original names only
Measured · Estimated · InconclusiveExact badge texts; Inconclusive is never "failed"

BannedWhyUse instead
"AI", "AI Coach", "AI Mentor", "AI Check", "Smart …"Misrepresents deterministic rule code"Feedback", or describe the feature
"Studio", "Engine", "Lab", "Hub", "Console", "Intelligence"Internal or marketing jargonCanonical terms

"Pronunciation score", "accent score"; "overall score", "fluency score", "x/100" for free speakingNot measured (NG-03); no composite (D-06)"Recognised as intended" (read and repeat only); individual labelled metrics
"Verified", "Objective", "Accurate" for app output; "Mastered"; "Fail", "Wrong", "Oops"Unprovable claims; inflated progress; blame or guiltMeasured / Estimated; "Long-term review"; "Not yet. Try again."

All strings live in resources with proper plurals; numbers, dates and the first day of the week follow the device locale; errors follow the formula "what happened + what to do"; metric explanations follow "what it is · how we measure it · why it matters · its limits".

DES-05 §12.1–§12.5; DES-03 I-8 (CL-62)

Chapter 12Usability and accessibility

A speaking coach must be easy to start, easy to understand and usable by everyone — including people who use a screen reader, large text, a switch or a keyboard. This chapter sets out the experience principles, the usability targets, the 38 accessibility rules grouped by area, the speech-specific questions still open, and how all of this will be tested. No usability or accessibility test has been run, and no research with disabled users exists yet.

12.1 Experience principles

IDPrincipleWhat it means in practiceVerified by (planned)
UX-1Simple to understandPlain words, no internal jargon, one name per concept everywhereDES-05 terminology list; content review
UX-2Easy to start≤ 2 taps from app open to recording in a returning sessionFLOW-02; UI test TC-UX-002
UX-3Feedback users can trustTranscript visible; metrics labelled; caveats one tap awayFS-05 acceptance tests
UX-4Visible progressToday shows today's progress; Progress shows trends from evidenceFS-08
UX-5PersonalSessions reflect goal, level and recent results; the user can always change themFS-02, FS-03
UX-6Minimal cognitive loadOne primary action per screen; progressive disclosure for detailDES-01 heuristic checklist
UX-7Offline-firstEvery core screen has a defined offline state that still worksDES-03 state matrix
UX-8Consistent navigationFour persistent destinations; predictable Back; no dead endsDES-02; navigation tests
UX-9AccessibleWCAG 2.2 AA-equivalent on Android: TalkBack, 200 % font scale, contrast, 48 dp targets, reduced motionDES-07; NFR-A11Y
UX-10Calm and premiumRestrained colour, generous spacing, purposeful motion; celebration reserved for real milestonesDES-05

The audit of the current app (DES-01) found 61 UX problems — 10 critical, 29 high, 20 medium and 2 low — across unnecessary steps, confusing navigation, duplicate features, inconsistent labels, overloaded screens, unclear progress and feedback, broken interactions, accessibility, missing states, developer jargon and fabricated displays. Its heuristic checklist will be used to review every new design.

RD-15 §3; DES-01 §3.14, §5

12.2 Usability targets

Target †MeasureSourceStatus
First valid attempt within 3 minutesMedian time from first open to the first valid attemptKPI-02; FLOW-01Estimated 2:06–2:41 on paper; at risk for slower readers; Not run
Two taps to recordingTaps from app open to capture in a returning sessionUX-2; FLOW-02Meets on paper except when the next step is a word "Learn" stage; Not run
Fast, steady appCold start ≤ 2.0 s (RD-MID); tab switch ≤ 300 ms to first frame, jank ≤ 5 % framesNFR-PERF-001, -003Not measured
Short daily routine≈ 11–14 taps for a 10-minute session; 3 taps per speaking stepDES-04 §4.2Estimate
