A small, learnable app≤ 30 screens, counted as distinct layouts (38 R1 IDs in 16 layout families)KPI-11; DES-03 §3Counting rule to confirm (I-2)
Feedback people trust"Was this accurate?" ratings collected locally and shared by testers through the beta summaryADR-013; BRD §8Beta measure

Guidance without tours

Four overlapping tutorial systems are replaced by self-explanatory screens and at most one contextual first-use tip per screen (six tips in total for R1, replacing 24 tutorial entries). Tips are never blocking — no scrim, spotlight, dialog or focus trap — are dismissed with "Got it" or a close button, are stored per profile, never reappear unless the user chooses "Show tips again" in About, never appear at launch, in onboarding or while recording, and are at most 20 words of B1 English.

TipScreenTriggerText
TIP-01TodayFirst visit after onboarding"Your session changes every day, based on your goal and recent results."
TIP-02Session resultFirst valid result ever"Measured means we counted it from your recording. Estimated is our best guess. Tap (i) to see how."
TIP-03PracticeFirst visit"Everything you practise here counts towards your streak and progress."
TIP-04WordsFirst visit"Each word has five stages. Saying it aloud moves it forward."
TIP-05ProgressFirst visit with a valid attempt"Trends appear after three valid attempts of the same kind."
TIP-06Attempt detailFirst opened"You can still tell us whether this feedback was accurate."

DES-04 §1.3, §4; DES-06 §2.4; NFR §2, §7; DES-03 §3, I-2

12.3 Accessibility target

A11Y-01 sets the target: WCAG 2.2 Level A and AA applied to a native Android app, interpreted as W3C's guidance for non-web software describes, plus Android-specific requirements (TalkBack, Switch Access, 200 % font scale, 48 dp targets). Two AAA-level behaviours are adopted because they matter here: animation from interactions can be removed, and targets are 48 dp. A release may say it "meets DES-07" only after the release test pack has been executed on the reference devices with captured evidence and every deviation approved at gate G6; until then the status is "target", not "conformant". This is not a legal conformance claim.

PrincipleIn practice
AP-1 Built into componentsDesign-system components ship with correct names, roles, states, targets and reduced-motion variants; screens add only titles, order and focus moves
AP-3 The same honest information for everyoneWhatever a sighted user learns — Measured, Estimated, Inconclusive, target status, recording state, tier — a TalkBack, low-vision or colour-blind user learns too, in words
AP-4 Speaking is the task, but nobody is trapped by itRecording works without precise touch, sight or colour; text alternatives exist for all audio; the rule for users who cannot speak is an explicit decision
AP-5 Calm announcementsAnnounce what the user needs, once, politely; never narrate timers or meters
AP-7 No unverified claimsA check is "passed" only with captured evidence

DES-07 §1.2–§2

12.4 Accessibility rules by area

AreaRulesSummary
TalkBack and semanticsA11Y-02…12Every interactive or informative element has a plain name stating its purpose (the action for icon buttons); decoration and the level meter are silent; screen and section titles are headings; correct roles and action labels; every visible state is exposed (selected, expanded, loading, "Step 2 of 4", "Recording, 23 seconds"); cards, tiles and rows read as one phrase ("Pace, 128 words per minute, measured. Target 120 to 160, within target."); live regions for recording start and stop, processing, results, Inconclusive and interruptions — polite by default, never on timers; focus moves to "Here's how it went" when a result appears; modal content only in real dialogs and sheets with a Close button; focus order follows reading order; charts have a text summary and a "View as list" alternative
Font scaling and reflowA11Y-13…14All text scales to 200 % with no fixed-height text containers; essential text is never truncated; buttons stack and tiles reflow; everything is reachable by vertical scrolling; portrait and landscape both supported
ContrastA11Y-15…17Text ≥ 4.5:1 at every size (stricter than WCAG's large-text allowance); boundaries, focus indicators, chart marks and meaningful icons ≥ 3:1; system tokens only, both themes tested, no dynamic colour, no text over images
Touch targets and gesturesA11Y-18…20One target per action and no nested targets; every interactive element ≥ 48 × 48 dp (the Record button is 80 dp); every swipe or drag has a single-tap alternative; no long-press-only actions and no press-and-hold recording
MotionA11Y-21…22Reduced motion follows the system setting reactively and an in-app preference; no flashing, no unrequested movement or sound; audio never starts without a tap and always has Pause or Stop
Colour independenceA11Y-23…24Colour never carries meaning alone — evidence labels are always text, pass and below-threshold results use words, streak cells add an icon, chart series differ by dash and marker; no instructions that depend on colour, position or sound
Speech-specificA11Y-25…29Labelled reference-speed control ("Normal", "Slower, 0.75 times speed") whose label matches the real rate; the Record button keeps one semantics node from Ready to Recording so two double-taps start and stop without moving focus; hands-free and low-dexterity recording (single tap, 500 ms debounce, keyboard toggle, auto-stop with a 10-second warning); reference audio always has its text on screen and the user's recording has its transcript as a text alternative with the tier label; preparation can be skipped and nothing advances automatically from a result
Keyboard, switch and voice accessA11Y-30…31Focused content is never hidden behind sticky bars, snackbars or the keyboard; every action works with a hardware keyboard, Switch Access and Voice Access, with visible focus; accessible names contain the visible label words
Errors, status and spoken valuesA11Y-32…35Errors say what happened and what to do and are announced; status messages never move focus; units are spelled out ("128 words per minute"); timers, symbols and placeholders have spoken equivalents ("23 seconds of 1 minute 30 seconds")
Localisation readinessA11Y-36…38Every visible and accessibility string in resources with plurals (about 386 hard-coded strings today); locale-aware numbers, dates and first day of the week; right-to-left and text-expansion ready (pseudolocale checks)

DES-07 §3–§12

12.5 Speech-specific questions for the Product Owner

QuestionRecommendation (not yet a decision)Decision
TalkBack speech played through the speaker can be captured by the microphone and appear in the transcript (INFERRED)Announce "Recording" before capture starts, make no announcements while recording, announce the result after Stop; suggest headphones when touch exploration is on. Delaying capture conflicts with the ≤ 300 ms start target and needs measuringD-33 Pending (G2)
Users who cannot complete a speaking step (temporary voice loss, speech disabilities, poor recognition of their speech)Keep the rule that a streak day needs a valid attempt; Skip never penalises; "This attempt doesn't count against you"; revisit after beta feedback and research with users with speech disabilitiesD-34 Pending (G2)
Users who stammer or have atypical speech may find pace, pause and filler metrics discouragingKeep neutral wording now; decide on a "hide selected metrics" preference after researchOQ-A11Y-3
Listen & repeat could hide the sentence to train listening, excluding deaf and hard-of-hearing usersSentence visible by default, with an optional "Hide text while listening"OQ-A11Y-7
No NFR IDs exist for keyboard and switch access, status messages, time limits, focus not obscured, locale formatting or RTLThe NFR owner adds measurable IDs; DES-07 does not invent themOQ-A11Y-4

DES-07 §9.1, §17; RD-99 §8 (D-33, D-34)

12.6 How usability and accessibility will be tested

DES-07 defines 39 accessibility test cases (TC-A11Y-001…039). None exists yet and none has been executed. Testing stays within the existing stack — no new test dependency is proposed for R0/R1.

CadenceChecksGate
Every pull request touching UIJVM and Robolectric Compose tests: token contrast computed from the palette, semantics (names, headings, roles, states, live regions), touch bounds ≥ 48 dp, text overflow at font scale 2.0 on a 360 × 640 dp window, keyboard toggles, reduced-motion and theme variants; the per-screen checklist for changed screensDefinition of Done
End of each UI epicManual run on one reference device: TalkBack traversal and announcements, dialogs and sheets, focus, snackbar timeouts, greyscale walkthroughEpic acceptance
Each release candidateRelease pack TC-A11Y-039: all automated tests plus manual TalkBack, Switch Access, Voice Access, Accessibility Scanner and pseudolocale runs on RD-LOW and RD-MID in both themesGate G6; test-strategy exit criteria
  • Tools available now: JUnit 4, Robolectric 4.16.1 with Compose UI test, instrumented Compose tests, and Compose previews for design review. Robolectric cannot run TalkBack or prove its real traversal order, so those checks stay manual.
  • Manual tools on the device: TalkBack, Switch Access, Voice Access, Accessibility Scanner, font and display size, "Remove animations", accessibility timeout, a monochrome colour simulation, pseudolocales and a hardware keyboard.
  • Would need approval: the Accessibility Test Framework checks, screenshot-testing libraries and UI Automator — each a new dependency, deferred (OQ-A11Y-6).
  • Usability research: the R1 beta screens 10+ testers and runs 5–8 interviews to validate the personas (ASM-01…06), and DES-07 recommends moderated sessions with TalkBack users, low-vision and motor-impaired users and people with speech disabilities on the R1 prototype (OQ-A11Y-5). Time-to-first-attempt and taps-to-recording are measured by TC-ONB-018 and TC-UX-002.

DES-07 §14–§15, §17; RD-08 §6; DES-04 §1.3; RD-99 §7.3

Part III

Development planning

How the approved scope becomes an ordered backlog and a sprint plan that one developer, working with AI assistance and with the Product Owner as the approver, can deliver one release at a time.

Chapter 13Product backlog

The backlog turns 78 product requirements, 75 user stories and 12 feature specifications into 27 delivery epics and 102 tasks. This chapter explains how the backlog is organised, when an item is ready to start and when it is done, and lists the R0 and R1 work in the order it is planned.

Planning status Every estimate in this chapter is preliminary (†): ideal engineering days for one developer working with AI assistance, made before any code spike. They will be re-estimated at gate G4 and again after spike SPK-01. Every decision named here is Proposed except D-08, which the Product Owner decided on 2026-11-21 (Decided — original wording, link out). No backlog item has started; Stage A produced documents only (PLN-05 GR-01).

PLN-02 v0.2 header and estimate note; PLN-05 §4 and §7.

13.1 How the backlog is structured

The backlog has two views of the same scope. The delivery view (PLN-02, the Work Breakdown Structure) is what gets scheduled: initiatives contain epics, epics contain features and tasks. The user view (USER_STORIES) is what gets accepted: one story epic per capability, each with user stories written as "As a learner, I want…, so that…" and Given/When/Then acceptance criteria. The Requirement Traceability Matrix (GOV-01) joins the two views.

LevelID patternExampleCountDefined in
InitiativeIN-nIN-2 Core loop (MVP)4PLN-02 §2
Delivery epicEP-nnEP-07 Evaluation & feedback27PLN-02 §2 (R0 5 · R1 13 · R2 6 · R3 3)
FeatureF-nn.nF-01.2 PR quality gates12PLN-02 §3 (named for R0 epics only; R1 epics list tasks directly)
TaskT-nn.n.n / T-nn.nT-07.8 Issue detection and tip selection102PLN-02 §3–4 (R0 40 · R1 62)
Story epicE-nnE-05 Honest feedback & evaluation13USER_STORIES §3 (one per capability, plus platform)
User storyUS-nnnUS-026 See what went wrong and a coach tip after every attempt75USER_STORIES §3.1 (71 in R0/R1, 4 in R2)

Every task carries a type and a size. Types: ENG engineering, TST test, DOC documentation, CNT content, DES design asset, OPS DevOps. Sizes: S ≤ 2 d, M 3–5 d, L 6–10 d, XL > 10 d. An XL item must be split before it can be scheduled, and the Definition of Ready (§13.2) asks for items of 5 days or less, so L tasks are split across sprints (for example T-03.1.1 Room entities appears as "part" in Sprint 5).

PLN-02 §1 and estimate note; PLN-03 §2; USER_STORIES §2–3.

13.2 Definition of Ready and Definition of Done

Two short lists protect the plan. The Definition of Ready stops work from starting before its decisions exist; the Definition of Done stops work from being called finished before it is evidenced. PLN-05 is authoritative for both; USER_STORIES §2.3 repeats them in story terms.

Definition of Ready — a story enters a sprint when

  1. The linked SRS-FR or SRS-BR and the FS acceptance criteria exist.
  2. The ADRs and decisions it depends on are approved.
  3. A design is available for UI work (DES-03, DES-06).
  4. The test approach is known: TST-01 level and fixtures.
  5. It is estimated at 5 days or less; otherwise it is split.

The story view adds: open decisions affecting the story are listed, and dependencies are approved or stubbed.

Definition of Done — a story or epic is done when

  1. The code implements the referenced SRS-FR/SRS-BR and passes code review against the ARC-02 conventions.
  2. Unit and Robolectric tests were written alongside the code; all CI gates are green (build, unit, Robolectric, lint, rules tests where relevant).
  3. FS/US acceptance criteria are demonstrated, with evidence attached (CI link, screenshots or recordings).
  4. Accessibility checks for new UI (DES-07) and the honesty checklist (TST-01 §9) pass.
  5. No new Critical/High defects; known issues are logged.
  6. Documentation is updated: the authoritative document and GOV-01 traceability.
  7. Feature-flag state is documented; no dead code is left for removed features.

PLN-03 §2 (Ready); PLN-05 §6 (Done); USER_STORIES §2.3.

At epic level, "done" is tested at gate G5: the epic's FS test cases are implemented and Passed in CI with the run URL, new UI has passed the automated DES-07 cases and the honesty-checklist items for the touched screens, GOV-01 rows are updated, and no S1 or S2 defect is open in the epic (TST-01 §10.4).

13.3 Prioritisation and ordering

PriorityRuleExamples
P0Unsafe, legally exposed, broken core, or blocks other workEP-01, EP-04, EP-05, EP-06, EP-07
P1Needed for the MVP to be useful or compliant, but can follow P0EP-15, EP-16
P2Valuable depth, not required for the MVPEP-21, EP-22
P3Optional—

Within a priority, items are ordered by dependency first, then risk reduction (spikes before the features that depend on them), then user value (the core loop before breadth), then effort. A user story inherits the priority of the product requirement it traces; where it traces several, it takes the highest.

PLN-02 §6; USER_STORIES §2.2.

13.4 R0 Foundation backlog (ordered)

R0 makes the existing codebase safe to build on. It removes unsafe, unlicensed and misleading features, puts tests in front of every merge, restructures the app and lays the local data foundation. It also answers the biggest technical question, whether on-device speech recognition is good enough, before any R1 code depends on it.

R0 total38–55 d≈ 5–7 sprints

EP-09 Content pack & content production

Goal. 13.7 The content-verification lane

One piece of R1 work is not engineering at all and does not fit inside the developer's sprint capacity: checking the existing creator-derived content against the original videos. Under decision D-08 ("original wording, link out", Decided 2026-11-21), the curriculum keeps the frameworks, techniques, exercises and tips taught on the source channel, but every item must be re-expressed in SpeakCraft's own words and examples, linked to its specific source video and timestamp, and shipped without the creator's name, likeness or branding. The audit of the current app (CNT-01) found 307 creator-derived items, none with specific-video provenance. The backlog therefore treats task T-09.6 as its own lane.

307
existing creator-derived items to verify (CNT-01)
≈ 300 h
≈ 38 person-days of 8 h † (CNT-01 §6.3)
≈ 15 †
techniques proposed for the R1 set (D-36, Pending)

Who does what

The Product Owner watches the source videos, confirms each technique and timestamp, and acts as the reviewer. The coding agent drafts the rewrites, fills the provenance records and runs the content-pack validator. A second reviewer checks wording, examples, branding and evaluation honesty before an item becomes active (CNT-01 step 7). The lane runs in parallel with engineering but sits on the R1 critical path for content freeze (RSK-04, RSK-17; dependency DEP-10).

Workflow per item and its Definition of Done

StepActionDone when
1 Find the videoSearch the channel by hand from the cited title or candidateThe specific video is open and is on the channel
2 Confirm technique and timestampWatch the relevant part; note start (and end) time; record any mismatchThe start time points at the teaching moment
3 RewriteName, one-line idea, why it works, 2–7 steps and original examples, at the target levelNo sentence is traceable to the video's wording
4 Record provenanceFill the provenance block; add &t=<seconds>s to the source URL; pseudonymous verifiedBy, verifiedOnValidator rules V-11 and V-12 pass
5 Set licence statuslicenceStatus = ORIGINAL_REEXPRESSION; attributionText = nullV-11 passes
6 Link exercises and tipsLink ≥ 1 activity both ways; general and issue-triggered tips; self-check items for what audio cannot showV-03, V-09, V-15, V-17 pass
7 Second reviewSecond reviewer checks wording, examples, branding, evaluation honestyBoth reviewers recorded; the item passes validateContentPack; status ACTIVE
Rules that apply at every step No ingestion (no scraping, caption download or YouTube Data API — the reviewer watches the video like any viewer). No verbatim text in any user-visible field. No creator name, handle, likeness or coined framework name in displayed text. Every example is newly invented. A technique's target metrics list only real ARC-05 metric keys; anything else becomes a self-check item. No match, no ship: an item that cannot be matched to a specific video stays PENDING_REVIEW and is excluded from release builds.

CNT-01 §6.1–6.2; ARC-03 §6.12 (validator rules V-01…V-22).

Chapter 14Sprint plan

Work is planned in two-week sprints, never above capacity, and only with items whose decisions are already made. Sprint 0 prepares Stage B; Sprints 1–5 are planned task by task; everything after Sprint 5 is indicative and will be re-planned from real velocity.

Planning status The sprint plan is preliminary. No sprint has run, so there is no measured velocity. Sprint numbers count from the start of Stage B, which begins only after the Product Owner's explicit "start Stage B" instruction at gate G4. No calendar dates are committed (PLN-01).

PLN-03 v0.2 header and §1; PLN-01 header note; PLN-05 §3.

RuleDetail
CommitmentOnly stories whose dependencies and decisions are resolved may enter a sprint (Definition of Ready, §13.2). No sprint is planned above capacity.
Split large itemsItems above 5 days are split; an L task appears in two sprints as "part 1" and "part 2" (for example T-05.1 in Sprints 3 and 4).
One epic at a timeEach approved epic is developed on a feature branch with small, reviewable pull requests; incomplete work stays behind feature flags (PLN-05 GR-02).

A capacity question for G4 PLN-01 converts the estimates into sprints using roughly the full 8 focus days (R0 38–55 d ≈ 5–7 sprints; R1 101–146 d ≈ 13–18 sprints, plan uses 14). PLN-03 plans each sprint at 6.5 d. At 6.5 d per sprint, the same estimates would need about 6–8.5 sprints for R0 and 15.5–22.5 sprints for R1. The re-baselining at G4 should pick one basis. The content-verification lane (§13.7) is outside both figures because it is Product Owner time.

PLN-01 §1–2; PLN-03 §1; arithmetic on PLN-02 §2 totals.

The content lanes

Content tasks T-09.2…T-09.5 run continuously from Sprint 4 at about 1–2 d per sprint. The verification task T-09.6 is Product Owner time and, per RD-99 §9, starts right after G4 and must finish before content freeze. PLN-01 §4 warns that content becomes critical if it is not started during R0 (Sprints 3–5).

Open point Sprints 4 and 5 are already planned at the full 6.5 d, so the 1–2 d of content work per sprint is not inside their planned load. Either part of the content work is done by the Product Owner and the agent outside engineering capacity, or the engineering items in those sprints move. This needs a decision when Sprint 0 orders the backlog.

PLN-03 §5–6; PLN-01 §4; RD-99 §9.

14.7 Ceremonies for a one-person team

SpeakCraft is an individual project. The coding agent operates every delivery role and prepares the artefacts; the Product Owner is the single accountable decision-maker and approves. The agent never approves its own work, never passes a gate without explicit approval, and commits, pushes or releases only when the Product Owner asks (PLN-05 §1, GR-10). The ceremonies are kept short and evidence-based.

CeremonyWhenAgent preparesProduct OwnerOutput

Part IV

Delivery lifecycle

How the plan will be carried out once Stage B starts: implementation, testing, the build and release pipeline, release and deployment, and maintenance. None of these activities has started; each chapter states which deliverable it will produce and how that deliverable will be accepted.

Chapter 15Implementation

Implementation rebuilds SpeakCraft inside the existing technology stack, one approved epic at a time. This chapter describes how code will be written and reviewed, in which order, and what each technical area — screens, Firebase, the local database, internal interfaces and platform integrations — will look like.

Deliverable status — Working application Not yet produced — created in Stage B after approval (gate G4). No line of the redeveloped application has been written. Stage A changed documentation only (PLN-05 GR-01); there was no JDK or Android SDK in the analysis environment, so nothing was compiled or run (GOV-02 DC-4).

How it will be produced. Epic by epic in the order of Chapter 13, each on a feature branch, merged to main through small pull requests with all required CI checks green, and reviewed by the Product Owner at gate G5. R0 produces internal, release-signed builds (never debug builds) and the R0 review pack. R1 produces a release candidate, beta builds and, after gates G6 and G7, the R1 release.

What it will contain (R1). Four primary destinations — Today, Practice, Words, Progress — plus onboarding, the session player, profile and settings: 38 R1 screens (42 including R2). Five tracks with five activity templates, at least 300 words, a Techniques collection under D-08, deterministic on-device feedback with mistakes and coach tips, speech tiers T1–T3b, offline-first storage with optional Google sign-in and sync.

Acceptance. R0: the RD-06 §5 exit criterion as verified in TST-01 §10.3, CI green on main, SPK-01 go/no-go recorded. R1: PRD §21 items 1–8 and the TST-01 §10.3 R1 exit criteria, signed at G6 and released at G7.

PLN-05 §3–4; PLN-01 R0/R1 phases; DES-02 §3; GOV-01 §3; PRD §21; TST-01 §10.3.

15.3 Frontend — Compose screens, ViewModels and the design system

The user interface is rebuilt, not re-skinned. Most current screens are retired; the new screens follow the information architecture (DES-02), screen inventory (DES-03), flows (DES-04) and UI specifications (DES-06), and are built only from design-system components (DES-05).

Feature packageScreens (DES-02 IDs)ViewModels (component)Epic
feature.onboardingSCR-ONB-01…08, SCR-PERM-01OnboardingViewModel (CMP-63)EP-13
feature.todaySCR-TODAY-01, -02TodayViewModel (CMP-64)EP-08
feature.sessionSCR-SESS-01, -02SessionViewModel (CMP-65)EP-08
feature.practiceSCR-PRAC-01…04PracticeHome, Track, ActivityDetail (CMP-66); Techniques, TechniqueDetail (CMP-76)EP-10
feature.wordsSCR-WORD-01…04 (05–06 in R2)WordsHome, WordDetail, WordStageFlow, ReviewSession (CMP-67)EP-11
feature.progressSCR-PROG-01…04 (05 in R2)Progress, MetricDetail, History, AttemptDetail (CMP-68)EP-12
feature.profileSCR-PROF-01ProfileViewModel (CMP-69)EP-14
feature.settingsSCR-SET-01…09LearningSettings, SpeechPrivacy, Appearance, DataPrivacy, About (CMP-70)EP-14
feature.settings.accountSCR-SET-07, SCR-ACC-01, -02Account, SignIn, DeleteAccount, DeletionResume (CMP-71)EP-13
feature.remindersSCR-SET-03, SCR-PERM-01ReminderSettingsViewModel (CMP-72)EP-15
feature.inbox (R2)SCR-INBOX-01InboxViewModel (CMP-73), behind FEATURE_INBOXEP-22

ARC-02 §2.1, §2.3.6; DES-02 §3.

Conventions every screen follows

  • Route and screen split. XRoute collects state from the ViewModel; XScreen(state, onIntent) is stateless and previewable.
  • One state stream per ViewModel. StateFlow<XUiState> built with stateIn(viewModelScope, WhileSubscribed(5_000), Loading); composables use collectAsStateWithLifecycle().
  • Type-safe navigation. @Serializable AppRoute objects; four tabs with saved state; full-screen routes for the session player, onboarding and settings. Feature packages never import each other.
  • No business objects in composables. Composables never reference repositories, AppContainer or Firestore; ViewModels never hold Context or Activity. ViewModels are created by one factory each from the process-scoped container (CMP-05).
  • Every FS §9 state is drawn. Default, loading, empty, error, offline, permission and feature-specific states for every screen, each rendered in tests.
  • Accessibility by construction. Strings, plurals and content descriptions in resources; locale-aware number and date formatting; 48 dp targets; headings; errors announced; meaning never carried by colour alone.

Design-system components (EP-17)

Tokens for colour (Calm Indigo, decision D-18 pending), type, shape, spacing and motion come first (T-17.1). The core components (T-17.2) are the building blocks of every R1 screen: buttons, cards, MetricTile, FeedbackCard, InconclusiveCard, RecordButton, MistakeList, CoachTipCard, SelfCheckList and the state templates. Accessibility test utilities (T-17.3) — font scale 2.0 and semantics checks — are in place before the screens are built, so each screen PR can run the automated TC-A11Y cases.

ARC-02 §3.3, §4.4, §11.4, §11.7; PLN-02 EP-17; DES-05 §11.

15.4 "Backend" — Firebase Auth, Firestore rules and sync

There is no custom server SpeakCraft has no server of its own and none is proposed: no REST or GraphQL APIs, no Cloud Functions, no admin endpoints. The device talks only to managed services through their SDKs, and only when the user is signed in. In no-account mode the app makes no network request at all. Any server-side component would need an ADR.

ARC-04 §1 (P-1…P-6); SEC-01 §1.2.

PartDesignDecision / ADR
Firebase AuthenticationOptional Google sign-in through Credential Manager. The web client id is looked up at runtime, so a build without Firebase configuration still runs the full no-account core loop and simply hides sign-in. Errors are mapped to user-safe messages; account deletion requires re-authentication.D-05 (account optional); ENG-01 §3.2 option C; SEC-REQ-05/06
Cloud FirestoreThe existing named database, used as a sync target only. Per-user paths under users/{uid}/…; bounded, watermark-based pulls instead of unbounded listeners; writes never awaited on UI paths.ADR-002, ADR-014; ARC-03 §8
Security rulesAllow-list rules per collection: unauthenticated and cross-user access denied, field validation, attempt immutability, forbidden content (no transcript, audio or file paths), size limits, shared and admin collections denied. Tested by the emulator suite TC-SEC-020…038 as the required rules-test check.SEC-01 §5; ENG-T-09
Rules deploymentRules are deployed before the app release when the change is additive. Recommended: manual deployment by the Release Manager from a clean checkout of the tag, with the CLI output and ruleset id recorded in the release checklist.ED-06 / D-29 (Pending)
Sync engineLocal first: every write goes to Room in a transaction and creates an outbox row. SyncWorker drains the outbox when signed in and online. Facts (attempts, stage passes, reviews) are immutable with client UUIDs; aggregates such as streaks are derived locally, never stored as cloud counters. Mutable documents use last-writer-wins per document, except vocabulary state, which keeps the more advanced stage.ADR-014; ADR-005 (WorkManager)
What syncsProfile, preferences, plans, attempt metrics, vocabulary progress, streak facts. Never by default: audio and transcripts (transcript sync is a possible R2 opt-in, D-16).ADR-014 §6; ARC-03 §8.4
Legacy cloud dataProfile and vocabulary progress are imported once (D-19); legacy documents stay untouched for a migration window and are then removed by an operator script; legacy rules move through transition stages L1/L2.D-19; SEC-01 §5.6; ENG-T-27
App CheckDebug provider in the debug source set only; Play Integrity once distributed through Play; until then not enforced, and per-user security relies on Auth plus rules.ADR-008; D-26

Not present today and not proposed for R1: Firebase Storage, Cloud Functions, Cloud Messaging, Remote Config, Analytics and Crashlytics (ARC-04 §2). Crashlytics would need a separate approval if the local diagnostic log and Play vitals prove insufficient (ADR-007); opt-in analytics is to be revisited, under a new approval, before any public store launch (ADR-013). TC-PRIV-009 checks that no analytics or crash-reporting SDK is on the release classpath.

15.5 Database — Room, DAOs, migrations and the legacy import

Room becomes the single local source of truth for learning data (ADR-002, decision D-11 pending), with DataStore kept for preferences. Data is split across two databases so that the most sensitive content can be handled separately.

StoreContentsNotes
learning.db v1 (R1)ENT-01 Profile, ENT-02 LearningPreferences, ENT-03 Attempt (with attempt_issue), ENT-04 AttemptMetric, ENT-05 SessionPlan, ENT-06 SessionStep, ENT-07 WordProgress, ENT-08 WordReview, ENT-09 LearningEvent, ENT-10 SyncOutbox, ENT-11 RecordingFile, ENT-12 SavedActivity, ENT-13 FeedbackRating, ENT-15 NotebookEntry (table in v1, UI in R2)Indexes and immutability triggers per ARC-03 §5
sensitive.db v1 (R1)attempt_transcript, ENT-14 SyncState, ENT-18 DeletionStateTranscripts are personal data that never sync by default
DataStoresettings and device_state storesTheme, reminders, recognition locale, seen tips
FilesnoBackupFilesDir/recordings/<profileId>/<attemptId>WAV at capture, transcoded to AAC after evaluation; 30-day default retention (ADR-015, D-10)

Write paths and transactions

Saving an evaluated attempt writes the attempt, its issues and metrics, the recording row, the plan step or session-completed event, any word progress and the outbox rows in one transaction; the transcript goes to sensitive.db first and is removed again if the transaction fails. No network or file I/O happens inside a Room transaction; facts insert with "ignore on conflict" on their UUID, so a replay never duplicates them; Flows re-emit after commit, so screens update without manual refresh.

ARC-03 §3, §7.8, §9.3; ARC-02 §7.5.

Migration policy

RuleDetail
Two version lineslearning.db and sensitive.db each start at version 1; versions start counting from the first distributed build that contains Room
Exported schemasOne JSON per version committed under app/schemas; CI fails if the schema changes without a version bump and a migration test
Forward onlyEvery step n → n+1 has an AutoMigration or a hand-written SQL migration; downgrade is unsupported and the app says so without touching the files
No destructive fallbackfallbackToDestructiveMigration is never used in a distributed build (static check in TC-MIG-002)
Heavy data fixesRun as a one-off WorkManager job after open, not inside a migration
Cloud documents and content packCloud schemaVersion stays 1 while changes are additive; the content pack has its own schemaVersion and packVersion and never needs a Room migration

ARC-03 §9.1–9.3.

Legacy import (decision D-19, Pending)

On first launch of an R1 build with a Firebase session, the app imports the legacy profile (display name, level, goal, daily minutes) and vocabulary progress, mapping legacy values onto the new model: levels to the five plain levels, free-text goals to tracks by keyword, and matched words to stage 1 only, because legacy reviews were self-rated flashcards rather than verified speech. Client-written aggregates (streaks, totals) and unvalidated scores are not imported. The import is idempotent — a re-run produces the same rows — and does not modify the legacy documents. It is tested on the Firestore emulator with synthetic legacy snapshots LEG-01…LEG-07 (TC-MIG-003…006).

ARC-03 §9.4; TST-01 §6.4.

An embedded official YouTube player is not proposed; it would be a new integration needing an ADR, a privacy review and data-safety updates.

ARC-04 §2, §3.1–3.10.

Concurrency rules the integrations rely on

DispatcherUsed forRule
mainCompose, ViewModel state, all SpeechRecognizer calls and callbacks, TTSNever disk or network I/O (NFR-PERF-012)
defaultEvaluation pipeline, plan generation, progress calculationsCPU work only; pipeline under a timeout
ioDataStore, recordings, export, diagnosticsRoom suspend DAOs use Room's own executors
audio (single thread)AudioRecord read loop, WAV writer, streaming featuresPre-allocated buffers; no allocation per frame
pipe (single thread)Writes PCM to the T1 recogniser pipe from a bounded queueNever blocks capture: on overflow the pipe closes and capture continues

Only one recording can be active app-wide: a non-blocking RecordingArbiter grants a lease or refuses at once, instead of queueing a second recording behind a lock. Workers use unique names (sync-<profileId>, reminder-<profileId>, cleanup-retention); sign-out and deletion cancel all work tagged with the profile. Leaving the player during processing lets the save finish so a valid attempt is never lost (NFR-REL-004).

15.8 Reuse plan — keep, refactor or delete

Every existing source file has a decision. Of the 106 main Kotlin files, ARC-02 keeps 1, refactors 24 (their logic or pattern is ported into a named component and the file is then deleted), re-verifies and migrates 4 (their content moves into the bundled content pack after editorial review) and deletes 77.

1 · 24

4
files re-verified and migrated as content
77
files deleted with no successor code
AreaNotable decisions
Application and enginesAppContainer rewritten as a process-scoped container (CMP-02). SpacedRepetitionScheduler refactored into CMP-21 with the new grades and intervals. StreakAndCompletionEngine refactored into the single streak definition (CMP-20). Duplicate plan engines, the parallel activity system and LearningProgressManager deleted. LocalVocabularyQuizEngine is the only file kept, for the R2 quiz.
Content held in codeThe three activity catalogue files (150 hand-written activities) and CurriculumCatalog are re-verified and migrated: reviewed, de-branded, re-expressed under D-08 and moved into the content pack.
Speech and evaluationEvaluationEvidenceValidator becomes the EvidenceGate, keeping its fail-closed design. The alignment in PronunciationAnalysisEngine is reused and relabelled "Recognised as intended". LocalVocabularyEvaluator keeps the 75/75/70 thresholds. LiveTranscriptionManager is rewritten without the silent network fallback. The fabricated Whisper path, cloud-AI services, mentor templates and video engines are deleted.
Firebase and preferencesAuthHelper and AppCheckConfig refactored (launch-intent debug token removed). The 1,351-line SpeakCraftRepository is deleted; only its legacy reads survive in the migrator (CMP-58). The updater and YouTube clients are deleted.
UIThe 1,548-line AppNavigation, the large component files and almost all current screens and ViewModels are deleted; the theme files are refactored into the design system; the launch animation is cut to a brand moment of at most 1 second.
TestsOf 161 existing tests in 24 files: 1 file (15 tests, the evidence-gate suite) kept and ported; 9 files (86 tests) rewritten; 14 files (60 tests) deleted together with their features. The Robolectric + Compose harness is kept.

ARC-02 §10.1–10.5 and totals; TST-01 §2.2.

15.9 Coding standards

TopicStandard
Size and structureFiles ≤ 600 lines unless justified in the PR; functions ≤ 60 lines as a guideline; one public top-level type per file; implementations internal
NamingXRepository / RoomXRepository; FakeX test doubles; XViewModel, XUiState, XEvent; XRoute + XScreen; Proposed (†) constants only in AppConfig, PolicyConstants, EvaluationConfig, SyncConfig
Time and datesNo direct clock or time-zone calls outside core/common/time; inject Clock and DateKeyProvider; the dateKey is computed at event time and stored with the fact
Layeringdomain imports no Android, AndroidX or Firebase; only named components import Firestore, Firebase Auth, SpeechRecognizer or AudioRecord (CI import allow-list)
Honesty in codeA metric without inputs is null with a reason — no defaults, baselines or placeholders (?: 0, ?: 62 are review blockers); confidence only when supplied; every metric carries its label; feedback, issues and tips are deterministic, with no generative or cloud model; counters derived from facts
Testing hooksInterfaces and fakes for every repository and service; injected clock, ids and dispatchers; fake recogniser seam; audio and transcript fixtures; in-memory Room; work-testing (with ADR-005); Firestore emulator
Logging, errors, dependenciesLogging only through AppLogger with enumerated codes and no personal data; Outcome and AppError at boundaries; one JSON stack (kotlinx-serialization); only ADR-approved dependencies, versions in the catalogue

ARC-02 §11.1–11.8.

15.10 Code review

Every pull request is reviewed against the checklist below before it is merged. The agent prepares the PR and completes the checklist; branch protection requires a pull request and the four required checks, and with a solo developer self-merge is allowed only when those checks are green (ENG-01 §4.3, decision ED-08). Changes to sensitive paths — workflows, rules, Gradle files, the manifest and backup rules — are routed to the Security and Release Engineer roles through CODEOWNERS. The Product Owner reviews at the epic level (G5), not every PR.

#CheckWhat the reviewer looks forAutomated support
1CorrectnessImplements the referenced SRS-FR/SRS-BR and FS acceptance criteria; boundary values handled; failures returned as Outcome; CancellationException rethrown; no I/O inside a Room transactionUnit and Robolectric suites in build-test
2Tests alongside codeTests in the same PR; TC ID in the test name; assertions on meaning, not presence; hand-written fakes; below/at/above cases for each business rule; a fixed defect carries a test that failed before the fixTraceability script; TC-REG-005, TC-REG-021
3Honesty rulesNull over default; Measured/Estimated labels as text; no composite score for free speaking; no "AI", "pronunciation" or "accent" labels on deterministic output; empty state never implies perfection; counters derivedTC-REG-019, -020, -025, -026
4Privacy egressNo new network call, SDK, permission or endpoint without an approved ADR and the SEC-01 §9.7 review; no audio or transcript leaves the device; logs go through AppLogger; recordings stay under noBackupFilesDir; PrivacyFacts updated when data flows changeTC-SEC-010, -011, -012, -041; TC-PRIV-009
5AccessibilitySemantics and roles; headings; 48 dp targets; text labels, not colour only; layout at font scale 2.0; reduced-motion variantAutomated TC-A11Y cases (Robolectric)
6Strings in resourcesAll user-visible strings, plurals and content descriptions in resources; locale-aware formattingTC-REG-012; TC-A11Y-030
7No secretsNo keys, keystores, tokens or Firebase configuration files in code or workflows; secrets only in protected environments; no secrets in ci.ymlTC-SEC-043, TC-SEC-045
8Architecture and layeringImport boundaries respected; one engine per concern; file-size rule; only allow-listed dependenciesTC-REG-007, -008, -009, -011
9Data changesSchema change comes with a version bump, a migration and a migration test; no destructive fallbackTC-MIG-001, -002
10Content changesNo creator name or branding; specific-video provenance; nothing PENDING_REVIEW in release; no verbatim fieldsTC-CONT-001…005
11Docs and traceabilityAuthoritative document and GOV-01 updated in the same PR; no "verified" or "passed" claim without a CI link; flag state documented; no dead code for removed featuresTC-REG-010

PLN-05 §4, §6; ARC-02 §11; TST-01 §1.2, §7.4, §14.1, §16.5; SEC-01 §13; DES-07 §15; ENG-01 §4.3.

Review gate G5 — epic review

What the Product Owner receives

  • The epic's pull requests
  • The test report from CI: run URL, JUnit results, coverage, golden diffs, rule-precision table
  • Updated documents (docs diff) and GOV-01 rows with PR and CI-run links
  • Demo notes, screenshots or recordings
  • Open defects by severity and the flag state

When it can be approved

  • The Definition of Done (§13.2) holds for every story in the epic
  • The epic's FS test cases are implemented and Passed in CI, with the run URL
  • New UI passed the automated accessibility cases and the honesty-checklist items for the touched screens
  • No open Critical/High (S1/S2) defect in the epic

PLN-05 §3 (G5), §8 (epic review pack); TST-01 §10.4.

Chapter 16Testing and quality assurance

Quality in SpeakCraft means more than "it does not crash". The product's promise is honesty: no number, label or status on screen that is not backed by evidence, no audio leaving the device by default, and no user data lost. The test strategy (TST-01) is built around proving those promises with evidence rather than asserting them.

Deliverable status — Test reports Not yet produced — created in Stage B after approval (gate G4). No test report exists. Stage A executed no build and no test, so every one of the 510 planned test cases is Not run. The existing repository contains 161 JVM and Robolectric tests, but none has ever run in CI, and the documented "161 passed" in an old report cannot be verified.

How reports will be produced. Every pull request run publishes JUnit XML/HTML, lint SARIF, coverage, the rules-coverage report and the traceability report as CI artefacts. At the end of each epic these become the epic test report for gate G5. For each release, manual and device runs are recorded in test-run records (TRRs) and combined into the release-candidate test report and the UAT report for gate G6. The template is in §16.14.

Acceptance. A test is Passed only when it was executed and its output is linked. Release exit follows TST-01 §10.3; the Product Owner signs the UAT report at G6.

TST-01 Stage A notice, §2, §8.4; GOV-01 §3; PLN-05 GR-03.

16.1 Objectives and principles

IDObjectiveMeasured by
TO-01The core loop works on real devices of every speech tierCritical features CF-01…CF-19 passed on the device matrix
TO-02Honesty: no fabricated, defaulted or mislabelled output, including mistakes and coach tipsEvaluation-quality gates (§16.7); honesty checklist (§16.8)
TO-03Privacy claims hold: no egress by default, backup exclusions, complete export and deletionTC-PRIV, TC-SEC
TO-04User data is protected: no lost valid attempt, idempotent events, safe migrationsTC-REL, TC-MIG
TO-05Every approved NFR target is measured on the reference devices and reported honestlyTC-PERF, TC-COMP
TO-06Regressions are stopped earlyRequired PR gates; broader suites nightly and per release candidate
TO-07The Product Owner gets evidence, not assertions, at G5, G6 and G7Evidence rules; exit criteria
TO-08Creator-derived content complies with D-08TC-CONT-001…008; content-compliance checklist
IDPrincipleRule in practice
TP-01Never mark an unexecuted test as passedPassed needs a CI run URL with JUnit output or a test-run record with screenshots and logs; "should pass" is not allowed
TP-02Tests run in CI before claimsNo PR, CHANGELOG or release note says "tested" or "verified" without the run that proves it (checked by TC-REG-010)
TP-03Real audio fixturesRecorded, annotated speech and recogniser-output transcripts — a sine wave is not speech
TP-04Assert meaning, not presenceCompare values with annotations and goldens; "score in 1..100" or "node exists" is never the only assertion
TP-05Null over defaultMissing evidence must produce null, "Not available" or Inconclusive; a test that accepts a default is itself a defect
TP-06Hermetic and deterministicInjected clock and dispatchers, virtual time, fixed locale and zone, no network, no google-services.json; Firestore only on the emulator with a demo- project
TP-07Fakes, not mocksHand-written fakes of the ARC-04 interfaces; a mocking library would be a new dependency and is not proposed
TP-08Test pyramidMostly JVM unit tests; Compose UI on Robolectric; instrumented tests only for what a device alone can show
TP-09Boundaries for every ruleEach SRS-BR tested at, just below and just above each boundary
TP-10Proposed targets are reported, not bentA missed target is reported as Failed with the measured value; only the Product Owner can waive or re-baseline; emulator timings never count for device targets
TP-11Test-data privacyVoice fixtures only with written consent; no real user data; test accounts only in the dev Firebase project
TP-12One home per test IDEach TC is defined in one document; a fixed defect gets a regression test before it closes

TST-01 §1.1–1.2.

TST-01 §1.3.

16.2 Starting point

The current repository has 161 test methods in 24 files under app/src/test, one trivial instrumented test, and four Firestore rules tests that cannot run because there is no package.json. No test has ever run in CI: the only workflow builds and publishes. Repositories, authentication, plan engines, the scheduler, the recorder, navigation, offline behaviour, rotation and process death, font scaling and TalkBack are untested, and several tests give false assurance (for example a "zero-cloud" interceptor attached to no client). The harness itself — Robolectric 4.16.1 with Compose UI tests and fake-based state-machine tests — is worth keeping.

TST-01 §5.1–5.7 (abridged).

16.6 Test data

Audio fixtures

WAV, 16 kHz mono PCM16, each with a JSON annotation (word timings, pause count, pace, expected gate outcome, consent id), labelled independently by two annotators. FX-A-01…23 cover silence, noise, short and boundary cases, normal, fast and slow speech, long pauses, clipping, read-aloud, structured answers, fillers and word stages; FX-A-24…63 are ≥ 40 consented accented B1–B2 recordings from ≥ 5 speakers. Recogniser outputs per device class are stored as transcript fixtures, so JVM tests never need a live recogniser. A SHA-256 manifest catches silent changes.

Transcript corpus (SPK-02)

For each candidate grammar rule: ≥ 30 positive and ≥ 30 negative examples in lower-case, unpunctuated, recogniser-style text, including the false positives found in the audit. Two annotators; a development split and a held-out split, with held-out precision reported alongside the gate.

Legacy snapshot

Synthetic Firestore emulator exports LEG-01…LEG-07 (complete, missing fields, each legacy level vocabulary, large, malformed, already migrated, interrupted). A copy of the Product Owner's own legacy account only with explicit consent.

Seeded databaseContentUsed by
DB-EMPTYNew local profile, no dataFirst-run tests
DB-1DAYOnboarded profile, baseline attempt, today's planStart-up timing
DB-30D30 days of daily use, default retentionStorage after 30 days; retention
DB-2YTwo years of history: ≈ 3,000 attempts, ≈ 20,000 metric rows, ≈ 9,000 events, ≈ 2,500 transcriptsQuery performance, migrations, export size
DB-EDGETime-zone changes, a DST transition, missed days, retired content versions, Inconclusive attempts on streak boundariesBusiness-rule boundaries; export golden
DB-2PROF · DB-OUTBOX50Two interleaved profiles · two years plus 50 un-uploaded attemptsProfile isolation · sync recovery after 7 days offline

Other data sets include valid and invalid content packs (CP-VALID, CP-INVALID-*, including branding, provenance, pending-review and verbatim cases), the brand deny-list maintained by the Product Owner, hashed 8-word sequences of legacy creator-derived text (no raw text stored), the export golden, the privacy-facts snapshot, a PII log corpus and the tip fixtures (TIP-FIX) with fixed attempt IDs. Fixture and golden changes go through PR review; a golden change requires an evaluationVersion bump.

TST-01 §6.1–6.6.

16.7 Evaluation-quality testing

The evaluation pipeline is what makes SpeakCraft trustworthy or not, so it has its own quality gates on top of ordinary tests. Every gate below runs on fixtures in CI (except the device-only ones), and every gate is Not run today.

Golden fixtures per evaluationVersion

For every speech fixture and its transcript fixture, the full evaluation result is stored as a golden file: gate outcome and reason, every metric value or null with its reason, Measured/Estimated label, headline, band, strength and focus, rule hits and the "How we measured this" sources. Goldens live under fixtures/golden/<evaluationVersion>/; mistakes and coach-tip goldens add a <contentVersion> level, because issues are versioned with the evaluation rules and tips with the content pack. Comparison is exact. A CI script fails any PR that changes goldens of the current version without bumping the version, and the previous version's goldens are kept so reviewers can diff behaviour.

GateTarget (NFR, Proposed †)Data setBlockingTC
Evidence-gate correctness100 % of gate fixtures classified correctly, outcome and reasonFX-A-01…10 + valid fixturesPRTC-REG-015
Rule precisionEach shipped rule ≥ 0.90 † on ≥ 30 positive / ≥ 30 negative examplesSPK-02 corpusPRTC-REG-016
Metric accuracyWPM within ± 10 %, long pauses within ± 1 of annotationFX-A-11…16, FX-A-24…63PR (JVM); RC (device record)TC-REG-017
Inconclusive rate≤ 15 % on T1/T2 for valid-speech fixturesFX-A-24…63 played to DEV-H1, DEV-M1RCTC-REG-018
DeterminismSame inputs give identical metrics and feedback, under other default locales and time zonesAll goldens, run twicePRTC-REG-014, -023
No fabrication0 default or baseline scores, constant confidences or simulated analysisStatic scan + null-on-missing-evidence testsPRTC-REG-019
Labels carry textMeasured / Estimated / Inconclusive / tier labels are text, not colour onlyRobolectric result screensPRTC-REG-020
Mistakes and coach tipsDetected issues and selected tips equal the goldens per evaluationVersion and contentVersionTIP-FIX with fixed attempt IDsPRTC-REG-024
Honest empty state, self-check, non-AI tipsEmpty state never implies perfection; self-check is self-reported; tips never presented as AIRobolectric result screensPRTC-REG-025
Candidate acoustic metricsPitch range and volume variation absent from R1 until validated by SPK-01 and approvedRelease build; stringsPR / releaseTC-REG-026
Field signalsKPI-05 ≥ 75 % positive accuracy ratings †; KPI-06 ≤ 15 % Inconclusive †Beta summariesR1 exit input, not a pass/fail testUAT

How the rule-precision gate works

  1. A rule registry lists each rule with id, version and status shipped or candidate; only shipped rules can run in production.
  2. Precision = true positives ÷ (true positives + false positives); recall is reported, not gated.
  3. CI fails if any shipped rule has fewer than 30 positive or 30 negative examples, or precision below 0.90 †. The Wilson 95 % lower bound is shown for information.
  4. A failing rule is demoted to candidate through a reviewed code change.
  5. The grammar issue ISSUE_GRAMMAR_POSSIBLE can only come from a shipped rule, so mistake detection inherits this gate; at most two observations per attempt, worded as "Possible issue".

Mistakes found and coach tips

Golden tests map fixed transcripts and metric sets to the expected issues and tips. They assert that an issue is raised only when its source metric is non-null and outside its target band by more than the metric's noise band (boundary fixtures sit at the edge plus the noise band, just beyond it, and at null); that each issue carries its evidence and label; that issues are ordered by priority with the first three shown; that an Inconclusive attempt shows the gate reason and no issues; that tip selection ranks by trigger specificity, then tip priority, then a stable hash of tip id and attempt id — so the same attempt always gets the same tips; that a no-issue attempt shows "Our checks didn't find issues in this attempt", the coverage note, the strength and exactly one general tip; and that each tip links to its technique and, where provenance exists, to the specific source video.

Known gap before goldens can be fixed ARC-05 defines a target band only for M-WPM and gives no noise band for several metrics that raise issues, and its priority table omits some issue codes. Boundary goldens for ISSUE_TOO_SHORT, ISSUE_HEDGING_HIGH, ISSUE_FILLERS_HIGH, ISSUE_PAUSES_LONG, ISSUE_LOW_VARIETY and ISSUE_TOO_QUIET cannot be written until these values exist (TST-01 §17 issues 20–21; risk TR-21).

TST-01 §7.1–7.6, §17.

16.8 Honesty review checklist

The checklist is run before every release (R0 internal builds, every R1 and R2 release candidate, before G6) and, for the screens a story touches, as part of the Definition of Done. It implements PRD §21 item 3: no screen shows a metric, score, status or label that is not backed by evidence. The QA Architect role runs it on a release candidate with T1 and T3b devices and the DB-EMPTY, DB-1DAY and DB-EDGE data sets, records Pass/Fail with evidence in a TRR, and raises a defect for each failure. Any Fail blocks the release.

IDCheckIDCheck
HON-01No fabricated values — every number traces to a metric or a statistic from valid attemptsHON-13Tier labels match the tier actually used
HON-02No default, baseline or fallback valuesHON-14Celebrations only at real streak milestones
HON-03No fabricated statuses (capability, pack, sync, permission)HON-15Reminder copy says "around" the chosen time
HON-04No "AI", "pronunciation", "accent" or "fluency score" labels on deterministic outputHON-16No promises of exam scores, fluency or guaranteed improvement
HON-05Measured / Estimated labels present, as textHON-17Retired features leave no claims in strings or notes
HON-06Inconclusive instead of guesses; never counts as practiceHON-18Test and release claims are evidenced
HON-07No composite score for free speakingHON-19No unexecuted test recorded as passed
HON-08Confidence only when the recogniser supplied itHON-20"No issues found" never implies perfection
HON-09No trend before 3 valid attempts; noise band shownHON-21Every shown mistake has evidence
HON-10Counters change only with valid attempts and eventsHON-22Tips are never presented as AI
HON-11Every "How we measured this" includes its limitsHON-23Self-check is labelled self-reported and changes no metric
HON-12Privacy text matches behaviourHON-24Unvalidated metrics are not shown

Recommended as non-waivable: HON-01, HON-02, HON-12, HON-19 and HON-21. A failed honesty item is at least an S2 defect; these five are S1.

Every item is blocking. Any failure is an S1 defect: the item goes back to PENDING_REVIEW and is removed from the release, or the release is held.

TST-01 §9.1–9.2, §11.1.

16.9 Traceability and the critical-feature list

Automated tests carry their TC ID in the method name (tcEval003_…) or a KDoc tag. A traceability script (recommended; no new dependency) reads the TC IDs defined in FS-01…12, SEC-01 §13, DES-07 §15, RD-15 and TST-01 §16, finds them in the test sources and the rules suite, reports test cases that are defined but not implemented, implemented but not defined, and duplicated, and sets Passed or Failed from the CI run's JUnit XML with the run URL. The report is attached to every G5 and G6 pack. A release exits only when every P0 requirement in it has at least one Passed acceptance test, every SRS-FR has at least one Passed test, every SRS-BR has boundary tests, and every NFR has a Passed or Product-Owner-waived test.

R2 adds: Passed acceptance tests for every R2 requirement, a full R1 regression, migration tests for the R2 schema change, and — if transcript sync (D-16) is approved — its privacy review and HON-12 repeated.

Testing of a build is suspended when the smoke suite fails, an S1 defect blocks a core flow, the environment makes results unreliable, or the build under test is not the tagged RC; it resumes when the cause is fixed and the affected runs are repeated from the start.

TST-01 §10.1–10.4.

16.11 Severity, priority and the defect workflow

SeverityDefinitionExamplesDefault priority
S1 CriticalPrivacy or security breach, data loss, a fabricated result shown as real, crash on launch or in the core loop for a tier, legal/policy exposure, release-integrity failureAudio sent off-device without consent; recordings in a backup; a default score shown; a debug-signed build distributed; creator branding or a PENDING_REVIEW item shippedDP1 Immediate
S2 HighA core-loop capability broken for a tier, device class or segment, with no workaround; any honesty failure not covered by S1T2 devices never produce a transcript; Inconclusive counted as practice time; TalkBack cannot start a recordingDP2 Before release
S3 MediumFeature partly wrong with a workaround, or a non-core feature brokenSwap offers the same activity; text clipped at 200 % on a secondary screenDP3 Planned
S4 LowCosmetic, no functional impactSpacing off-token; typo in help textDP4 Backlog

Defect priority uses DP1…DP4 so it is never confused with requirement priority P0–P3. A failed TC-SEC or TC-PRIV case is at least S2; a failed TC-CONT case or content-compliance item is S1. Lowering an S1/S2 priority below DP2 needs Product Owner approval.

SeverityTriageFix targetRelease impact
S1≤ 1 working day (immediately during a rollout)≤ 48 h † for released builds; before any further release otherwiseHalt rollout / block release
S2≤ 2 working days≤ 7 days † for released builds; before release exit otherwiseBlocks release exit unless waived
S3≤ 5 working daysNext planned releaseListed in the release report; accepted at G6
S4Next triage sessionBacklogNone

Service levels are Proposed — requires approval. Triage happens at least twice a week during development and daily during an RC or beta period. Defects are tracked as GitHub Issues with labels for severity, priority, area (TC prefix), tier and release (recommended; OQ-TST-4). Each report contains the build tag and SHA, the device record and tier, preconditions and data set, steps, expected result with its requirement or TC, actual result, evidence (screenshot, recording, PII-checked log), frequency and workaround. A defect found outside any test case gets a new or updated test case before it closes, and every release report includes an escape analysis of defects found in beta that RC testing missed.

The 49 security cases (TC-SEC) cover the no-account core loop and network silence, sign-out and deletion, release artefact checks, classpath and manifest checks, backup rules, consent gating, export, profile isolation, the full rules emulator suite (TC-SEC-020…038), log redaction, cleartext blocking, secret scanning, workflow hardening and the manual RC security review. The 39 accessibility cases (TC-A11Y) cover token contrast, semantics of every key component, font scales up to 2.0, touch targets, reduced motion, announcements, the TalkBack core-loop script, keyboard and switch access, the Accessibility Scanner sweep and a per-release accessibility pack.

16.13 Ownership and risks to testing

The coding agent operates every testing role except the Product Owner and the beta testers, prepares all test artefacts and never approves its own work. The Product Owner approves TST-01 itself (G4), waivers, S1/S2 deferrals, target re-baselines, new test dependencies, the content-compliance sign-off (TC-CONT-006 and the §16.8 checklist, including ownership of the brand deny-list), the UAT report (G6) and the release (G7).

IDRisk to testingMitigation
TR-01Reference devices for a class are not availableSprint 0 inventory; borrow/buy decision; deviations recorded; emulators labelled "functional only"
TR-02 · 03Recognisers are non-deterministic and missing on emulatorsTranscript fixtures for JVM tests; tolerances and recorded recogniser versions; tier claims only from physical devices
TR-04No nightly CI (cost, flakiness)Device-lab runs before each G5 and each RC, reported as "device-lab run", never as CI
TR-07The same agent writes the code and the testsAssertion rules; goldens and corpus reviewed in PRs; Product Owner reviews evidence at G5/G6; escape analysis
TR-08room-testing / work-testing not approved with ADR-002/005Robolectric-based schema tests and manual worker tests; reduced coverage flagged at G4
TR-19 · 20Own-words compliance cannot be fully automated; the deny-list may miss variants or imagesSigned manual spot check; overlap proxy; Product Owner owns the deny-list; image review in TC-CONT-006/008
TR-21Mistake goldens depend on bands not yet defined for every metricGoldens for defined bands first; regenerated under a version bump once bands are approved

TST-01 §13, §15 (selected risks).

16.14 Test report template

Three kinds of report will exist in Stage B. All follow the same rule: a test without a CI run or a test-run record is reported as Not run, never as passed.

SectionEpic test report (G5)Release-candidate test report (G6)
1 ScopeEpic ID, stories and FS acceptance criteria in scope; feature-flag stateRelease (R0, R1, R2), epics included, requirements in scope, known exclusions and waivers requested
2 BuildCommit SHA(s) and PR linksTag, commit SHA, APK/AAB SHA-256, signing-certificate fingerprint, evaluationVersion, content packVersion
3 EnvironmentCI runner and image; emulator images; device-lab devices if usedDevice records per §16.5 (model, Android version and patch, fingerprint, RAM, recogniser package and version, language packs, tier detected); Firebase environment

7 Coverage against the RTMGOV-01 rows for the epic with TC status and evidence linksCF-01…CF-19 status; every P0 requirement with ≥ 1 Passed test; every NFR with a Passed or waived test

Test-run record (TRR) — manual and device runs

TRR id; build tag, commit SHA and APK SHA-256; environment and device record; tester role; date; test cases run with status; actual results; evidence links (screenshots, screen recordings, Perfetto traces, PII-checked logcat excerpts); defects raised; deviations from the script. Release and RC evidence is kept 90 days and copied to the offline release archive.

UAT report — signed at G6

Build tag and SHA; device list with tiers; results of UAT-01…UAT-10 with evidence; beta duration, active testers and KPI-01…06 and KPI-08 values with their denominators; open defects by severity; honesty checklist result; accessibility and privacy results; waivers requested. The QA Architect role prepares it and does not approve its own work.

TST-01 §3.4.3, §8.4, §14.5; PLN-05 §8; ENG-01 §8.3 item 8.

Chapter 17CI/CD and DevOps

The pipeline is the first thing R0 changes, because today it is the riskiest part of the project: every push to main publishes a release, and no test runs before it does. The target design puts required quality gates in front of every merge and makes releases tag-driven, release-signed and approved by a person.

Deliverable status — Deployment pipeline Designed, not yet built. Not yet produced — created in Stage B after approval (gate G4). The current workflow (release_on_push.yml) auto-releases on every push and runs no tests; when no release keystore secret is set, beta builds fall back to debug-signed, debuggable APKs (finding FND-SEC-02, issue ISS-02). Nothing in this chapter has been built, run or deployed, and no GitHub, Play Console or Firebase setting was inspected online; their current state is unknown unless visible in the repository. The YAML in ENG-01 is illustrative and untested.

How it will be produced. Through EP-01 (Chapter 13) and the ENG-01 work items ENG-T-01…28, starting with immediate containment (ENG-T-26: make the current workflow manual-only or require the release keystore) right after approval.

Acceptance. The R0 verification of GAP-01, GAP-30 and GAP-31 (TST-01 §10.3): branch-protection settings evidence and a deliberately failing PR shown as blocked; TC-SEC-009 and TC-SEC-010 passing on a signed R0 artefact; wrapper validation (TC-SEC-046) and a clean-checkout CI run.

ENG-01 evidence rule, §1, §12; PLN-04 ISS-02; TST-01 §10.3.

Common settings: permissions: contents: read; concurrency per ref with cancel-in-progress; runner pinned to an explicit image label (for example ubuntu-24.04); job timeout 30 minutes (Proposed); reports uploaded even on failure. Branch protection for main: pull request required, direct pushes blocked (admins included), the four checks required with the branch up to date, linear history, no force pushes; tags v* created only by maintainers; CODEOWNERS for workflows, rules, Gradle files, the manifest and backup rules. With a solo developer the PR requirement and required checks stay and self-merge is allowed (ED-08).

Test hygiene: no automatic retries for JVM tests; an instrumented test may retry once and a pass on retry is reported as flaky and ticketed; quarantined tests still run but are excluded from the required check (at most 5 % of a suite, fixed or deleted within 14 days, never a critical-feature test at release exit); the JVM suite targets ≤ 10 minutes. Each PR job summary shows tests run, passed, failed and skipped, coverage, golden diffs and the rule-precision table.

CI automated tests never use the dev project: they use the Firestore emulator with a demo- project id. Feature flags are declared once per build type (§15.7). The applicationId decision (D-15) matters here: a new applicationId is a new app, so existing installs could not update in place and local legacy data would be unreachable; it should be decided before any Play upload and before R1 beta distribution, so the identity changes at most once.

Local development (documented in CONTRIBUTING.md in Stage B): JDK 21, Android Studio and SDK 36; ./gradlew assembleDebug testDebugUnitTest lintDebug works with no Firebase file (option C of ED-03, recommended); dev-project access and a personal debug-key fingerprint are optional, only for sign-in and sync; rules tests run with npm ci and the Firestore emulator.

Handling rules: secrets are passed through env:, never interpolated into scripts or printed; key material is decoded into $RUNNER_TEMP with chmod 600 and deleted in an always() step; there are no secrets in ci.yml and pull_request_target is never used; a rotation log (name, date, who, reason) is kept in the vault; when someone leaves, everything they could access is rotated within 7 days (Proposed). The policy itself is SEC-01 §8.

17.6 Signing and key custody

KeyCustodianStorage and backupCI access
App-signing key (K_app)Release Manager; Product Owner as secondaryPassword-manager vault; encrypted offline copies in two separate places; restore tested once a year (Proposed)Interim only, through release secrets; removed from CI after Play onboarding
Upload key (K_upload)Release ManagerVault with backuprelease secrets
Developer debug keysEach developerAGP default locationNever; registered in the dev project only

Recommended strategy (ED-04 / D-28): a project-owned K_app signs GitHub builds now and is uploaded to Play App Signing at Play onboarding, so GitHub-installed builds can later update to Play builds without reinstalling (to be verified at onboarding). Existing debug-signed installs must reinstall in every option because the certificate changes. The K_app certificate SHA-256 is published in the README and every release note so that people who install manually can check it with apksigner verify --print-certs. Release builds fail if signing variables are missing.

ENG-01 §5.

17.7 Versioning

version.properties at the repository root holds only versionName (SemVer, optional -beta.N). Gradle computes versionCode = MAJOR × 1,000,000 + MINOR × 10,000 + PATCH × 100 + B, where B is N for a beta and 99 for a final release. A release PR bumps the file and adds the CHANGELOG section; the tag is created on that merge commit and release.yml asserts tag = file. A versionCode is never reused.

versionNameversionCodeversionNameversionCode
1.2.0-beta.11,020,0011.2.01,020,099
1.2.0-beta.71,020,0071.2.11,020,199

A final release is always greater than its betas, and the scheme is greater than any legacy code (2 + run number), so in-place upgrades from release-signed legacy installs stay possible if D-15 keeps the applicationId. Constraints: MINOR and PATCH ≤ 99. The override properties, the GitHub-repository build field and the test that asserts fixed version values are removed; a unit test of the formula replaces them (ED-07).

ENG-01 §7.

17.9 Monitoring and diagnostics

PhaseMechanismNotes
R0/R1 interim betaLocal, PII-free diagnostic log with an uncaught-exception recorder; Settings → About → "Share diagnostics" with a preview; tester feedback channelNo SDK; nothing is sent automatically (ADR-007, D-13)
Play tracksPlay Console Android vitals: crash rate, ANR rate, stack traces; R8 mapping uploaded with each AABNo SDK
Only if the above prove insufficientFirebase Crashlytics, opt-in and PII-scrubbedNew dependency — requires approval (ADR); data-safety update

All logging goes through AppLogger; release builds emit WARN and ERROR only, redacted, and CI enforces that no raw Log call exists (TC-SEC-041). There is no analytics SDK in R1: product success is measured through a structured beta programme, in-app "Was this accurate?" ratings stored locally and a user-initiated "Share my beta summary" with aggregate counts only (ADR-013 option A, decision D-09). Opt-in analytics is to be revisited before any public store launch, under a new approval. TC-PRIV-009 checks that no analytics or crash-reporting SDK is on the release classpath. Operational items — GCP budget and usage alerts, Firebase project hygiene and point-in-time recovery (ED-10) — are listed in Chapter 18.

Chapter 18Release and deployment

A release reaches people only after two explicit approvals: the Product Owner signs the QA and UAT results (G6) and then gives the release instruction (G7). Until a Google Play account exists, releases are release-signed APKs published as GitHub Releases, with no in-app installer.

Deliverable status — Production release None. Not yet produced — created in Stage B after approval (gate G4); each release is produced only after gates G6 and G7. No release of the redeveloped product exists. The GitHub releases published so far came from the current auto-release workflow (Chapter 17) and belong to the legacy app; some may be debug-signed.

How it will be produced. A release candidate is tagged, tested on the device matrix, checked against the honesty and content-compliance checklists, put through beta and UAT, signed at G6, checked against the 19-item release checklist, approved at G7, and published from the draft GitHub Release (or the Play track the Product Owner chooses under D-03).

What it will contain. A release-signed APK (and an AAB for Play), SHA256SUMS, the signing-certificate fingerprint, release notes generated from the CHANGELOG with install and verification instructions, and an archived evidence pack (CI runs, RC test report, UAT report, checklist).

Acceptance. PLN-05 GR-09: no release without G6 and G7 and a release-signed build from CI.

PLN-05 §3–4; ENG-01 §1.1, §4.4, §8; ADR-004.

18.1 Release channels

ChannelAudienceArtefactDistributionWhen

The channel decision is D-03 (Pending): the recommendation is Play testing tracks once the account exists and, until then, release-signed GitHub downloads without any in-app installer (ADR-004 option B). Play policy does not allow an app to update itself outside Play, so the existing self-updater is removed in R0. If the Play account is delayed (RSK-10), the GitHub channel continues; the data-safety form and the account-deletion page are prepared early.

ENG-01 §8.1; ADR-004; RD-99 §8 D-03; PLN-04 RSK-10, DEP-03.

18.5 Rollback plan

Android cannot downgrade an installed app, and Room migrations are forward-only, so "rollback" mostly means stopping the spread of a bad build and fixing forward quickly.

LayerStrategy
Android appPlay: halt the rollout, then ship a fix-forward hotfix or a "rollback build" — the previous good code rebuilt with a higher versionCode. GitHub interim: mark the release "Do not install" in its notes, remove the asset or revert it to prerelease, and publish the hotfix.
Firestore schemaChanges within a schema version are additive only; a breaking change bumps schemaVersion and rules accept both versions for at least two releases; fields that supported versions read are never renamed or removed; rules accepting old and new are deployed first, then the app; a client that sees newer data than it understands stops uploading and asks the user to update.
Firestore rulesKeep the previous ruleset id; roll back by redeploying the previous tagged firestore.rules. A rules rollback must not re-open retired legacy paths.
RoomMigrations are never destructive; downgrade is not supported, so rollback means fix forward.
Feature flagsCompile-time, so disabling a shipped feature needs a new release. Mitigation: ship risky features off in release and on in beta first. A remote kill switch would need Remote Config — a new dependency and an ADR — and is not proposed.

Hotfix process

  1. Open an incident issue with the severity (S1: privacy leak, data loss, crash on launch, security vulnerability, sign-in broken for everyone — fix released within 48 h †, rollout halted at once; S2: a core-loop feature broken for a segment — next patch within 7 days †; S3: normal release train).
  2. Halt the rollout or mark the GitHub release.
  3. Fix on a branch from main; if main contains unreleased risky work, branch hotfix/x.y.(z+1) from the release tag and cherry-pick back afterwards.
  4. Open a PR with the same required checks — no bypass.
  5. Bump PATCH and update the CHANGELOG.
  6. Tag, run release.yml, get environment approval.
  7. Write a post-incident note within 5 working days: cause, detection, prevention, test added.

ENG-01 §8.4–8.5; ARC-03 §9.7.

Chapter 19Maintenance and continuous improvement

After the first beta, SpeakCraft has to stay honest as devices, recognisers, content and dependencies change. This chapter describes how defects are handled, what is watched, how performance and storage are kept within budget, how user feedback flows back into the rules and the content, and how releases and KPIs are reviewed.

Status Nothing has been released, so nothing is being maintained yet. This chapter describes the operating model that the Stage A documents define for the beta period and after R1. Every service level, threshold and target in it is Proposed — requires approval.

19.1 Bug triage and service levels

SeverityExampleTriageFix target †Release impact
S1Audio or transcript sent off-device without consent; data loss; crash on launch; debug-signed build distributed; creator branding shipped≤ 1 working day; immediately during a rollout≤ 48 h for released builds (hotfix)Halt rollout; block release; post-incident note within 5 working days
S2A core-loop feature broken for one tier or OEM; any honesty failure not covered by S1≤ 2 working daysNext patch ≤ 7 daysBlocks release exit unless the Product Owner waives it
S3Partly wrong with a workaround; non-core feature broken≤ 5 working daysNext planned releaseListed and accepted at G6
S4CosmeticNext triage sessionBacklogNone

Intake. Tester reports through the beta channel and feedback form, shared diagnostics, the user-initiated beta summary, Play vitals once on Play, and defects found by tests. Beta reports are transcribed into GitHub Issues by the QA Architect role with the tester's consent and without personal data. Cadence. Triage at least twice a week during development and daily during an RC or beta period. Rules. S1 is never deferred; deferring an S2 or lowering an S1/S2 below DP2 needs the Product Owner; every fixed S1/S2 gets an automated regression test where technically possible; every release report includes an escape analysis.

TST-01 §11–12; ENG-01 §8.5.

Nothing is collected automatically: there is no analytics or crash-reporting SDK in R1 (ADR-007, ADR-013). That keeps the privacy promise, but it means field monitoring depends on testers sharing diagnostics and summaries, and on Play vitals once the app is on Play (RSK-07).

A performance regression found at an RC is a defect like any other. Storage is also controlled by the retention options (delete after feedback, 7 days, 30 days, keep until deleted), the daily CleanupWorker, and immediate deletion of discarded or failed recordings.
