19.5 Content updates

Content ships only inside app releases: the pack is bundled and validated at build time, there is no Remote Config, and side-loaded packs are not supported in R1–R2. Each content change follows the same path as the initial work.

StepRule
Author or reviseThe seven-step CNT-01 workflow for creator-derived items (find the specific video, confirm technique and timestamp, rewrite in own words, record provenance, set licenceStatus, link exercises and tips, second review); new original content uses licenceStatus = ORIGINAL
VersionpackVersion increases by 1 for any content change and is stored as content_version on attempts, plans and saved activities (TST-01 calls this the contentVersion). Old pack versions are not shipped; retired items stay in the pack and ids are never reused, so history stays readable
TestThe validator (V-01…V-22) runs on every build; tip changes need new tip goldens under the new content version (TC-REG-024); TC-MIG-012 covers a content-version change; TC-CONT gates run on the release commit and the signed APK
ReleaseThe content-compliance checklist (CC-01…07), with the own-words spot check signed by the Product Owner

Broken source-video links

A source video can be removed or moved after an item ships. The existing rules already cover this case: the link-out is checked on a device at every RC (TC-CONT-007, CC-05), and the own-words spot check opens each sampled link at its timestamp (TC-CONT-006). A link that no longer opens the specific video fails the content-compliance checklist, which makes it an S1 item: it is set back to PENDING_REVIEW and removed from the release, or the release is held. The fix repeats CNT-01 steps 1, 2 and 4 (find the video, confirm the timestamp, update provenance with a new verifiedOn), takes a second review, bumps packVersion and ships in the next release. If no specific video can be found, "no match, no ship" applies.

Growing the curriculum

R1 ships the verified existing items plus the highest-value techniques (about 15 †, D-36). Further channel videos are catalogued with the CNT-01 template and released as content updates in R2 (T-09.8, EP-23: +10 activities per track, +300 words), so the Product Owner's aim of covering all techniques is reached in steps rather than squeezed into R1 (RSK-17).

ARC-03 §6.11–6.14; CNT-01 §6; TST-01 §6.5, §9.2, §16.8; PLN-02 EP-23; RD-99 §8 D-36.

19.6 Evaluation-rule updates

  • Every change is versioned. evaluationVersion is the semantic version of the rule set and metric definitions, stored with every attempt and listed in a changelog. Thresholds live in EvaluationConfig, versioned with it.
  • Goldens decide. A change that alters any golden output needs a new evaluationVersion, a changelog entry, a new golden folder and reviewer sign-off; CI fails a PR that changes the current goldens without a bump. Previous goldens are kept so behaviour can be compared between versions.
  • New rules start as candidates. A grammar rule ships only after it passes the precision gate (≥ 0.90 † on ≥ 30 positive and ≥ 30 negative examples); the corpus grows with recogniser-captured examples, and a rule that later fails is demoted.
  • New metrics need validation and approval. The candidate acoustic metrics (pitch range, volume variation) stay hidden until SPK-01 validates them on the reference devices and the Product Owner approves them; they then enter the fixtures and goldens under a new version.
  • No model updates. Feedback, issues and tips stay deterministic functions of stored inputs; a generative or cloud model would need a new ADR, a privacy review and the Product Owner's authorisation (NG-01).

ARC-05 §10, §12; TST-01 §7.1–7.6; ARC-02 §9.4, §11.5.

19.7 Dependency currency

ADR-006 (Proposed) sets the policy: upgrade Compose BOM, lifecycle, navigation and activity to current stable versions verified compatible with the toolchain at implementation time, each in its own PR with a full test run, recording exact versions in the PR rather than prescribing them in advance; remove unused catalogue entries; fix the JUnit reference; keep the App Check debug artefact out of release. After R1 the same pattern continues: Dependabot proposes weekly grouped updates for Actions, Gradle and npm; the dependency allow-list check (TC-REG-011) blocks any unapproved or unused dependency; the release checklist blocks open critical or high alerts without a waiver (TC-SEC-047). Any new dependency, SDK or service — including tooling named as optional in the sources — requires approval through an ADR (PLN-05 GR-04).

ADR-006; ENG-01 §11; TST-01 §16.5; SEC-01 §13.

19.8 Release cadence and KPI review

No calendar cadence is committed. Releases are tag-driven: S1 fixes ship as hotfixes, S2 fixes in the next patch, S3 fixes on the normal release train, and feature releases follow the roadmap (R2 indicatively about five sprints after R1). Each release report carries a test summary, the checklist and — during beta — the KPIs; the R1 exit needs KPI-01…06 with their denominators; R2 starts only after the R1 KPIs are reviewed and the R2 scope is re-confirmed at G1.

KPINameTarget †Release
KPI-01Activation — new users completing ≥ 1 valid attempt in the first session≥ 70 %R1 beta
KPI-02Time to first valid attempt (median)≤ 3 minR1
KPI-03Practice days per week in the first 4 weeks≥ 3R1

KPI-09Privacy incidents; data-rights test pass rate0; 100 %R1

KPI-11Duplicate engines per concern; user-facing screens1 per concern; ≤ 30 screensR1
KPI-12Unlicensed third-party content items; policy-violating mechanisms0; 0R0

Two KPI points need attention at review time. KPI-11 sets "≤ 30 screens" while the screen inventory lists 38 R1 screens, so the definition of a user-facing screen should be agreed before R1 is measured against it. And because there is no analytics SDK, every user KPI depends on beta summaries; a cohort below 10 active testers is the RSK-07 trigger, and the release report must always state the denominator.

BRD §8; PLN-01 §2; PLN-05 §8; TST-01 §10.3; GOV-01 §3 (screen count); PLN-04 RSK-07.

19.9 Keeping documents and traceability current

The documents remain the source of truth after launch. Behaviour changes update the authoritative document and GOV-01 in the same PR (GR-07); each G5 adds the implementing PR and CI run to the matrix; the RAID log stays a living document; technology changes go through ADRs; scope changes go through a PRD change approved by the Product Owner; changes to principles or non-goals are recorded decisions (GR-06). Retired IDs are marked Withdrawn and never reused.

PLN-05 §4; GOV-01 §2; GOV-02 §7.

Part V

Future evolution

How SpeakCraft could add artificial intelligence later — optionally, on the device first, and without weakening the two principles the product is built on: honest feedback and private by default. This part is a design for decision, not a plan. Releases R1 and R2 ship with no AI.

Chapter 20Future AI implementation (design)

The Product Owner asked for a system design for future AI. This chapter summarises it. The design adds an optional AI layer after the deterministic feedback pipeline. That layer can only add a separate, clearly labelled suggestion. It never changes a metric, a mistake found, a coach tip, a pass mark, a streak or progress. It starts on the device, and it uses the cloud only after a separate consent, a privacy review and two further decisions.

Status: proposed for R3+ — not in R1/R2; the app works fully without AI Everything in this chapter is Proposed and R3+ conditional. It becomes eligible for planning only after the new decisions D-37…D-41 are recorded, ADR-016 is approved, a privacy review under SEC-01 §9.7 is completed and the data-safety inputs are updated. D-04 still recommends removing cloud AI from R1 (ADR-003), and the non-goals NG-01 (cloud AI or LLM generation), NG-02 (AI tutor or chatbot) and NG-03 (phoneme scoring) stay in force for R1 and R2. Every new dependency named here needs its own ADR. Values marked † are placeholders to be tuned by spikes.

ARC-06 v0.1 header and §1; ADR-016; ADR-003; RD-16 §4; Stage A brief ("Do not make large LLMs mandatory"; "Cloud AI must not be introduced without explicit product authorization, privacy review and a secure implementation design").

20.1 What this design is, and what it is not

ADR-003 removes the dormant cloud-AI code from the current app and says that any future AI feature needs a new ADR, a privacy review and explicit authorisation. ARC-06 is the secure implementation design that such an authorisation would review. It answers three questions: which AI features would be worth having, how they could be added without breaking honesty or privacy, and what cheap preparations R1 and R2 could make so that adding AI later is not a rebuild.

ARC-06 is

  • A conditional target architecture for R3 and later
  • An optional augmentation layer after the deterministic pipeline
  • Advisory text labelled "AI suggestion" that the user can ignore
  • On-device first; cloud only after consent, review and D-38/D-39
  • The owner of proposed IDs until other documents adopt them

ARC-06 is not

  • A plan, an R1/R2 epic or a commitment
  • A replacement for ARC-05 local intelligence
  • A source of metrics, scores, pass/fail, streaks or progress
  • A mandatory large-language-model dependency
  • A change to any existing document (amendments follow approval)

ARC-06 §1.2, §1.4.

Binding constraints

#ConstraintSourceHow the design enforces it
C-1The app works fully without AI; the default provider is "no AI"; every feature falls back to the ARC-05 outputBrief; PP-6NoAiProvider default; result rendered before any AI call
C-2No large LLM is mandatoryBriefNo core-loop step depends on a model beyond the ADR-009 recogniser
C-3AI never produces or changes metrics, scores, pass/fail, streaks or progressPP-1, D-02, D-06No write path; import rule forbids evaluation code from using the AI package
C-4AI output is always labelled "AI suggestion", says it may be wrong, and is ratedPP-1; DES-05 §12.3Separate card with badge, caveat and "Was this accurate?"
C-5Every quote must exist in the user's transcriptPP-1Mechanical grounding check; output dropped on failure
C-6Audio never leaves the device for AIPP-2No provider accepts audio
C-7Cloud receives minimised text only, after separate per-feature consent and a D-25-style disclosureSEC-01 §7.3, §9.6Allow-listed prompt slots; consent sheet CA-1…CA-13
C-8No personal identifiers; no training on user dataPP-2Payload tests; provider terms checklist PT-1
C-9No AI in no-account mode unless the user opts inSEC-REQ-02; D-05D-41 recommendation: on-device after opt-in; no cloud
C-10Never use AI to copy creator transcripts (D-08)D-08 DecidedInternal drafting aid from the Product Owner's own notes only; human verification
C-11No API keys in the appSEC-01 §8.1Firebase AI Logic with enforced App Check, or an approved proxy
C-12Threat model with schema output, validation, quotas, kill switch, rate limits, PII-free logsBriefTHR-23…38; AI-SEC-01…23
C-13Each new dependency needs its own ADRPO-REQ-1120 §2Dependency table in §20.5

ARC-06 §1.3.

Principles AI-P1…AI-P10

The ten AI principles rank below the product principles PP-1…PP-8. When an AI idea conflicts with a product principle, the AI idea loses.

IDPrincipleRule in one line
AI-P1OptionalEvery feature off by default and individually switchable; the default provider is "no AI"
AI-P2HonestAdvisory text only; no numbers, scores, levels or measurement claims the pipeline did not produce
AI-P3PrivateNo audio to any provider; on-device first; cloud only signed in and consented
AI-P4Deterministic fallbackAny failure leaves the result exactly as ARC-05 defines it
AI-P5LabelledOnly in the "AI suggestion" card; deterministic features are never called AI
AI-P6GroundedQuotes exist in the transcript; issues are ones the checks raised; ungrounded output is dropped, never repaired
AI-P7MinimisedSmallest input that works; redaction of e-mail, phone and long digit strings
AI-P8ConsentedOpt-in on device; separate revocable consent per cloud feature
AI-P9MeasurableGolden sets, rubric, validator and grounding metrics, latency and battery budgets, beta ratings
AI-P10ReversibleA kill switch returns the app to "no AI" without data migration

ARC-06 §2.

20.2 Candidate use cases

Eight candidates were assessed for value, risk, feasibility on the device (OD) or in the cloud (CL), dependencies and effect on the non-goals. Three are recommended first, all on-device. One is worth having later in the cloud. Three are not recommended. The content-authoring assistant is a team process, not an app feature.

UCUse caseValueMain riskODCLNon-goal effectPriority
UC-AI-1Better transcripts with on-device ASR (ML Kit GenAI speech recognition, or a Whisper-class model; SPK-03)HighModel "corrects" grammar or drops "um", hiding real mistakes; locale gaps✔—None (ADR-009 option B)P1
UC-AI-2Coach-tip rewording and personalisation, grounded in the deterministic issue codesMediumMeaning drift; invented quotes✔✔NG-01 (R3 revision)P1
UC-AI-3High-recall grammar and word-choice suggestions, labelled "Possible", never counted in M-GRAMMedium–HighFalse positives✔✔NG-01P1
UC-AI-4Answer-content feedback: relevance, STAR/PREP depth, a better-phrasing exampleHighHallucination; privacy if cloud◐✔NG-01P2
UC-AI-5Dynamic role-play partnerMediumOpen-ended chat; safety; multi-turn not supported on-device✗✔Revises NG-02Not recommended
UC-AI-6Phoneme-level pronunciationMediumNeeds validated acoustic modelsResearchNG-03Research only
UC-AI-7Internal content-authoring assistant for D-08 re-expressionMediumCopying creator wordingTeam tool, outside the appNoneP2 (process)
UC-AI-8Plan personalisationLowOpaque, untestable plans✔✔—Keep deterministic

ARC-06 §3.1. ◐ = possible with the Prompt API (beta) on few devices.

Recommended order (D-40) UC-AI-1 first, inside a re-scoped SPK-03, because every metric and mistake depends on the transcript. Then UC-AI-2 and UC-AI-3 on the device. UC-AI-4 only after spike SPK-05, a privacy review and decisions D-38/D-39. UC-AI-7 may run as a team process after D-37, because it never ships in the app.

Why UC-AI-1 is not "AI judging the user"

Transcription is input, not judgement. The platform recogniser already plays that role (ADR-009). An on-device AI recogniser would plug into the existing recognition session as one more backend (CMP-92). It would feed the same evidence gate, metric engine and rule engine, which stay deterministic. Two conditions keep it honest. First, it must return verbatim recognition: any formatting or "clean-up" mode stays off, because a model that turns "he don't" into "he doesn't", or drops "um", would hide exactly the mistakes the coach exists to find. Second, the attempt records which engine produced the transcript (transcript_source), so a trend change caused by an engine switch can be recognised. SPK-03 therefore measures an error-preservation rate and a filler-retention rate as well as word-error rate. On 2026-11-21 the GenAI speech-recognition page listed en-US, but not en-GB, en-IN or en-AU, among its English locales. SpeakCraft offers all four, so this is risk RSK-27.

ARC-06 §3.2 UC-AI-1; ADR-009; VERIFIED on the ML Kit GenAI speech-recognition page, 2026-11-21 (alpha API; basic mode on API 31+, advanced mode on Gemini Nano devices).

UC-AI-3: "Possible" means possible

The deterministic rule engine ships only rules with precision of at least 0.90 (NFR-QUAL-003), so it misses many errors. An on-device proofreading model catches more, but with less certainty. The design keeps the two apart. The transcript is proofread, punctuation and capitalisation changes are discarded, and each remaining change becomes a "Possible wording" item in the AI card. These items never enter "Mistakes found", are never counted in M-GRAM, never raise ISSUE_GRAMMAR_POSSIBLE and never affect word-stage 4. Where a deterministic rule already flagged the same span, the deterministic item wins and the AI item is removed.

ARC-06 §3.2 UC-AI-3; ARC-05 §8.1, §9.

20.3 Target architecture

The existing pipeline runs first and completely. Its EvaluationResult.Valid is saved and the result screen is rendered before any AI work starts. The AI layer receives a read-only copy through a seam called FeedbackEnhancer. In R1 and R2 that seam is a no-op. The layer can do one thing only: add a separately labelled card. No arrow returns from the AI layer into the pipeline.

ARC-06 §4.1–§4.2; ARC-02 §3.2 pipeline order; ARC-05 §2.

Components

Sixteen components are proposed, numbered after ARC-02's last component (CMP-76). ARC-02 adopts them only after approval.

CMPComponentResponsibility
CMP-77AiCapabilityServicePer-feature availability from four separate facts: user choice, model status (unavailable · downloadable · downloading · available), device capability and runtime state. Never shows "ready" until verified
CMP-78AiPolicyGateThe only place that decides whether and where a call runs; re-checked before every call; forces on-device-only mode unless a valid cloud consent exists
CMP-79AiOrchestratorRuns enabled features in a fixed order with timeouts and cancellation; records usage; never writes ENT-01…18
CMP-80AiProvider + NoAiProviderOne interface for every backend: single turn, schema-constrained JSON, no tools. NoAiProvider is the default binding
CMP-81OnDeviceAiProviderML Kit GenAI through AICore (Rewriting, Proofreading, Prompt API); handles download, warm-up, BUSY, background and battery-quota errors
CMP-82HybridFirebaseAiProviderOption C only: Firebase AI Logic with enforced App Check and a pinned model
CMP-83ProxyAiProviderOption D, future: an approved backend proxy (the project's first backend)
CMP-84PromptBuilderVersioned templates shipped in the APK; allow-listed slots only; redaction; input fingerprint
CMP-85OutputValidatorSchema, grounding, number, issue, banned-term, safety, length and language rules; drop on failure
CMP-86AiSuggestionStoreENT-19 persistence, ratings and reports; deletion with every relevant wipe
CMP-87AiKillSwitchCompile-time flags, local switches with a known-good model list, and a remote or provider-side switch
CMP-88AiQuotaManagerPer-attempt, per-minute, per-day and per-month limits; reacts to provider quota errors
CMP-89FeedbackEnhancerThe R1/R2 hook: a seam called after a valid result is saved; a no-op until AI is approved
CMP-90PurposeConsentStoreThe T3a consent store generalised to one record per purpose (online transcription; each cloud AI feature)
CMP-91AI card + AI settingsThe labelled card under Coach tips and the "AI suggestions" settings sub-screen
CMP-92AiTranscriptionBackendUC-AI-1 recogniser backend behind the existing recognition factory; verbatim output; no network path

ARC-06 §4.2–§4.3 (Kotlin signatures are illustrative and PROPOSED).

A package rule makes constraint C-3 structural rather than procedural. The new app.speakcraft.ai package may read the domain model, but the domain, analysis, rules, feedback and repository packages may never import it. The existing CI import check (WBS T-02.2.3) enforces this, and test TC-AI-010 verifies it. With every AI flag off, the code shrinker can remove the AI package entirely.

20.4 Provider chain and fallback

For each call the policy gate walks a fixed chain. "No AI" is the starting point and the default. On-device AI is tried only if the user opted in and the model is ready. The cloud is reached only in option C, only for a signed-in user who consented to that specific feature, within quota and with an attested app. Whatever path is taken, the output passes the validator or is dropped. In every case the deterministic result is already on the screen.

ARC-06 §4.4, §4.5, AI-SEC-04; Firebase AI Logic hybrid inference modes VERIFIED on the get-started page, 2026-11-21.

Cloud access path (decision D-39)

PathHowForAgainstStatus
Firebase AI Logic + enforced App CheckClient SDK → Firebase AI Logic → Gemini; App Check token with Play Integrity; no provider key in the appNo backend to run; App Check is set per service; fits the stackRe-adds a cloud-AI SDK (amends SEC-REQ-28, TC-SEC-010, NFR-SEC-006); hybrid inference is experimental with no SLA; sideloaded builds cannot attest, so cloud AI needs Play distribution (D-03)Recommended if cloud
Approved backend proxyClient → SpeakCraft function holding credentials → model providerFull server-side control of quotas, redaction and provider choiceThe project's first backend (ARC-04 P-1): new ADR, threat model, CI/CD, on-call, costFuture option

20.5 Honesty mechanics: how the validator keeps AI truthful

Prompt instructions ask the model to be honest, but the design does not trust them. Every output is checked mechanically. If one rule fails, the whole output is dropped — never edited into shape.

RuleCheckExample rejected
SchemaOutput parses against the feature's JSON schema (for tip rewording: tipText ≤ 280 chars †, basedOnIssue, at most one quote)Free text, extra fields
GroundingAfter normalising case and punctuation, every quote is a contiguous run of the transcript's tokens; any quoted text outside the quote field is rejected"You said 'I basically managed it'" when the user said "I managed it"
NumbersAny digit or number word must already appear in the deterministic evidence shown to the user"You used 'um' 9 times" when the evidence said 7
IssuebasedOnIssue must be one of the issues the deterministic checks raisedInventing a pace problem the checks did not find
Banned termsscore, %, pronunciation, accent, fluency, band, level names, CEFR/IELTS/TOEFL, "perfect", "measured", "verified", creator names and borrowed framework names"Your fluency is now B2"
Safety, length, languageSafety lexicon; maximum length; English outputOffensive or off-topic text

ARC-06 §4.2 CMP-85, §4.3 grounding algorithm, §4.8 prompt rules.

20.6 Data model

The augmentation layer leaves the eighteen existing entities untouched; the attempt record stays immutable and deterministic. AI data goes into two new local entities in sensitive.db, which is excluded from backup. AI suggestions can quote the transcript, so they are treated like the transcript itself (class C1).

IDEntityHoldsSyncedBackup
ENT-19AiSuggestion (ai_suggestion)Feature, feedback_source (AI on-device or cloud), provider, ai_model_version as reported (never guessed), prompt template and version, evaluation and content version, input fingerprint, validation result, output text (only when passed), latency, rating and reportNeverExcluded
ENT-20AiUsageDay (ai_usage_day)Daily counters per feature and provider: requests, passes, rejections by reason, fallbacks, latency buckets, ratings, estimated tokensNeverExcluded
ENT-03 (+)Attempt — UC-AI-1 onlyTwo nullable provenance columns: transcript_source and recogniser_model_versionNoIncluded

Export. The ARC-03 export gains optional fields only, so formatVersion stays 1. Every deterministic feedback item carries source: "DETERMINISTIC", which makes the honesty boundary visible in the file. Passed AI suggestions are exported under aiSuggestions[] with their label, source, provider and model version. The "not included" list adds the exact prompts (only a fingerprint is kept) and rejected outputs (only the reason is kept).

Deletion. AI suggestions are deleted with their attempt, with the attempt's transcript (because they may quote it), by "Delete AI suggestions", by Clear history, by sign-out with "Remove from this device", and by account or local-data deletion. Turning a feature off offers to delete its past suggestions, and this option is on by default.

ARC-06 §5.1–§5.10; ARC-03 §3.0, §8.4, §10.7; SEC-01 §3.1, §6.4.

20.7 Privacy design

Use caseWhat is processedLeaves the device?To whomAccount mode
UC-AI-1 on-device ASRAudio → transcriptNo (model download only)AICore model delivery, or an approved model hostBoth, after opt-in
UC-AI-2/3, UC-AI-4 on-deviceTip text, issue codes, transcriptNo — "processed locally" (VERIFIED, ML Kit GenAI)—Both, after opt-in
UC-AI-4 cloud (option C)Activity prompt, redacted transcript text, framework, issue codesYes — text only, never audioGoogle via Firebase AI Logic and the Gemini API, under verified termsSigned-in only
UC-AI-7 authoringThe Product Owner's own notesNo app data involvedThe approved tool's providern/a

Never sent to any AI provider: audio in any form, account identifiers (uid, e-mail, name, device id), profile fields, history and other attempts, metric values (only bands and numbers already shown), file paths, consent records, location and advertising ID.

No-account mode (D-41 recommendation) On-device features are available after the user opts in, because they make no SpeakCraft network call and keep SEC-REQ-02 true. Cloud AI is not offered without an account, for four reasons: the no-account mode promises network silence; per-user quotas and abuse controls need an identity; a cloud call would also start Firebase traffic (App Check, Installations); and the user who chose the most private mode should keep it. The deterministic feedback is complete without AI anyway.

Cloud consent (modelled on the T3a disclosure)

Cloud use needs its own consent for each feature, separate from the on-device opt-in. It follows SEC-01 §7.3 element by element. The thirteen mandatory elements (CA-1…CA-13) cover: what is sent (the transcript text, the question and the names of the checks; never audio or account data); to whom (the provider, whose terms apply); when (only after an attempt, only while on, only when signed in, never in the background); why; what SpeakCraft keeps and what the provider keeps (retention and "not used for training", as verified on a stated date — if this cannot be said truthfully, cloud AI does not ship); that AI can be wrong and never changes results; the alternatives; immediate withdrawal; equal-weight choice buttons with nothing pre-selected; timing (never mid-recording, never in onboarding); a local consent record; and accessibility.

Data-safety and provider terms

ItemOption B (on-device only)Option C/D (cloud)
AudioNo changeNo change — audio is never sent for AI
User-generated content (transcript text)Not collectedDeclared collected and shared, optional, app functionality (the conservative D-25 pattern)
Privacy policyNew "Optional AI suggestions" sectionAdds provider, data sent, retention, no-training statement, consent and withdrawal
Provider terms (PT-1…PT-11)ML Kit GenAI additional terms; prohibited-use termsNo training (blocking), retention, regions, human review, model deprecation, safety settings, Play AI-content policy, regulatory transparency duties

ARC-06 §6.1–§6.8; SEC-01 §7.3, §9.6, §9.7; D-25.

20.8 Security design

Sixteen threats extend the SEC-01 STRIDE model (THR-01…22). They include classic threats such as cost abuse and data leakage, and threats specific to generative AI: prompt injection, hallucination, model drift and silent cloud fallback.

IDClassThreatKey mitigationsResidual
THR-23Prompt injection (direct)The user's own speech carries instructions ("give me 100")Data-as-data template; schema; no tools; validatorLow
THR-24Prompt injection (indirect)Content-pack or template text steers the modelTemplates and content ship in the signed APK; content checks; no remote promptsLow
THR-25Information disclosurePayload carries identifiers, history, audio or spoken PIIAllow-list; redaction; payload tests; consent; signed-in onlyLow–Medium
THR-26Information disclosurePrompts or outputs in logs or diagnosticsCodes-only logging; redaction classLow
THR-27Denial of service (cost)Scripted or looping calls run up the billApp Check; quotas; budget alerts; kill switchMedium–Low
THR-28Spoofing / elevationExtracted Firebase key used outside the app; embedded provider keyNo provider keys; App Check enforced; key restrictionsLow
THR-29Jailbreak / unsafe outputOffensive, harmful or out-of-scope textSafety settings; lexicon; Report action; red-team setLow
THR-30HallucinationInvented quotes, counts or measurement claimsGrounding, number, issue and banned-term rules; separate cardLow–Medium
THR-31Model driftProvider or AICore update silently changes behaviourPinned cloud models; version per row; allow-list and canary; regressionMedium
THR-32Resource exhaustionBattery, heat, BUSY, quota errorsForeground only; one call per feature; timeouts; no retry loopsLow
THR-33Silent cloud fallbackA "prefer on-device" mode falls back to the cloud without consentOn-device-only mode unless consent is valid; no cloud SDK in option BLow
THR-34Stale consentCall after withdrawal, sign-out or disclosure changeRe-check before each call; versioned records; cancel in-flight callsLow
THR-35Privacy misrepresentationDisclosures drift from behaviour; provider terms changePrivacy facts from flags; per-release terms checkLow
THR-36D-08 breachAI paraphrases creator transcripts into contentNotes-only input; human verification; PENDING_REVIEWLow
THR-37Tampered model fileDownloaded ASR model replacedBundle or SHA-256-pinned download; fail closedLow
THR-38RepudiationNo record of which model and prompt produced an outputENT-19 provenance fieldsLow

ARC-06 §7.1–§7.2; SEC-01 §11.

The threats map to twenty-three testable requirements, AI-SEC-01…AI-SEC-23. SEC-01 would adopt them as SEC-REQ-41 onward. The most important are: "no AI" is the default (01); no provider keys, and App Check enforced before any cloud build (02, 03); cloud-capable modes only with valid consent (04); allow-listed payloads (05) and never audio (06); schema output and mechanical validation (07, 08); no write or read path into evaluation, plan, progress or pass/fail code (09); labelling (10); per-feature consent (11); no cloud in no-account mode (12); quotas, kill switch and PII-free logging (13–15); pinned model versions (16); single turn with no tools (17); model-file integrity (19); and classpath control (20).

ControlOperating design
App CheckEnforced for Firebase AI Logic from the first cloud build, with Play Integrity. Enforcement is per service, so D-26's Firestore criteria need not block it
Quotas †1 call per feature per attempt; ≤ 4 per minute; ≤ 40 per day on-device and ≤ 20 per day in the cloud per user; monthly cloud cap; budget alerts at 50/90/100 %
Kill switch(1) compile-time FEATURE_AI_* flags; (2) local switches and a shipped known-good model list, with a three-prompt canary for unknown on-device model versions; (3) Remote Config (if its ADR is approved) or a provider-side switch for cloud features
LoggingCodes only (AI_REJECTED{feature, reason} and similar); prompts, transcripts and outputs are never logged

ARC-06 §7.3–§7.4.

20.9 User experience

TopicProposal
PlacementOne "AI suggestion" card directly below Coach tips, above the optional self-check and the actions. It never replaces, hides or reorders the deterministic sections. When AI is off, the card does not exist
Late arrivalA compact "Preparing an AI suggestion…" placeholder for at most 4 s † on-device or 8 s † in the cloud. Then the card appears, or one quiet line says it is not available this time. Nothing blocks the actions
Label and badge (DES-05)New badge variant "AI suggestion" with an outline and the sparkle icon, which is reserved for genuine AI only (DES-05 already forbids it on coach tips). Caveat line: "Made by an AI model on this phone. It can be wrong and doesn't change your results"

20.10 Quality, evaluation and rollout

AI is kept only if it measurably helps. Each feature has golden sets and a human rubric, and must pass explicit gates before it reaches the next audience.

Golden setContentSize †
GS-AI-TIPAttempts with deterministic issues and tips (UC-AI-2)≥ 200
GS-AI-LANGSPK-02 rule corpus plus annotated unpunctuated transcripts (UC-AI-3)≥ 300
GS-AI-CONTENTPrompt and answer pairs with relevance and depth labels (UC-AI-4)≥ 150
RT-AIRed-team transcripts: injection, score requests, offensive and personal content≥ 120
ASR-EPSPK-01 utterances plus error-bearing utterances (UC-AI-1)80
CheckTarget †
RubricFaithfulness, groundedness, helpfulness, tone, honesty and safety. Any groundedness, honesty or safety failure fails the item
Schema compliance / validator pass rate≥ 98 % / ≥ 85 % (below this, the call is not worth making)
Ungrounded quotes or scores shown to users0
UC-AI-3 precision of shown "Possible" items≥ 0.70 (deterministic rules keep ≥ 0.90)
UC-AI-1 error preservation≥ 90 %; filler retention at least the platform recogniser's
Latency p50 / p95On-device ≤ 1.5 s / ≤ 3 s; cloud ≤ 3 s / ≤ 6 s
Battery≤ 1 % per 10 attempts with all on-device features on

ARC-06 §9.5, §12.1, §12.6. AG gates are approved at G5 epic reviews (PLN-05).

GateAudienceEntryExit (all must hold)
AG-0Lab onlyD-37 approvedGolden-set targets, latency and battery budgets, device-coverage estimate; go/no-go recorded
AG-1Developer and Product Owner buildsADR-016 and dependency ADRs; SEC-01 §9.7 reviewAll applicable TC-AI pass; red-team 0 failures; fresh rubric sample passes

AG-3All capable devices, still default offG6/G7 release approvalMonthly review; automatic kill on a Sev-1 incident

ARC-06 proposes thirty-two test cases, TC-AI-001…TC-AI-032 Not run. Among them: core loop with all AI off; byte-identical results with and without AI; hybrid-mode gating; payload allow-list; grounding, number and banned-term rules; the separation import check; label placement; consent sheet; no-account network silence; timeout and BUSY fallback; kill-switch layers; quotas; logs; model-version canary; red-team; model-file integrity; classpath; export and deletion; UC-AI-3 isolation from M-GRAM; UC-AI-1 error preservation; and accessibility of the card. TST-01 adopts them after approval.

ARC-06 §9.1–§9.7.

20.11 Cost and operations

On-device inference has no per-call server cost (VERIFIED on the ML Kit GenAI page). Its costs are battery, model storage, device fragmentation and the churn of alpha and beta APIs. Cloud cost depends on tokens. The design states a formula, not prices, which are taken from the provider's price list at decision time.

Monthly cloud cost ≈ U × A × r × [ (T_in × P_in) + (T_out × P_out) ] + C_fixed

U  = signed-in users with the cloud feature consented      A = eligible valid attempts per user per month
r  = share of eligible attempts that call the cloud (≤ 1)  T_in ≈ template (~400 †) + transcript (~1.3 tokens/word †) + context (~50 †)
T_out ≤ max output tokens (~200 †)                          P_in, P_out = prices of the pinned model at D-39
C_fixed = proxy infrastructure (option D only) + monitoring

Upper bound per user per month = daily cloud quota (20 †) × 30 × [(T_in × P_in) + (T_out × P_out)]
Operations topicDesign
Monitoring without an analytics SDK (ADR-013)ENT-20 counters on each device, shared only in the user-initiated, previewed "Share my beta summary"; aggregate provider-console metrics (to verify); GCP budget alerts. No prompts, transcripts or outputs are ever collected centrally
Incident responseSev-1 (harmful output, ungrounded quote or score shown, adverse provider-terms change): kill the feature, fix the template or validator, add a regression test, re-enter at AG-1. Sev-2 (cost spike, model drift): lower quotas or remove the model version from the allow-list. Sev-3 (API deprecation): feature off until updated
Model updates and pinningCloud models pinned by explicit version name with no "latest" aliases. AICore updates on-device models outside the app, so the app records the reported version, checks the allow-list and runs a canary for unknown versions. Prompt templates are versioned in the APK; each change re-runs the golden sets

ARC-06 §10.1–§10.5.

20.12 Hooks R1 and R2 can include at near-zero cost

These preparations add no dependency, network call, data flow or visible behaviour, so they need no ADR, privacy review or D-37. Each still amends a design document, so it goes through that document's normal approval: G3 for ARC-02, ARC-03 and ARC-04, and G2 for DES-05. The Product Owner can decline them; the cost would then be a small refactor in R3.

HookWhatCost †Approval
H-1FeedbackEnhancer seam with a no-op binding (CMP-89)0.5 dARC-02 amendment (G3)
H-2Reserved enum values (FeedbackSource, TranscriptSource, ConsentPurpose); export carries source: "DETERMINISTIC"; no new tables or columns0.25 dARC-03 §11 amendment (G3)
H-3"AI suggestion" reserved, unused, in the DES-05 vocabulary0 dDES-05 amendment (G2)
H-4T3a consent store generalised to one record per purpose; T3a behaviour unchanged1 dARC-04 §5.20 and ARC-02 amendment (G3)
H-5evaluationVersion and contentVersion — already in the design0 dNone (existing)
H-6Import rule: evaluation packages may never import app.speakcraft.ai0.1 dARC-02 §2.2 amendment (G3)
H-7, H-8Privacy facts keyed by flag; log redaction by text class — already implied0 dNone (existing)

Deliberately not included in R1/R2: any ML Kit, Firebase AI or Remote Config dependency, FEATURE_AI_* flags, the ENT-19/20 tables and any AI string resource. Including them would conflict with ADR-003, SEC-REQ-28 and RD-16 change control.

20.13 Roadmap, spikes and the proposed epic

SpikeQuestionGo criteria †Size †
SPK-03 (re-scoped)Can an on-device AI recogniser (GenAI basic/advanced, or Whisper-class) improve transcripts without hiding errors?WER at least 3 points better than the platform recogniser; error preservation ≥ 90 %; no network traffic; locale coverage beyond en-US6–10 d
SPK-04Is on-device tip rewording and proofreading good, fast and widely available enough?Validator pass ≥ 85 %; rubric targets; latency and battery budgets; zero red-team failures shown5–8 d
SPK-05Is cloud content feedback accurate, safe, affordable and private?Rubric; "no training" and retention confirmed in writing; App Check enforced; cost bound within budget6–9 d

EP-28 "Optional AI augmentation (R3+, conditional)" Proposed has fifteen tasks (T-28.1…T-28.15). They cover decisions and ADRs, the privacy review, the spikes, the core layer, the on-device provider, prompts, the validator, storage and migration, UI, tests, disclosures, rollout, UC-AI-1 integration, the conditional cloud work and the authoring-assistant process. Preliminary totals †: about 37–53 days for option B, plus 6–9 days for UC-AI-1 and 10–15 days for option C. This competes with the other R3 epics (EP-25 store launch, EP-26 video self-review, EP-27 recognition research) and with single-developer capacity (RSK-06).

ARC-06 §12.2–§12.6; PLN-02 (EP-25…27).

20.15 Risks

IDRiskProb.ImpactMitigation
RSK-19AI suggestions wrong or ungrounded, eroding trust in the whole coachMediumHighGrounding and number rules; separate card; AG gates; kill switch
RSK-20Users read AI text as measured resultsMediumHighBadge and caveat; no numbers; placement; copy testing
RSK-21Few target-segment devices support AICore/Gemini NanoHighMediumHonest availability; complete deterministic path; coverage estimate before building
RSK-22Alpha, beta and experimental APIs change or disappearHighMediumOne provider interface; flags; no core dependency
RSK-23Cloud cost overrun or abuseMediumHighApp Check; signed-in only; quotas; budget alerts
RSK-24Provider terms or regulation changeMediumHighTerms check every release; disable on adverse change
RSK-25AI work displaces core-loop quality or R3 prioritiesHighHighNothing before the R3 decision point; spikes first
RSK-26Silent model driftMediumMediumVersion recording; allow-list and canary; pinned cloud models
RSK-27GenAI speech recognition lacks en-GB, en-IN and en-AU coverageHighMediumPer-locale SPK-03 tests; keep platform tiers per locale
RSK-28Store-policy non-compliance for AI-generated contentLowHighIn-app Report action; policy check before any AI release

ARC-06 §14 (proposed additions to the PLN-04 RAID log, which ends at RSK-18).

In one paragraph SpeakCraft can add AI later without becoming a different product. The deterministic coach stays the source of truth, and AI may only add a separate, labelled, grounded and rateable suggestion. The order is on-device first, starting with better transcripts, then reworded tips and "Possible" wording suggestions. Cloud content feedback comes only after a separate consent, a privacy review, verified provider terms and an attested Play build. Every step has a gate and a kill switch. Nothing changes in R1 or R2 except, if the Product Owner agrees, a few no-cost seams.

ARC-06 v0.1; ADR-016. External facts checked on 2026-11-21 at developers.google.com/ml-kit/genai, developers.google.com/ml-kit/genai/speech-recognition/android and firebase.google.com/docs/ai-logic/hybrid/android/get-started; also cited: developer.android.com/ai/hybrid. Re-verify at spike time.

Appendix AArchitecture decision records

Fifteen ADRs record the technology choices behind the redevelopment. None has been approved or implemented: each is Proposed — requires approval and is decided individually by the Product Owner at gate G3. Any technology change needs an approved ADR before implementation (PLN-05 GR-04).

ADRTitleKindLinked decisionStatusDecision proposed (one line)
ADR-001Retain the existing technology stack as the baselineConfirmation—ProposedKotlin, Compose + Material 3, Navigation Compose, coroutines/Flow, kotlinx-serialization, manual DI through an Application-owned container, DataStore, Firebase Auth (Google), Firestore and App Check stay fixed for Stage B
ADR-002Room as the local source of truth for learning dataNew dependency (declared in the catalogue, unused)D-11ProposedRoom becomes the single local store for learning data; DataStore stays for preferences; Firestore becomes a sync target; JSON-file stores are retired (TST-01 asks to add room-testing to its scope)
ADR-003Remove Firebase AI Logic (cloud AI) from the productRemovalD-04ProposedRemove firebase-ai, the cloud-AI services, settings keys and the unused interceptor; a build-level check proves no cloud-AI SDK is on the runtime classpath
ADR-004Distribution channel and the in-app APK updaterPlatform / productD-03ProposedRemove the self-updater, release collections and publish script; until Play exists, distribute release-signed APKs as GitHub Releases without any in-app installer; CI never ships debug builds
ADR-005WorkManager for reminders and background syncNew dependencyD-12ProposedA reminder worker and a unique, network-constrained sync worker; POST_NOTIFICATIONS requested on API 33+ only when reminders are turned on; adds work-runtime-ktx and work-testing
ADR-006Dependency currency upgrade and clean-upVersion upgrade / removal—ProposedUpgrade Compose BOM, lifecycle, navigation and activity in separate verified PRs; remove unused entries; fix the JUnit reference; commit and validate the wrapper; keep KSP only with ADR-002
ADR-007Crash and stability reportingPossible new dependencyD-13ProposedA PII-free local diagnostic log the user can share, plus Play vitals once on Play; Crashlytics only with a separate approval, opt-in and scrubbed
ADR-008App Check provider and debug-provider hardeningProvider change—ProposedRemove the launch-intent debug-token injection; debug artefact only in debug builds; Play Integrity once on Play, not enforced until then
ADR-009Speech capture and recognition strategyArchitecture (platform APIs)D-02, D-14ProposedThree capability tiers — T1 app-owned microphone piped to the on-device recogniser, T2 recogniser-owned microphone, T3 consented online (T3a) or audio-only (T3b) — validated by SPK-01 before the R1 build
ADR-010Defer video capture and automated body-language analysisScope / removal—ProposedRemove CameraX, MediaPipe and the landmark engines from R1–R2; reconsider in R3 as video self-review without automated scoring
ADR-011Remove YouTube Intelligence and the creator-derived content pipelineRemovalD-08 DecidedProposedRemove scraping, caption download, the Data API, shared and per-user collections and creator branding in R0; revised 2026-11-21 so the techniques are kept as curriculum, re-expressed in own words with links to their specific videos
ADR-012Package-by-feature structure inside the single :app moduleStructure— (applicationId: D-15)ProposedPackage-by-feature under app.speakcraft with enforced layering; revisit modularisation beyond about 60k lines or two contributors
ADR-013Product success measurement and analyticsPossible new dependencyD-09ProposedNo analytics SDK in R1: structured beta, local accuracy ratings and user-shared aggregate summaries; revisit strictly opt-in analytics before any public launch
ADR-014Sync and conflict-resolution modelArchitecture—ProposedLocal first with an outbox; immutable facts with client UUIDs; derived aggregates; last-writer-wins per document except vocabulary merge; audio and transcripts never sync by default
ADR-015Recording storage format and retentionArchitecture / privacyD-10ProposedRecordings under noBackupFilesDir per profile; WAV transcoded to AAC after evaluation (mandatory); 30-day default retention with user options; never uploaded
ADR-016Optional AI augmentation layer (future, R3+)Possible new dependencies — futureD-37…D-41Proposed R3+No AI by default; an optional on-device layer first, cloud only after a privacy review and D-38/D-39; never produces scores; see Chapter 20 and ARC-06. Not in R1/R2 scope

D-08 is the only decision taken so far (2026-11-21, "original wording, link out"). ADR-011 was revised to match it, but the ADR itself still awaits approval at G3 like the others.

Appendix BGlossary

Delivery and quality terms used in Parts III and IV. Product and architecture terms are defined in the first half of this book.

TermMeaning in SpeakCraft
Acceptance criterionA Given/When/Then statement that must hold for a story or feature to be accepted; IDs US-nnn ACn (stories) and AC-FSnn-nn (feature specifications)
ADRArchitecture Decision Record: the only route for a technology change; each is Proposed until the Product Owner approves it
Approval gate (G1–G7)A point where the Product Owner reviews named artefacts and records a decision: G1 product, G2 UX, G3 architecture and security, G4 implementation start, G5 epic review, G6 QA and UAT, G7 release
BlockedTest status: the test could not be executed; the reason is recorded
Brand deny-listThe list of creator names, handles and brand tokens, owned by the Product Owner, that the content gates scan for
Coach tipsShort, deterministic advice chosen from the content pack for each valid attempt; never presented as AI; each links to its technique and, where provenance exists, to the specific source video
Content packThe bundled, versioned JSON content (tracks, templates, activities, techniques, tips, words) validated at build time
contentVersion / packVersionThe content pack's monotonic version, increased for any content change and stored with each attempt; tip goldens are kept per content version
Critical feature (CF-nn)One of 19 features whose listed acceptance tests must all be Passed or waived before a release exits
Critical pathThe chain of dependent epics that sets the earliest R1 date: G4 → EP-05 → EP-06 → EP-07 → EP-08 → EP-11/EP-12 → EP-18
Decision (D-nn)A product, design or engineering choice listed in RD-99 §8 and recorded by the Product Owner as Approved, Rejected or Deferred
Definition of Done (DoD)The seven conditions in PLN-05 §6 that make a story or epic done, including tests alongside code, green CI, evidence, accessibility and honesty checks, and updated documents
Definition of Ready (DoR)The five conditions in PLN-03 §2 that let a story enter a sprint, including approved decisions and an estimate of 5 days or less
Device labThe physical reference devices used for tier, performance and manual tests; also the fallback when no nightly CI exists
Epic (EP-nn) and story epic (E-nn)A delivery epic is a schedulable body of work reviewed at G5; a story epic groups the user stories of one capability
evaluationVersionThe semantic version of the evaluation rules and metric definitions, stored with every attempt; any change to golden output needs a new version
Evidence gateThe first stage of evaluation, which decides from duration, voiced time, voiced ratio and word count (SRS-BR-001) whether an attempt carries enough evidence to be judged; otherwise the result is Inconclusive
Feature flagA compile-time BuildConfig boolean read through FeatureFlags; hides retired or unfinished features; never changes data shape
Golden fixtureA stored expected output (a full evaluation result, or the mistakes and tips) for a fixed input; tests compare exactly against it
Honesty checklist (HON-nn)The 24 checks run before every release that no screen shows a value, label or status not backed by evidence
InconclusiveThe honest outcome when an attempt lacks enough evidence; shows its reason with Retry/Skip and never counts as completion, failure, streak day or practice time
Issue code (ISSUE_*)An entry in the catalogue of detectable mistakes, such as ISSUE_PACE_FAST or ISSUE_GRAMMAR_POSSIBLE, versioned with evaluationVersion
K_app / K_uploadThe app-signing key and, once on Play, the separate upload key; held in a vault with two offline backups
licenceStatusContent field: ORIGINAL, ORIGINAL_REEXPRESSION (derived from a specific video and re-expressed), LICENSED (reserved, none in R1) or PENDING_REVIEW (never ships)
Measured / EstimatedThe fixed label on every metric (SRS-BR-003): Measured for duration, speaking time, speech ratio, pace, long pauses, word count, target word used and recognised-as-intended %; Estimated for filler rate, lexical variety, grammar observations, structure elements and hedging. Always shown as text, never by colour alone
Milestone (M0–M9)A roadmap point with named evidence, from M0 "Stage A approved" to M9 "R2 release"
Mistakes foundThe list of detected issues shown after each valid attempt, each with its evidence and label; the first three shown, the rest behind "Show all"
Noise bandThe variation in a metric that is not meaningful; an issue is raised only when a value is outside its target band by more than the noise band, and trends report small changes as "no clear change"
Not runDefault status of every test case; the status of all 510 planned test cases in Stage A
OutboxThe local table of changes waiting to be uploaded; written in the same transaction as the change and drained by the sync worker
Preliminary (†)Marks an estimate or target that is Proposed and not yet approved or measured
ProvenanceThe record linking a creator-derived item to its specific source video and timestamp, the technique name as taught (never displayed), and who verified it and when
QuarantineA flaky test kept running and reported but excluded from the required check, with a linked issue; at most 5 % of a suite and never a critical-feature test at release
"Recognised as intended"The label for comparing what the recogniser heard with the expected words; never called pronunciation
Reference device (RD-LOW / RD-MID / RD-HIGH)The device classes against which performance and tier targets are defined and measured
Release candidate (RC)A tagged, release-signed build that goes through device regression, checklists, beta and UAT before G6 and G7
Required checkOne of the four CI jobs that must be green before a PR can merge: wrapper-validation, build-test, security-static, rules-test
RTMRequirement Traceability Matrix (GOV-01), linking requirements to stories, acceptance criteria, design, tasks, tests and evidence
Rule precision gateThe CI test that only lets a grammar rule ship with precision ≥ 0.90 † on at least 30 positive and 30 negative examples
Self-checkSelf-reported checklist items for aspects audio cannot show; labelled self-reported and never affecting metrics, trends or streak
Severity (S1–S4) and priority (DP1–DP4)Defect impact from Critical to Low, and the order of fixing from Immediate to Backlog; DP is used so it is never confused with requirement priority P0–P3
Spike (SPK-01, SPK-02, SPK-03)Time-boxed investigations: on-device recognition across devices, the grammar-rule corpus and precision harness, and (R3) Whisper-class recognition research
SprintA two-week planning unit with a planned load of at most 6.5 engineering days
Story (US-nnn)A user story with Given/When/Then acceptance criteria, tracing to product and software requirements
Task (T-nn.n.n)The smallest scheduled unit of work, with a type (ENG, TST, DOC, CNT, DES, OPS) and a size (S, M, L)
Test-run record (TRR)The evidence record for a manual or device run: build, device, tester role, cases, results, evidence links, defects
Tier (T1, T2, T3a, T3b)Speech capability of a device: on-device with app-owned microphone; on-device with recogniser-owned microphone; consented online; audio-only
Traceability scriptA proposed CI script that matches defined test IDs with implemented tests and CI results, and reports gaps, orphans and duplicates
UATUser acceptance testing: the beta programme and the ten UAT scripts, ending in the UAT report signed at G6
WaivedTest status set only by the Product Owner, with reason, risk and expiry

PLN-02 §1; PLN-03 §1–2; PLN-05 §3, §6; TST-01 §1.3, §5, §7, §9, §11; ARC-02 §9; ARC-03 §6, §11.20; ARC-05 §8.4, §10; ENG-01 §5.
